مشكاة المصابيح (١)

Mishkat 1 — Study Notes (402–1051)
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤٠٢
«لَا وُضُوءَ لِمَنْ لَمْ يَذْكُرِ اسْمَ اللهِ عَلَيْهِ»
Narrated by Saʿīd b. Zayd (سعيد بن زيد)  ·  Riwayah: al-Tirmidhī, Ibn Mājah
Class Notes
  • The negation (لا) — the whole dispute turns on what «لا» is negating. Two readings:
  • نَفْي الْجِنْس (nafy al-jins) — negation of the kind/genus, i.e. it negates the thing's very existence. On this reading the wuḍūʾ is invalid without the tasmiya.
  • نَفْي الْكَمَال (nafy al-kamāl) — negation of completeness/perfection. On this reading the wuḍūʾ is valid, but not the complete one. (Framing from the teacher's notes; Mirqāt adjudicates between the two below.)
  • The «لا» does not negate the existence of the wuḍūʾ — it negates its completeness/perfection (nafy al-kamāl). You still have a valid wuḍūʾ without the tasmiya; what's negated is the perfect one. (Against the Ẓāhirīs.)
  • Mirqāt (quoting al-Qāḍī ʿIyāḍ): «هَذِهِ الصِّيغَةُ حَقِيقَةٌ فِي نَفْيِ الشَّيْءِ، وَيُطْلَقُ مَجَازًا عَلَى نَفْيِ الِاعْتِدَادِ بِهِ لِعَدَمِ صِحَّتِهِ، كَقَوْلِهِ ﷺ: "وَلَا صَلَاةَ إِلَّا بِطَهُورٍ"، وَعَلَى نَفْيِ كَمَالِهِ كَقَوْلِهِ ﷺ: "لَا صَلَاةَ لِجَارِ الْمَسْجِدِ إِلَّا فِي الْمَسْجِدِ". وَهَاهُنَا مَحْمُولَةٌ عَلَى نَفْيِ الْكَمَالِ خِلَافًا لِأَهْلِ الظَّاهِرِ» — the form is literal (ḥaqīqa) in negating a thing's existence, used metaphorically (majāz) for negating validity or for negating perfection; here it is perfection.
  • Madhhab positions on the tasmiya:
  • Ahl al-Ẓawāhir, a riwāya of Aḥmad, a riwāya of Isḥāq (b. Rāhwayh) take «لا» as nafy al-jins: no tasmiya = no valid wuḍūʾ. The two are not identical on the forgetful person, though — Aḥmad and Isḥāq say that if one leaves it forgetfully the wuḍūʾ is still valid, whereas Ahl al-Ẓawāhir hold it invalid even if he forgot. (This distinction is from the teacher's notes.)
  • Mālik, Abū Ḥanīfa, al-Shāfiʿī + a riwāya of Aḥmad hold that tasmiya is a sunnah, not required.
  • Supporting proof (Ibn ʿUmar & Ibn Masʿūd) — exact wording from Mirqāt:
  • «مَنْ تَوَضَّأَ وَذَكَرَ اسْمَ اللهِ كَانَ طَهُورًا لِجَمِيعِ بَدَنِهِ، وَمَنْ تَوَضَّأَ وَلَمْ يَذْكُرِ اسْمَ اللهِ كَانَ طَهُورًا لِأَعْضَاءِ وُضُوئِهِ»
  • With the tasmiya, purification covers the whole body; without it, only the washed limbs.
  • What "purification" means (Mirqāt): «وَالْمُرَادُ بِالطَّهَارَةِ الطَّهَارَةُ عَنِ الذُّنُوبِ؛ لِأَنَّ الْحَدَثَ لَا يَتَجَزَّأُ» — purification from sins, not physical — because ḥadath does not divide into parts (you aren't "partly" physically pure).
  • Qurʾān argument: the wuḍūʾ āya names only four limbs; one cannot add to the Qurʾān by a single (āḥād) report, so making tasmiya a requirement would add to the Qurʾān via khabar al-wāḥid — therefore sunnah, not farḍ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 402); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤٠٤
«لَا وُضُوءَ لِمَنْ لَمْ يَذْكُرِ اسْمَ اللهِ عَلَيْهِ» — with the added opening «لَا صَلَاةَ لِمَنْ لَا وُضُوءَ لَهُ»
Narrated by Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī
Class Notes
  • Two mistakes (corrected from Mirqāt):
  • 1. Isnād slip: Mirqāt (al-Ṭībī & al-Sayyid Jamāl al-Dīn): «قَوْلُهُ: عَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ عَنْ أَبِيهِ سَهْوٌ بِلَا شَكٍّ» — "from his father" is a certain error (sahw). The correct Dārimī isnād: «رُبَيْحُ بْنُ عَبْدِ الرَّحْمَنِ بْنِ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ الْخُدْرِيِّ، عَنْ أَبِيهِ، عَنْ جَدِّهِ، عَنِ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ» — Rubayḥ b. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. Abī Saʿīd, from his father, from his grandfather (Abū Saʿīd).
  • 2. Misattributed ziyāda: the addition «لا صلاة لمن لا وضوء له» is not al-Dārimī's alone; «وزادوا» = Aḥmad, Abū Dāwūd, and al-Dārimī together (the author's wording wrongly implies it's al-Dārimī's).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 404); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤٠٧
«وَعَنِ الْمُسْتَوْرِدِ بْنِ شَدَّادٍ قَالَ: رَأَيْتُ رَسُولَ اللهِ ﷺ إِذَا تَوَضَّأَ يُدَلِّكُ أَصَابِعَ رِجْلَيْهِ بِخِنْصَرِهِ»
Narrated by al-Mustawrid b. Shaddād  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Interlace the toes using the (left) pinky.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 407); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤٠٨
«وَعَنْ أَنَسٍ قَالَ: كَانَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ إِذَا تَوَضَّأَ أَخَذَ كَفًّا مِنْ مَاءٍ فَأَدْخَلَهُ تَحْتَ حَنَكِهِ فَخَلَّلَ بِهِ لِحْيَتَهُ وَقَالَ: «هَكَذَا أَمَرَنِي رَبِّي»»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Mirqāt on the matn: takhlīl is done during washing the face (part of it, not after it): «وَكَانَ عِنْدَ غَسْلِ الْوَجْهِ لِأَنَّهُ مِنْ تَمَامِهِ لَا بَعْدَ فَرَاغِهِ»; and «هكذا أمرني ربي» = «بِالْوَحْيِ الْخَفِيِّ أَوْ بِوَاسِطَةِ جِبْرِيلَ» (by hidden waḥy, or via Jibrīl).
  • الْحَنَك (al-ḥanak) — al-Abharī (in Mirqāt): the inside of the mouth, and «تَحْتَ الْحَنَكِ» = beneath the chin. Lamaʿāt adds: the lower part from the front edge of the two jawbones. So he put a handful of water under the beard from the throat side, so water reached it from every side.
  • Mirqāt — when exactly? The ẓāhir of the wording suggests after finishing the wuḍūʾ, but it more likely falls within it, after washing the face, and that is al-awjah (the sounder view) «لِأَنَّهُ مِنْ مُكَمِّلَاتِهِ» — because it is among the things that complete it.
  • Takhrīj (Mirqāt, from Mīrak): Abū Dāwūd narrated it «وَسَكَتَ عَلَيْهِ» — and stayed silent on it, i.e. gave no grading.
  • Is takhlīl of the beard obligatory?
  • Lamaʿāt answers this directly on «هَكَذَا أَمَرَنِي رَبِّي»: «وَلِهَذَا ذَهَبَ الْمُزَنِيُّ وَأَحْمَدُ — فِي مَا اخْتَارَهُ بَعْضُ الْأَئِمَّةِ مِنْ مَذْهَبِهِ — إِلَى أَنَّ تَخْلِيلَ اللِّحْيَةِ وَاجِبٌ، كَذَا فِي الْحَوَاشِي» — al-Muzanī and Aḥmad (on the view some imams of his madhhab preferred) held takhlīl obligatory, precisely because of this "thus my Lord commanded me".
  • Divergence noted: the teacher's notes attribute the obligation view to Ahl al-Ẓawāhir + Abū Thawr. Neither Mirqāt nor Lamaʿāt names either of them on this point (checked both); Lamaʿāt names al-Muzanī and Aḥmad instead. Both rest on the same proof, «هَكَذَا أَمَرَنِي رَبِّي» — so the argument is identical, only the attribution differs. Teacher's version kept as given:
  • Ahl al-Ẓawāhir + Abū Thawr hold it obligatory. Proofs: (1) «هَكَذَا أَمَرَنِي رَبِّي»; (2) the word «كَانَ» in 409 = istimrār (continuous practice).
  • Jamhūr (not obligatory): the wuḍūʾ āya names only the four limbs; most narrations don't mention beard-takhlīl, meaning it wasn't continuous, so «كان» = a single occurrence (wuqūʿ), not istimrār. al-Nawawī (Sharḥ Muslim): «كان» does not necessarily denote continuity, so it's not wujūb.
  • Ruling (label): Abū Yūsuf & al-Shāfiʿī: sunnah; jamhūr of the Ḥanafīs: mustaḥabb; fatwā follows Abū Yūsuf (sunnah).
  • Washing the beard with the face (ghasl al-liḥya) (from teacher's commentary):
  • Thin (خفيف khafīf) + not hanging: by agreement, wash it.
  • Hanging (مسترسلة mustarsila), thick or thin: wash only what meets the skin (the face part).
  • Thick (كثيف kathīf) + not hanging: six opinions; two: Kanz says wipe (masaḥ) one-fourth; the mukhtār Ḥanafī view (Ibn ʿĀbidīn, Radd al-Muḥtār) says wash all of it.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 408); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤٠٩
«وَعَنْ عُثْمَانَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُ أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ كَانَ يُخَلِّلُ لِحْيَتَهُ»
Narrated by ʿUthmān (R.A.)  ·  Riwayah: al-Tirmidhī, al-Dārimī
Daleel For
  • Daleel for: those who hold beard-takhlīl obligatory — per the teacher's notes, Ahl al-Ẓawāhir & Abū Thawr; per Lamaʿāt, al-Muzanī and Aḥmad (on the view some imams of his madhhab preferred). The proof drawn from this hadith is the word «كَانَ» — read as istimrār (continuous practice). Cited in hadith 408.
  • Our jawāb (Ḥanafī): «كَانَ» here does not establish continuity, so it does not establish wujūb. It denotes a single occurrence (wuqūʿ), not istimrār — supported by the fact that most narrations of the Prophet's ﷺ wuḍūʾ do not mention beard-takhlīl at all, which is itself evidence it was not his continuous practice. al-Nawawī (Sharḥ Muslim) states the principle: «كان» does not necessarily denote continuity. Add to this that the wuḍūʾ āya names only the four limbs, and a requirement cannot be added to the Qurʾān by a single (āḥād) report. Ruling: Abū Yūsuf & al-Shāfiʿī — sunnah; jamhūr of the Ḥanafīs — mustaḥabb; fatwā follows Abū Yūsuf (sunnah). (Full argument at 408.)
  • On the strength of the takhlīl reports (Lamaʿāt, quoting Safar al-Saʿādah): a ḥadīth has indeed come regarding takhlīl of the beard — «فَقَبِلَهُ بَعْضُ أَهْلِ الْحَدِيثِ، وَرَدَّهُ بَعْضٌ» — some of the hadith scholars accepted it and some rejected it. Lamaʿāt also cites al-Tirmidhī via Ḥassān b. Bilāl: «رَأَيْتُ عَمَّارَ بْنَ يَاسِرٍ تَوَضَّأَ وَخَلَّلَ لِحْيَتَهُ» — he saw ʿAmmār b. Yāsir (R.A.) perform wuḍūʾ and do takhlīl of his beard.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 409). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 408's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤١٠
«وَعَنْ أَبِي حَيَّةَ قَالَ: رَأَيْتُ عَلِيًّا تَوَضَّأَ فَغَسَلَ كَفَّيْهِ حَتَّى أَنْقَاهُمَا ثُمَّ مَضْمَضَ ثَلَاثًا وَاسْتَنْشَقَ ثَلَاثًا وَغَسَلَ وَجْهَهُ ثَلَاثًا وَذِرَاعَيْهِ ثَلَاثًا وَمَسَحَ بِرَأْسِهِ مَرَّةً ثُمَّ غَسَلَ قَدَمَيْهِ إِلَى الْكَعْبَيْنِ ثُمَّ قَامَ فَأَخَذَ فَضْلَ طَهُورِهِ فَشَرِبَهُ وَهُوَ قَائِمٌ ثُمَّ قَالَ: أَحْبَبْتُ أَنْ أُرِيَكُمْ كَيْفَ كَانَ طَهُورُ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ»
Narrated by Abū Ḥayya  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Abu Hayya told that he saw 'Ali perform ablution. He washed the palms of his hands till he cleaned them, then rinsed his mouth three times, snuffed up water three times, washed his face three times and his forearms three times, wiped his head once, then washed his feet up to the ankles, after which he stood up and, taking the remainder of his ablution water, he drank it standing. Then he said, "I wanted to show you how God's messenger performed ablution."
Class Notes
  • Do maḍmaḍa and istinshāq together (waṣl) or one after the other (faṣl)? This matn has «ثُمَّ مَضْمَضَ ثَلَاثًا وَاسْتَنْشَقَ ثَلَاثًا» — three each, described separately. Mirqāt: «ظَاهِرُهُ الْفَصْلُ الْمُطَابِقُ لِمَذْهَبِنَا» — its apparent sense is separating (al-faṣl), which agrees with our (Ḥanafī) madhhab. On the next hadith (411, ʿAbd Khayr) al-Qārī adds that its wording is summary and «لَيْسَ فِيهِ … دَلَالَةٌ عَلَى الْفَصْلِ أَوِ الْوَصْلِ» (indicates neither), and that «وَهِمَ ابْنُ حَجَرٍ» — Ibn Ḥajar erred in claiming joining (al-waṣl) is sunnah in them.
  • Lamaʿāt (بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ، الفصل الأول) frames it as five ways (الْوُجُوهُ الْخَمْسَةُ) of combining/separating the two: al-Shāfiʿī's well-known position is the first way; Abū Ḥanīfa's well-known position is al-faṣl — the fifth way.
  • The Ḥanafī proof (Lamaʿāt): the ḥadīth of Ṭalḥa b. Muṣarrif, from his father, from his grandfather (Abū Dāwūd) — he saw the Prophet ﷺ performing wuḍūʾ, water running from his face and beard onto his chest, «فَرَأَيْتُهُ يَفْصِلُ بَيْنَ الْمَضْمَضَةِ وَالِاسْتِنْشَاقِ» (separating between them). A second report from the same chain (Ibn Mājah) has three rinses and three sniffs from a single handful — but the first is preferred, because the mouth and the nose are two distinct limbs, so water is not combined between them any more than between the other limbs; and it is settled in uṣūl al-fiqh that a ḥadīth agreeing with qiyās takes precedence over one contradicting it.
  • Objection & answer (Lamaʿāt): the Ṭalḥa b. Muṣarrif ḥadīth was criticised on the grounds that Ṭalḥa's grandfather is majhūl and his companionship not established. Answer (from Jāmiʿ al-Uṣūl): Ṭalḥa b. Muṣarrif is among the eminent, reliable Tābiʿīn, and his grandfather is Kaʿb b. ʿAmr — or, it is said, ʿAmr b. Kaʿb.
  • Wiping the head once: Mirqāt notes this matn («وَمَسَحَ بِرَأْسِهِ مَرَّةً») is a proof that the head is not wiped three times — the jamhūr's position, against al-Shāfiʿī. Al-Qārī rejects Ibn Ḥajar's reading that ʿAlī (R.A.) was merely showing permissibility or being light in his own purification: ʿAlī is not a legislator, and on that logic he would have had to drop the other sunnahs too — besides which he washed the feet once as well.
  • Drinking the leftover water: not an emphasized sunnah (not muʾakkada), but try it at least once in a lifetime.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 410); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤١٦
«وَعَنْ أَبِي أُمَامَةَ ذَكَرَ وُضُوءَ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ قَالَ: وَكَانَ يَمْسَحُ الْمَاقَيْنِ وَقَالَ: الْأُذُنَانِ مِنَ الرَّأْسِ … قَالَ حَمَّادٌ: لَا أَدْرِي: الْأُذُنَانِ مِنَ الرَّأْسِ مِنْ قَوْلِ أَبِي أُمَامَةَ أَمْ مِنْ قَوْلِ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ»
Narrated by Abū Umāma  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Hard word — الْمَاقَيْنِ: dual of مَأْق (also مُوق; with or without hamza — Lamaʿāt lists the variants from al-Qāmūs). It is the corner of the eye nearest the nose, the tear-channel (مَجْرَى الدَّمْعِ) — so al-Tūrbishtī, citing Abū ʿUbayd al-Harawī; al-Jawharī (al-Ṣiḥāḥ) makes it the corner adjoining the nose and the ear. Lamaʿāt notes the wording carries both senses: on the first, the dual counts the two eyes; on the second, the two corners of each eye. And يَمْسَحُ here means يُدَلِّكُهُمَا — he rubs them.
  • Why the eye-corners at all: al-Ṭībī — wiping them is mustaḥabb, a further thoroughness in isbāgh, because the eye is seldom free of matter (قَذًى from kuhl, or رَمَص) that runs down and cakes at the corner. Wiping both corners is the more cautious course, since the reason for it applies equally to each.
  • Washing vs. wiping; are the ears a "follower" (تابع) to the head? Lamaʿāt draws two separate rulings out of «الْأُذُنَانِ مِنَ الرَّأْسِ»: (1) the ears are wiped along with the head, and (2) with the head's own water, not fresh water. It states that the four imams agree on the first; the dispute is over the second.
  • Mirqāt (from Sharḥ al-Sunna): «اخْتُلِفَ فِي أَنَّهُ هَلْ يُؤْخَذُ لِلْأُذُنَيْنِ مَاءٌ جَدِيدٌ، قَالَ الشَّافِعِيُّ: هُمَا عُضْوَانِ عَلَى حِيَالِهِمَا يُمْسَحَانِ ثَلَاثًا بِثَلَاثَةِ مِيَاهٍ جُدُدٍ، وَذَهَبَ أَكْثَرُهُمْ إِلَى أَنَّهُمَا مِنَ الرَّأْسِ يُمْسَحَانِ مَعَهُ … وَبِهِ أَخَذَ أَبُو حَنِيفَةَ وَمَالِكٌ وَأَحْمَدُ» — al-Shāfiʿī: the ears are two independent limbs, wiped three times with fresh water; jamhūr (Abū Ḥanīfa, Mālik, Aḥmad): they are of the head, wiped with it, i.e. with one water.
  • If they are tābiʿ to the head, then no fresh water; if not tābiʿ, fresh water is needed.
  • The remaining reported positions (Mirqāt, same passage): al-Zuhrī — the ears are of the face, wiped with it (Lamaʿāt reports this from him as well); al-Shaʿbī — their outer side belongs to the head, their inner side to the face; Ḥammād — he washes both outer and inner; Isḥāq — the preferred course is to wipe their front with the face and their back with the head.
  • The Ḥanafī case, now located — Ibn al-Humām, Fatḥ al-Qadīr (quoted in Lamaʿāt): he gathers the routes showing the sentence is the Prophet's ﷺ own words rather than Abū Umāma's, reports the criticism levelled at them, and then establishes their strength; he also cites a report of the Prophet ﷺ wiping the ears with the head's water, and notes that al-Nasāʾī headed a chapter «بَابُ مَسْحِ الْأُذُنَيْنِ مَعَ الرَّأْسِ». On the counter-proof that he ﷺ took fresh water for the ears: «يَجِبُ حَمْلُهُ عَلَى أَنَّهُ لِفَنَاءِ الْبِلَّةِ قَبْلَ الِاسْتِيعَابِ» — it must be read as the wetness having run out before the wiping was complete, exactly as one would take more water if it ran out partway over a single limb. And on weight of evidence, «كَانَ مَا رَوَيْنَاهُ أَكْثَرَ وَأَشْهَرَ» — reported from eight Companions: Abū Umāma, Ibn ʿAbbās, ʿAbdullāh b. Zayd, Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī, Abū Hurayra, Anas, Ibn ʿUmar and ʿĀʾisha, بِطُرُقٍ كَثِيرَةٍ.
  • Is the sentence marfūʿ or mawqūf? This is what Ḥammād was unsure of in the matn itself. al-Tirmidhī: «إِسْنَادُهُ لَيْسَ بِذَلِكَ الْقَائِمِ» (the chain is not that firm); al-Dāraquṭnī: «رَفْعُهُ وَهْمٌ، وَالصَّوَابُ أَنَّهُ مَوْقُوفٌ». al-Qārī's answer: «مِثْلَ هَذَا لَا يُقَالُ مِنْ قِبَلِ الرَّأْيِ، فَمَوْقُوفُهُ فِي حُكْمِ الْمَرْفُوعِ» — a statement of this kind is not arrived at by personal reasoning, so even in its mawqūf form it carries the force of a marfūʿ report.
  • Teacher's framing — now corroborated, with one difference of attribution: the teacher gave this Ḥanafī case as al-Zaylaʿī's in Naṣb al-Rāya (eight Companions and four more, the report rising to ḥasan li-ghayrihi), and answered the fresh-water proof with «محمول على جفاف اليد» — his hand had dried, so he re-wet it. Our two commentaries carry the same argument, the same eight Companions and the same "the wetness had run out" answer, but under Ibn al-Humām's name in Fatḥ al-Qadīr. Both are Ḥanafī authorities working from the same takhrīj material, so this is a difference of attribution rather than of substance; the teacher's version is kept as given. Still teacher-only, not found in either commentary here: reading «الأذنان من الرأس» as an anatomical claim (rebutted — the Prophet ﷺ is stating a ruling, not biology); al-Nasāʾī's «حتى تخرج من أذنيه»; and the weak narrator ʿUmar b. Abān.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 416); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤١٧
«وَعَنْ عَمْرِو بْنِ شُعَيْبٍ عَنْ أَبِيهِ عَنْ جَدِّهِ … جَاءَ أَعْرَابِيٌّ … يَسْأَلُهُ عَنِ الْوُضُوءِ فَأَرَاهُ ثَلَاثًا ثَلَاثًا ثُمَّ قَالَ: «هَكَذَا الْوُضُوءُ فَمَنْ زَادَ عَلَى هَذَا فَقَدْ أَسَاءَ وَتَعَدَّى وَظَلَمَ»»
Narrated by ʿAmr b. Shuʿayb (from his father, from his grandfather)  ·  Grade: ḥasan
Translation
A nomadic Arab came to the Prophet and asked him about ablution. He demonstrated it, performing each detail three times and then said, "This is how ablution should be performed. If anyone does more than this, he has done wrong, transgressed, and done wickedly."
Class Notes
  • «هَكَذَا الْوُضُوءُ» means the complete wuḍūʾ — Mirqāt glosses it «أَيِ الْكَامِلُ», and Lamaʿāt reads the bedouin's question «عَنِ الْوُضُوءِ» as being «عَنْ كَمَالِهِ», about its perfect form. This is what makes the rest of the hadith coherent: three times is the perfected form, and the censure attaches to going beyond it, not to the basic validity of the wuḍūʾ.
  • Why he was shown rather than told: فَأَرَاهُ — he showed him by action, which carries further than speech, being closer to exactness and leaving a deeper mark on the heart; Mirqāt cites «لَيْسَ الْخَبَرُ كَالْمُعَايَنَةِ» — a report is not like seeing for oneself. There is an ellipsis in the wording: he wished to show him what he had asked about, so he performed wuḍūʾ washing each limb three times.
  • The three words of censure (Mirqāt): أَسَاءَ — he did badly, by abandoning the sunnah; تَعَدَّى — he overstepped its limit by the addition; ظَلَمَ — he wronged himself: by going against the Prophet ﷺ, or because he wearied himself past three with no reward accruing for it, or because he destroyed water to no purpose. Ibn al-Malak: he was censured with these three words together to show how severe the rebuke was and to deter him from it.
  • al-Nasafī's qualification — found in Mirqāt: Imām Ḥāfiẓ al-Dīn al-Nasafī holds the censure applies where a man adds believing that this is the sunnah. But if he adds لِطُمَأْنِينَةِ الْقَلْبِ عِنْدَ الشَّكِّ — to settle his heart when in doubt — or intending a separate, fresh wuḍūʾ, then there is no harm in it, since the Prophet ﷺ commanded leaving what makes you doubt for what does not.
  • al-Qārī's rebuttal in his own «قُلْتُ» — found in Mirqāt. (a) On "to settle the heart when in doubt": doubt after washing three times has no ground to stand on, and if it does arise there is no end to it — that is waswasa, «وَهُوَ الْوَسْوَسَةُ». Taking the plain sense, Ibn al-Mubārak said «لَا آمَنُ إِذَا زَادَ عَلَى الثَّلَاثِ أَنْ يَأْثَمَ» — I do not consider him safe from sin if he exceeds three; and Aḥmad and Isḥāq: none exceeds three but a man afflicted (مُبْتَلًى) — with waswasa or with madness. Ibn Ḥajar adds that he has seen sufferers of waswasa wash one hand more than a hundred times and still hold their state of ḥadath to be certain. (b) On "intending another wuḍūʾ": renewal is not mustaḥabb before one has actually performed an act of worship with the first wuḍūʾ — and in any case renewal cannot be conceived mid-wuḍūʾ, only once it is complete. (c) On the "leave what makes you doubt" principle: it in fact cuts the other way — the extra washing is itself the doubtful thing, so it is what should be dropped in favour of what raises no doubt, namely the amount the Lawgiver fixed, precisely so as to be rid of doubt and waswasa.
  • An alternative reading of the three words (Mirqāt, «وَقِيلَ»): he was ill-mannered through laxity in overdoing it — since going beyond is a belittling of what the Sharīʿa had already perfected, a transgression past the limit set for him and past what was made the end-point of completion, and a wrong done by destroying water and putting it where it does not belong.
  • Wording variant (Lamaʿāt): one narration reads «فَمَنْ نَقَصَ أَوْ زَادَ» — whoever falls short or adds — but the sound wording omits the "falls short": «وَالصَّحِيحُ عَدَمُ ذِكْرِ النُّقْصَانِ».
  • Takhrīj: al-Nasāʾī and Ibn Mājah with this wording; Abū Dāwūd narrated its sense. Mīrak, from al-Takhrīj: Abū Dāwūd's version is longer than this, and he was silent about it (سَكَتَ عَلَيْهِ) — i.e. gave it no grading.
  • Teacher's note kept: renewing out of waswasa is the devil's whispering, and washing three times closes that door — which is al-Qārī's own conclusion above, now confirmed word for word. (The teacher's "two rewards" phrasing was not found in either commentary on this hadith, so it is not reproduced as theirs.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 417); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤١٨
«وَعَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ الْمُغَفَّلِ أَنَّهُ سَمِعَ ابْنَهُ يَقُولُ: اللَّهُمَّ إِنِّي أَسْأَلُكَ الْقَصْرَ الْأَبْيَضَ عَنْ يَمِينِ الْجَنَّةِ، قَالَ: أَيْ بُنَيَّ سَلِ اللهَ الْجَنَّةَ وَتَعَوَّذْ بِهِ مِنَ النَّارِ، فَإِنِّي سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللهِ ﷺ يَقُولُ: «إِنَّهُ سَيَكُونُ فِي هَذِهِ الْأُمَّةِ قَوْمٌ يَعْتَدُونَ فِي الطَّهُورِ وَالدُّعَاءِ»»
Narrated by ʿAbdullāh b. al-Mughaffal  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
'Abdallah b. al-Mughaffal on hearing his son say, "O God, I ask Thee for the white palace on the right of paradise," said, "Sonny, ask God for paradise and seek refuge in Him from hell, for I heard God's Messenger say, 'There will be some among this people who will go over the score in ablution and supplication.'"
Class Notes
  • The rebuke is for how one asks: either asking for things one isn't worthy of, or asking passively rather than imploring. Neither ifrāṭ (إفراط) nor tafrīṭ (تفريط) in duʿāʾ — supplicate with humility.
  • al-Tūribishtī (from Mirqāt): «أَنْكَرَ الصَّحَابِيُّ عَلَى ابْنِهِ … حَيْثُ طَمَحَ إِلَى مَا لَمْ يَبْلُغْهُ عَمَلًا وَسَأَلَ مَنَازِلَ الْأَنْبِيَاءِ وَالْأَوْلِيَاءِ، وَجَعَلَهَا مِنَ الِاعْتِدَاءِ فِي الدُّعَاءِ لِمَا فِيهَا مِنَ التَّجَاوُزِ عَنْ حَدِّ الْأَدَبِ، وَنَظَرُ الدَّاعِي إِلَى نَفْسِهِ بِعَيْنِ الْكَمَالِ … وَالِاعْتِدَاءُ فِي الطَّهُورِ اسْتِعْمَالُهُ فَوْقَ الْحَاجَةِ، وَالْمُبَالَغَةُ فِي تَحَرِّي طَهُورِيَّتِهِ» — he aspired to ranks (of prophets/saints) he had not earned, viewing himself with "the eye of perfection"; iʿtidāʾ in purification = using water beyond need and overdoing the pursuit of purity.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 418); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ
حديث ٤٢١
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ … كَانَتْ لِرَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ خِرْقَةٌ يُنَشِّفُ بِهَا أَعْضَاءَهُ بَعْدَ الْوُضُوءِ. رَوَاهُ التِّرْمِذِيُّ وَقَالَ: … أَبُو مُعَاذٍ الرَّاوِي ضَعِيفٌ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
'A'isha said that God's messenger had a cloth with which he dried his limbs after ablution.
Class Notes
  • One group: permissible to dry off. Another: not. The preferable view is to leave the wuḍūʾ water on. So the hadith is read either as (1) showing permissibility of the towel, or (2) that drying off is like deception (point to confirm from teacher's source).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 421); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ سُنَنِ الْوُضُوءِ — الفصل الثالث
حديث ٤٢٦
«وَعَنْ … عُبَيْدِ اللهِ بْنِ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ عُمَرَ … أَنَّ رَسُولَ اللهِ ﷺ كَانَ أُمِرَ بِالْوُضُوءِ لِكُلِّ صَلَاةٍ طَاهِرًا كَانَ أَوْ غَيْرَ طَاهِرٍ، فَلَمَّا شَقَّ ذَلِكَ … أُمِرَ بِالسِّوَاكِ عِنْدَ كُلِّ صَلَاةٍ وَوُضِعَ عَنْهُ الْوُضُوءُ إِلَّا مِنْ حَدَثٍ …»
Narrated by ʿAbdullāh b. Ḥanẓala (Ibn al-Ghasīl)  ·  Grade: ḥasan
Translation
Muhammad b. Yahya b. Hibban said that he asked 'Ubaidallah b. 'Abdallah b. 'Umar from whom 'Abdallah b. 'Umar got the practice of performing ablution for every prayer whether he was in a state of purity or not, and he replied that Asma' daughter of Zaid b. al-Khattab told him that 'Abdallah b. Hanzala b. Abu 'Amir al-Ghasil told her that God's messenger was commanded to perform ablution for every prayer whether in a state of purity or not; but when that became a burden to God's messenger he was commanded to use the tooth-stick before every time of prayer, and the performance of ablution was remitted to him unless his state of purity had been broken. He said that 'Abdallah considered he had the strength for it, so he kept on doing it till he died.
Class Notes
  • Ahmad transmitted it.
  • Two opinions on what became of the obligation of wuḍūʾ for every prayer: (1) it was fully obligatory then abrogated, so afterward done because preferable (mustaḥabb); (2) done merely to show permissibility / recommendation.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 426); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣٠
«عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ … «إِذَا جَلَسَ بَيْنَ شُعَبِهَا الْأَرْبَعِ ثُمَّ جَهَدَهَا فَقَدْ وَجَبَ الْغُسْلُ وَإِنْ لَمْ يُنْزِلْ»»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Class Notes
  • شُعْبة (shuʿba) = a piece/branch; the four shuʿab = a manner of sitting between her four limbs/branches — a kināya (allusion) to intercourse.
  • «جَهَدَهَا» (jahadahā) = exerted himself / had intercourse — complete entry (penetration); "the entrance" = maghīb al-ḥashafa. Ruling: ghusl obligatory on penetration, even without emission («وإن لم ينزل»).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 430); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣١
«وَعَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ … «إِنَّمَا الْمَاءُ مِنَ الْمَاءِ». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Meaning: ghusl-water obligated from sperm-water. Literal reading: ghusl only on emission — but intercourse without emission still requires ghusl.
  • Early ruling, abrogated by 430 (penetration leads to ghusl) and explicitly by 448.
  • Companion dispute: (1) iksāl obligates ghusl; (2) only emission does. In ʿUmar's caliphate he asked the people of Badr (البدريون) whom to consult, then ʿAlī, then the Prophet's wives, then ʿĀʾisha, who said «إِذَا جَاوَزَ الْخِتَانُ الْخِتَانَ فَقَدْ وَجَبَ الْغُسْلُ» (and that's how she and the Prophet ﷺ did it), reaching consensus.
  • Ibn ʿAbbās restricted «الماء من الماء» to the wet dream (احتلام) — see 432. Against him: the report's context was a man who interrupted intercourse without emission — so it's about (interrupted) intercourse, not a dream. Reconciling: either the full context didn't reach him, or it has two applications — awake (abrogated) vs asleep/wet dream (still holds).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 431); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣٢
«وَقَالَ ابْنُ عَبَّاسٍ: إِنَّمَا الْمَاءُ مِنَ الْمَاءِ فِي الِاحْتِلَامِ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās (R.A.)  ·  Riwayah: al-Tirmidhī (not found in the Ṣaḥīḥayn)
Daleel For
  • Not madhhab-specific — this is Ibn ʿAbbās's own qualification, restricting hadith 431's «الماء من الماء» to the wet-dream (iḥtilām) scenario only. Contested in 431's notes (that report's actual context is interrupted intercourse, not a dream).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 432). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 431's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣٣
«وَعَنْ أُمِّ سَلَمَةَ … قَالَتْ أُمُّ سُلَيْمٍ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ إِنَّ اللهَ لَا يَسْتَحْيِي مِنَ الْحَقِّ، فَهَلْ عَلَى الْمَرْأَةِ مِنْ غُسْلٍ إِذَا احْتَلَمَتْ؟ قَالَ ﷺ: «إِذَا رَأَتِ الْمَاءَ» … قَالَ: «نَعَمْ، تَرِبَتْ يَمِينُكِ، فَبِمَ يُشْبِهُهَا وَلَدُهَا؟»»
Narrated by Umm Salama (reporting Umm Sulaym)  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Umm Salama told how Umm Sulaim said, "Messenger of God, God is not ashamed of the truth. Is any washing necessary for a woman when she has a sexual dream?" He replied, "Yes, when she sees signs of liquid." Umm Salama then covered her face and said, "Messenger of God, does a woman have sexual dreams?" He replied, "Of course she does. In what way does her child resemble her?"
Class Notes
  • "Allah does not feel shy": literal ḥayāʾ is impossible for Allah — it is (1) an inner emotional change and (2) a change from fear of a critic. So what's meant is the concomitant (اللازم): the truth (الحق) is not abandoned/withheld.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 433); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣٤
«وَزَادَ مُسْلِمٌ بِرِوَايَةِ أُمِّ سُلَيْمٍ: «أَنَّ مَاءَ الرَّجُلِ غَلِيظٌ أَبْيَضُ، وَمَاءَ الْمَرْأَةِ رَقِيقٌ أَصْفَرُ، فَمِنْ أَيِّهِمَا عَلَا أَوْ سَبَقَ يَكُونُ مِنْهُ الشَّبَهُ»»
Narrated by Umm Salama / Umm Sulaym  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ. (Grouped with 433 on sunnah.com.)
Translation
Umm Salama told how Umm Sulaim said, "Messenger of God, God is not ashamed of the truth. Is any washing necessary for a woman when she has a sexual dream?" He replied, "Yes, when she sees signs of liquid." Umm Salama then covered her face and said, "Messenger of God, does a woman have sexual dreams?" He replied, "Of course she does. In what way does her child resemble her?" Muslim added in the version of Umm Sulaim, "The man's liquid is coarse and white, the woman's fine and yellow, so the resemblance comes from the one which prevails or comes first."
Class Notes
  • The statement reflects the predominant case (الغالب) — not invariably so.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 434); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣٦
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عَبَّاسٍ قَالَ: قَالَتْ مَيْمُونَةُ: وَضَعْتُ لِلنَّبِيِّ ﷺ غُسْلًا فَسَتَرْتُهُ بِثَوْبٍ … فَغَسَلَ فَرْجَهُ … فَمَضْمَضَ وَاسْتَنْشَقَ وَغَسَلَ وَجْهَهُ وَذِرَاعَيْهِ، ثُمَّ صَبَّ عَلَى رَأْسِهِ وَأَفَاضَ عَلَى جَسَدِهِ، ثُمَّ تَنَحَّى فَغَسَلَ قَدَمَيْهِ، فَنَاوَلْتُهُ ثَوْبًا فَلَمْ يَأْخُذْهُ فَانْطَلَقَ وَهُوَ يَنْفُضُ يَدَيْهِ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās (← Maymūna)  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Translation
I set out water for the Prophet to wash and concealed him with a garment. He poured water on his hands and washed them, then poured water with his right hand over his left, then washed his private parts, then put his hand on the ground and wiped it. He then washed it, rinsed his mouth, snuffed up water, washed his face and forearms, then poured water over his head and emptied it out over his body, after which he moved aside and washed his feet. I handed him a garment, but he did not take it; he went off shaking his hands.
Class Notes
  • Note the ending (he declined the towel, shaking off the water) — relevant to 421 (drying after wuḍūʾ/ghusl).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 436); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ
حديث ٤٣٧
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ … إِنَّ امْرَأَةً مِنَ الْأَنْصَارِ سَأَلَتِ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ عَنْ غُسْلِهَا مِنَ الْمَحِيضِ … قَالَ: «خُذِي فِرْصَةً مِنْ مَسْكٍ فَتَطَهَّرِي بِهَا» قَالَتْ: كَيْفَ أَتَطَهَّرُ؟ قَالَ: «تَطَهَّرِي بِهَا» قَالَتْ: كَيْفَ؟ قَالَ: «سُبْحَانَ اللهِ، تَطَهَّرِي» فَاجْتَبَذْتُهَا إِلَيَّ فَقُلْتُ: تَتَبَّعِي بِهَا أَثَرَ الدَّمِ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Class Notes
  • Full commentary (Mirqāt) on firṣa / mask:
  • «("خُذِي فِرْصَةً") بِكَسْرِ الْفَاءِ: قِطْعَةٌ مِنْ صُوفٍ أَوْ قُطْنٍ أَوْ خِرْقَةٍ تَمْسَحُ بِهَا الْمَرْأَةُ مِنَ الْحَيْضِ، مِنْ فَرَصْتُ الشَّيْءَ إِذَا قَطَعْتَهُ. ("مِنْ مَسْكٍ"): بِفَتْحِ الْمِيمِ وَهُوَ الْجِلْدُ، وَفِي نُسْخَةٍ بِالْكَسْرِ وَهُوَ طِيبٌ مَعْرُوفٌ. قَالَ الطِّيبِيُّ: … فَالْمَعْنَى مُطَيَّبَةٌ مِنْ مِسْكٍ … وَفِي شَرْحِ السُّنَّةِ: أَيْ خُذِي قِطْعَةً مِنْ صُوفٍ مُطَيَّبَةٍ بِمِسْكٍ، وَأَنْكَرَ الْقُتَيْبِيُّ هَذَا؛ لِأَنَّهُمْ لَمْ يَكُونُوا أَهْلَ وُسْعٍ يَجِدُونَ الْمِسْكَ … فَقَالُوا: الرِّوَايَةُ بِفَتْحِ الْمِيمِ، مِنْ مَسْكٍ أَيْ مِنْ جِلْدٍ عَلَيْهِ صُوفٌ. قَالَ التُّورِبِشْتِيُّ: هَذَا الْقَوْلُ أَمْتَنُ وَأَحْسَنُ وَأَشْبَهُ بِصُورَةِ الْحَالِ، وَلَوْ كَانَ الْمَعْنَى أَنَّهَا مُطَيَّبَةٌ بِالْمِسْكِ لَقَالَ: فَتَطَيَّبِي … وَأَمَرَهَا بِذَلِكَ لِإِزَالَةِ الدَّمِ عِنْدَ التَّطَهُّرِ، وَلَوْ كَانَ لِإِزَالَةِ الرَّائِحَةِ لَأَمَرَ بِهَا بَعْدَ إِزَالَةِ الدَّمِ. … (تَتَبَّعِي بِهَا أَثَرَ الدَّمِ): أَيِ اجْعَلِيهَا فِي الْفَرْجِ وَحَيْثُ أَصَابَهُ الدَّمُ لِلتَّنْظِيفِ أَوْ لِقَطْعِ رَائِحَةِ الْأَذَى.»
  • مَسْك (mask, fatḥa) = leather/hide ("a piece of leather with wool on it"); مِسْك (misk, kasra) = musk/perfume. al-Ṭībī takes it as "perfumed with musk"; al-Qutaybī & al-Tūribishtī reject that — they were not wealthy enough to find musk — so the stronger reading is مَسْك = wooled leather, since he said «تطهري» (purify) not «تطيبي» (perfume), and ordered it to remove blood at purification, not afterward for scent.
  • Why she kept asking: she thought there was a special method; the Prophet ﷺ was reticent (ḥayāʾ), so ʿĀʾisha clarified: «تتبعي بها أثر الدم» = wipe where the blood was, to cleanse / cut the odor.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 437); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٣٨
«وَعَنْ أُمِّ سَلَمَةَ قَالَتْ: قُلْتُ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ إِنِّي امْرَأَةٌ أَشُدُّ ضَفْرَ رَأْسِي، فَأَنْقُضُهُ لِغُسْلِ الْجَنَابَةِ؟ قَالَ: «لَا، إِنَّمَا يَكْفِيكِ أَنْ تَحْثِي عَلَى رَأْسِكِ ثَلَاثَ حَثَيَاتٍ ثُمَّ تُفِيضِينَ عَلَيْكِ الْمَاءَ فَتَطْهُرِينَ». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Umm Salama  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Umm Salama said she told God's messenger that she kept her hair closely plaited, and asked whether she should undo it when washing after sexual defilement. He replied, "No, it is enough for you to throw three handfuls over your head, then pour water over yourself, and you will be purified."
Class Notes
  • Must she untie the braids? Ibrāhīm al-Nakhaʿī: yes (proof: Ibn ʿUmar commanded his women to unbraid). Jamhūr: no, as long as water reaches the roots; supported by a similar ʿĀʾisha narration.
  • Reconciling Ibn ʿUmar: either (1) because water wouldn't reach the roots otherwise (and if it can't, untying is obligatory); or (2) untying was merely preferable.
  • Why three scoops (ثلاث حثيات)? because the water usually reaches the roots that way. al-Qāḍī ʿIyāḍ: Ḥudhayfa told his wife to be thorough in making water reach the roots — unpacking the summarized wording.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 438); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤٠
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ قَالَتْ: كُنْتُ أَغْتَسِلُ أَنَا وَرَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ مِنْ إِنَاءٍ بَيْنِي وَبَيْنَهُ وَاحِدٍ فَيُبَادِرُنِي حَتَّى أَقُولَ: دَعْ لِي دَعْ لِي. قَالَتْ: وَهُمَا جُنُبَانِ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Translation
'A'isha said: God's messenger and I used to wash from one vessel which stood between us, and he would get ahead of me, so that I would say, "Give me a chance, give me a chance." She said that they had had sexual intercourse.
Class Notes
  • Teaches: a junub's hands in a vessel don't make the water impure; and keep household matters private — ʿĀʾisha relates it only for the sake of teaching the religion.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 440); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤١
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ قَالَتْ: سُئِلَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ عَنِ الرَّجُلِ يَجِدُ الْبَلَلَ وَلَا يَذْكُرُ احْتِلَامًا، قَالَ: «يَغْتَسِلُ»، وَعَنِ الرَّجُلِ يَرَى أَنَّهُ قَدِ احْتَلَمَ وَلَمْ يَجِدْ بَلَلًا، قَالَ: «لَا غُسْلَ عَلَيْهِ». قَالَتْ أُمُّ سَلَمَةَ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ هَلْ عَلَى الْمَرْأَةِ تَرَى ذَلِكَ غُسْلٌ؟ قَالَ: «نَعَمْ، إِنَّ النِّسَاءَ شَقَائِقُ الرِّجَالِ»»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Class Notes
  • Mirqāt glosses «يجد البلل» = «مَنِيًّا كَانَ أَوْ مَذْيًا».
  • Ibn Nujaym's 14 scenarios (from teacher's commentary — not in our Mirqat/Lamaat cache; kept as your note):
  • Fluids: m = منيّ (sperm); w = وديّ (thick fluid, sometimes with urine/age); z = مذيّ (pre-coital). 7 base cases × 2 (remembers a wet dream / doesn't) = 14: certainty m / w / z; doubt m·z / z·w / m·w / m·w·z.
  • CaseRemembers wet dreamDoesn't remember
    Certainty mghuslghusl
    Certainty wno ghuslno ghusl
    Certainty zghuslno ghusl
    Any doubt (m·z, z·w, m·w, m·w·z)ghuslno ghusl
  • Rule of thumb: m means always ghusl; w means never; z & all doubt means ghusl only if a wet dream is remembered. Net: half (7) require ghusl, half don't.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 441); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤٢
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ … إِذَا جَاوَزَ الْخِتَانُ الْخِتَانَ وَجَبَ الْغُسْلُ، فَعَلْتُهُ أَنَا وَرَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ فَاغْتَسَلْنَا»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Proof of abrogation of «الماء من الماء»: ghusl is obligatory when the two circumcised parts meet, regardless of emission.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 442); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤٣
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ … «تَحْتَ كُلِّ شَعْرَةٍ جَنَابَةٌ فَاغْسِلُوا الشَّعْرَ وَأَنْقُوا الْبَشَرَةَ»»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra
Class Notes
  • Abu Dawud, Tirmidhi and Ibn Majah transmitted it, and Tirmidhi said, "This is a gharib tradition, and al-Harith b. Wajih who is the transmitter is a shaikh one cannot trust."
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 443); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤٥
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهَا قَالَتْ: كَانَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ لَا يَتَوَضَّأُ بَعْدَ الْغُسْلِ. رَوَاهُ أَبُو دَاوُدَ وَالتِّرْمِذِيُّ وَالنَّسَائِيُّ وَابْنُ مَاجَهْ»
Narrated by A'isha
Class Notes
  • Two reasons: (1) he had already made wuḍūʾ before the ghusl; (2) purification from the minor ḥadath is subsumed within the major — ghusl lifts the minor along with the major.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 445); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤٧
«وَعَنْ يَعْلَى: أَنَّ رَسُولَ اللهِ ﷺ رَأَى رَجُلًا يَغْتَسِلُ بِالْبَرَازِ، فَصَعِدَ الْمِنْبَرَ فَحَمِدَ اللهَ وَأَثْنَى عَلَيْهِ وَقَالَ: «إِنَّ اللهَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ حَيِيٌّ سِتِّيرٌ يُحِبُّ الْحَيَاءَ وَالسَّتْرَ، فَإِذَا اغْتَسَلَ أَحَدُكُمْ فَلْيَسْتَتِرْ»»
Narrated by Yaʿlā b. Umayya  ·  Grade: ḥasan
Class Notes
  • Cover yourself except for a need, because Allah is ḥayiyy and sittīr; cover even in seclusion (don't sleep shirtless/in boxers); keep modesty even between spouses.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 447); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٤٨
«إِنَّمَا كَانَ الْمَاءُ مِنَ الْمَاءِ رُخْصَةً فِي أَوَّلِ الْإِسْلَامِ ثُمَّ نُهِيَ عَنْهَا»
Narrated by Ubayy b. Kaʿb  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Ubayy b. Ka'b told that the saying, "Water is necessary only when there is an emission," was a licence granted in the early days of Islam, but that afterwards it was prohibited.
Class Notes
  • The explicit proof of abrogation for «الماء من الماء» (431): an early concession (rukhṣa), later forbidden.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 448); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ — الفصل الثاني
حديث ٤٥٠
«وَعَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: كَانَتِ الصَّلَاةُ خَمْسِينَ، وَالْغُسْلُ مِنَ الْجَنَابَةِ سَبْعَ مِرَارٍ، وَغَسْلُ الْبَوْلِ مِنَ الثَّوْبِ سَبْعَ مِرَارٍ، فَلَمْ يَزَلْ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ يُسْأَلُ حَتَّى جُعِلَتِ الصَّلَاةُ خَمْسًا، وَالْغُسْلُ مِنَ الْجَنَابَةِ مَرَّةً، وَغَسْلُ الْبَوْلِ مِنَ الثَّوْبِ مَرَّةً»
Narrated by ʿAbdullāh b. ʿUmar  ·  Grade: (lam tatimm dirāsatuh)
Translation
Ibn 'Umar said that the original requirement was fifty times of prayer, seven washings to remove sexual defilement, and seven washings when urine fell on a garment. God's messenger therefore kept on making requests till five times of prayer were appointed, one washing for sexual defilement, and one washing of a garment because of urine. Abu Dawud transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • Two opinions on the "seven": from the previous Sharīʿa, or from the early period of Islam.
  • al-Ṭībī on the "fifty": either prayer was made obligatory at the Miʿrāj as fifty, or the previous nations' prayers were fifty.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 450); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥١
«عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: لَقِيَنِي رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ وَأَنَا جُنُبٌ فَأَخَذَ بِيَدِي فَمَشَيْتُ مَعَهُ حَتَّى قَعَدَ فَانْسَلَلْتُ فَأَتَيْتُ الرَّحْلَ فَاغْتَسَلْتُ … فَقَالَ: «سُبْحَانَ اللهِ، إِنَّ الْمُؤْمِنَ لَا يَنْجُسُ»»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Class Notes
  • الرَّحْل (al-raḥl) = dwelling/lodging (originally a traveler's mount-gear/encampment); فَانْسَلَلْتُ = I slipped away.
  • junub (جُنُب) = one who is in a state of major ritual impurity (جَنَابَة, janāba). The root sense of the word is to keep away or be distant — hence the junub keeps away from worship until he performs ghusl.
  • Mirqāt — the believer/junub is not najis; and «إنما المشركون نجس» (Tawba 9:28):
  • «("إِنَّ الْمُؤْمِنَ لَا يَنْجَسُ") بِفَتْحِ الْجِيمِ أَيْ: لَا يَصِيرُ عَيْنُهُ نَجِسًا، وَهَذَا غَيْرُ مُخْتَصٍّ بِالْمُؤْمِنِ بَلِ الْكَافِرُ كَذَلِكَ، وَأَمَّا قَوْلُهُ تَعَالَى: {إِنَّمَا الْمُشْرِكُونَ نَجَسٌ} فَالنَّجَاسَةُ فِي اعْتِقَادَاتِهِمْ لَا فِي أَصْلِ خِلْقَتِهِمْ، وَمَا رُوِيَ عَنِ ابْنِ عَبَّاسٍ مِنْ أَنَّ أَعْيَانَهُمْ نَجِسَةٌ كَالْخِنْزِيرِ، وَعَنِ الْحَسَنِ: مَنْ صَافَحَهُمْ فَلْيَتَوَضَّأْ — فَمَحْمُولٌ عَلَى الْمُبَالَغَةِ فِي التَّبَعُّدِ عَنْهُمْ وَالِاحْتِرَازِ مِنْهُمْ … وَفِي شَرْحِ السُّنَّةِ: فِيهِ جَوَازُ مُصَافَحَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمُخَالَطَتِهِ … قَالَ الْقَاضِي: … لَا يَنْجَسُ أَيْ: حَقِيقَةً لَا حُكْمًا، بِخِلَافِ الْكَافِرِ فَإِنَّهُ نَجِسٌ بَاطِنًا لِنَجَاسَةِ اعْتِقَادِهِ.»
  • The believer's (and disbeliever's) body never becomes najis. {The mushrikūn are najas} = impurity in belief (iʿtiqād), not in creation (khilqa). Ibn ʿAbbās's "like the pig" and al-Ḥasan's "make wudūʾ after shaking their hands" = hyperbole urging distance. Shaking hands / mixing with the junub is permitted. The believer is not najis even while in a state of major ritual impurity (حَالَ الْجَنَابَةِ).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 451); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥٢
«وَعَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ عُمَرَ … ذَكَرَ عُمَرُ بْنُ الْخَطَّابِ لِرَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ أَنَّهُ تُصِيبُهُ الْجَنَابَةُ مِنَ اللَّيْلِ، فَقَالَ لَهُ: «تَوَضَّأْ وَاغْسِلْ ذَكَرَكَ ثُمَّ نَمْ»»
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar (about ʿUmar)  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Translation
Ibn 'Umar told how 'Umar b. al-Khattab mentioned to God's messenger that he was affected by seminal defilement during the night, and was told to perform ablution, wash his penis, and go back to sleep.
Class Notes
  • The variant «أَيَرْقُدُ أَحَدُنَا وَهُوَ جُنُبٌ؟» uses يَرْقُدُ (yarqud) = to sleep/lie down (= «نَم»). Answer: yes, after wuḍūʾ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 452); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥٣
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ … كَانَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ إِذَا كَانَ جُنُبًا فَأَرَادَ أَنْ يَأْكُلَ أَوْ يَنَامَ تَوَضَّأَ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Translation
'Aisha said that when the Prophet was defiled and wanted to eat or sleep, he performed the ablution prescribed for prayer.
Class Notes
  • Dāwūd al-Ẓāhirī & Ibn Ḥabīb al-Mālikī: a junub must make wuḍūʾ even for ordinary acts (eating, drinking) — proof = 452 & 453.
  • Jamhūr: it's mustaḥabb, not obligatory. Four proofs: (1) 468 (he slept junub without wuḍūʾ); (2) Maymūna's narration (adds eating); (3) ʿĀʾisha's (slept and did not touch water); (4) Ibn Khuzayma: «إِنَّمَا أُمِرْتُ بِالْوُضُوءِ إِذَا قُمْتُ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ» (wuḍūʾ only when standing for prayer).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 453); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥٤
«وَعَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ الْخُدْرِيِّ … «إِذَا أَتَى أَحَدُكُمْ أَهْلَهُ ثُمَّ أَرَادَ أَنْ يَعُودَ فَلْيَتَوَضَّأْ بَيْنَهُمَا وُضُوءًا». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Ẓāhirīs: the command = obligation; the rest: no. Ibn Khuzayma adds it is «أَنْشَط» (anshaṭ) — more invigorating/reviving to repeat. ʿĀʾisha: the Prophet would repeat without wuḍūʾ in between (so it's not obligatory).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 454); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥٥
«وَعَنْ أَنَسٍ قَالَ: كَانَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ يَطُوفُ عَلَى نِسَائِهِ بِغُسْلٍ وَاحِدٍ. رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Anas said that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) used to go round to all his wives with a single washing.
Class Notes
  • Asked if he could visit all his wives in one night — he had the strength of thirty (other narrations: forty) men. Abū Nuʿaym, from Mujāhid: "forty men of Paradise." From al-Tirmidhī: each person of Paradise gets the strength of a hundred men, so ≈ 40 × 100 = four thousand.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 455); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥٦
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ قَالَتْ: كَانَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ يَذْكُرُ اللهَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ عَلَى كُلِّ أَحْيَانِهِ. رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
'A'isha said that the Prophet used to mention God's name on all occasions.
Class Notes
  • Three meanings of «على كل أحيانه»: (1) tongue-dhikr, meaning abundantly, not literally every moment (not in the lavatory); (2) tongue-dhikr specific to the prescribed adhkār (الأدعية المسنونة); (3) heart-remembrance (dhikr al-qalb), meaning truly at all times.
  • Contradiction with «إِنِّي كَرِهْتُ أَنْ أَذْكُرَ اللهَ إِلَّا عَلَى طُهْرٍ» (see 467): (1) the "dislike" = khilāf al-awlā, not prohibition (doing it without purity showed permissibility); (2) the dislike applies to tongue-dhikr outside ritual purity (heart-dhikr unrestricted).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 456); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٥٧
«عَنِ ابْنِ عَبَّاسٍ … اغْتَسَلَ بَعْضُ أَزْوَاجِ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ فِي جَفْنَةٍ، فَأَرَادَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ أَنْ يَتَوَضَّأَ مِنْهُ، فَقَالَتْ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ إِنِّي كُنْتُ جُنُبًا، فَقَالَ: «إِنَّ الْمَاءَ لَا يُجْنِبُ»»
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Leftover water of a junub is not najāsa. Grammar: reading يُجْنِب (form IV) vs يَجْنُب (form I) — the verb is lāzim (intransitive).
  • Masʾala (suʾr / leftover water of the opposite sex): Aḥmad: a man may not use a woman's leftover water; a woman may use a man's. Some Ẓāhirīs: no one may. Jamhūr: both permissible. Reason for the restriction: there's a place of desire (محل الشهوة) with a non-maḥram woman.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 457); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦١
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ … «لَا تَقْرَأُ الْحَائِضُ وَلَا الْجُنُبُ شَيْئًا مِنَ الْقُرْآنِ»»
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
Ibn 'Umar reported God's messenger as saying, "The woman who is menstruating and the one who is seminally defiled must not recite any of the Qur'an." Tirmidhi transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • Touching the Qurʾān:
  • Ẓāhirīs: permissible — proof: the Prophet ﷺ sent letters containing Qurʾān to disbelievers.
  • Jamhūr (not permissible): (1) the ʿAmr b. Ḥazm letter (= 465) «أَنْ لَا يَمَسَّ الْقُرْآنَ إِلَّا طَاهِرٌ»; (2) the āya «لَا يَمَسُّهُ إِلَّا الْمُطَهَّرُونَ» (al-Wāqiʿa 56:79). Mirqāt (al-Ṭībī): the pronoun in «لا يمسه» is either the Qurʾān (so people are forbidden to touch it without ṭahāra) or the Preserved Tablet (اللوح) (then «لا» purely negates and «المطهرون» = the angels); the hadith shows the first is meant.
  • Response to the Ẓāhirī proof: (1) a letter (a few verses) ≠ the Muṣḥaf; (2) it was for necessity (daʿwa).
  • Reciting the Qurʾān:
  • Ẓāhirīs: yes. Jamhūr: no. Mālik: two views — (a) no; (b) yes, only for memorization.
  • Within the Ḥanafī school (this internal dispute is from the teacher's commentary — not in our Mirqat/Lamaat cache): a full āya or more: prohibited by consensus; less than an āya: disputed — al-Karkhī: impermissible (it's still Qurʾān); al-Ṭaḥāwī: permissible (less than an āya isn't "Qurʾān," since the taḥaddī/iʿjāz is by a full āya). Books of al-Hidāya / Kanz / al-Baḥr prefer al-Karkhī; a fatwa-summary (al-Bazzāziyya?) prefers al-Ṭaḥāwī. Resolution: al-Karkhī out of precaution; but for teaching, fatwā = al-Ṭaḥāwī, taught fragmented (مقطّعاً) (Ibn ʿĀbidīn). By consensus: «بسم الله الرحمن الرحيم» and «الحمد لله» may be said as duʿāʾ; reciting a Qurʾānic dhikr intending it — Ḥanafīs permit, Shāfiʿīs don't.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 461); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦٢
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ … وَجِّهُوا هَذِهِ الْبُيُوتَ عَنِ الْمَسْجِدِ، فَإِنِّي لَا أُحِلُّ الْمَسْجِدَ لِحَائِضٍ وَلَا جُنُبٍ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
'A'isha reported God's messenger as saying, "Turn these houses so as not to face the mosque, for I do not make the mosque lawful for a menstruating woman or one who is seminally defiled." Abu Dawud transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • Lamaʿāt (the four positions): «فَعِنْدَ أَبِي حَنِيفَةَ لَا يَجُوزُ لِلْجُنُبِ وَلَا لِلْحَائِضِ الدُّخُولُ فِي الْمَسْجِدِ لَا بِالْمُكْثِ وَلَا بِالْمُرُورِ، وَجَوَّزَ الشَّافِعِيُّ الْمُرُورَ، وَبِهِ قَالَ مَالِكٌ، وَجَوَّزَ أَحْمَدُ الْمُكْثَ أَيْضًا» — Abū Ḥanīfa: no entry at all (neither lingering nor passing); al-Shāfiʿī & Mālik: passing through (murūr) only; Aḥmad: even lingering (mukth).
  • Ẓāhirī proofs & Ḥanafī responses (from teacher's commentary — āya & responses not in our Lamaat cache; kept as your note): they read «الصلاة» in «لا تقربوا الصلاة … ولا جنباً إلا عابري سبيل» (al-Nisāʾ 4:43) as the masjid; we read «عابري سبيل» = travellers (tayammum). Responses: (1) a prohibitor takes precedence over a permitter; (2) the permitting hadith are abrogated; (3) «عابري سبيل» literally = a traveller.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 462); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦٣
«وَعَنْ عَلِيٍّ … «لَا تَدْخُلُ الْمَلَائِكَةُ بَيْتًا فِيهِ صُورَةٌ وَلَا كَلْبٌ وَلَا جُنُبٌ»»
Narrated by ʿAlī  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf (Abū Dāwūd, al-Nasāʾī)
Translation
The angels do not enter a house in which there is a picture, a dog, or one who is defiled.
Class Notes
  • Full commentary (Mirqāt):
  • «("الْمَلَائِكَةُ"): اللَّامُ لِلْعَهْدِ الذِّهْنِيِّ، أَيِ الَّذِينَ يَنْزِلُونَ بِالْبَرَكَةِ وَالرَّحْمَةِ وَلِلزِّيَارَةِ وَاسْتِمَاعِ الذِّكْرِ، لَا الْكَتَبَةُ فَإِنَّهُمْ لَا يُفَارِقُونَ الْمُكَلَّفِينَ طَرْفَةَ عَيْنٍ … ("بَيْتًا فِيهِ صُورَةٌ"): أَيْ لِحَيَوَانٍ عَلَى شَيْءٍ مُرْتَفِعٍ كَالْجِدَارِ وَالسَّقْفِ، لَا عَلَى الْبِسَاطِ وَمَوْضِعِ الْأَقْدَامِ … لِحُرْمَةِ التَّصْوِيرِ وَمُشَابَهَتِهِ بَيْتَ الْأَصْنَامِ، بِخِلَافِ صُورَةِ مَا لَا رُوحَ فِيهِ، وَالصُّورَةِ الَّتِي فُقِدَ مِنْ بَدَنِهَا … كَالرَّأْسِ … وَبِخِلَافِ الصُّورَةِ الَّتِي … عَلَى مَا يُدَاسُ أَوْ يُتَّكَأُ عَلَيْهِ … ("وَلَا كَلْبٌ"): لِأَنَّهُ نَجِسٌ وَهُمْ أَطْهَارٌ … غَيْرَ كَلْبِ الصَّيْدِ وَالزَّرْعِ وَالْمَاشِيَةِ … ("وَلَا جُنُبٌ"): أَيِ الَّذِي اعْتَادَ تَرْكَ الْغُسْلِ تَهَاوُنًا حَتَّى يَمُرَّ عَلَيْهِ وَقْتُ صَلَاةٍ … لَا أَيَّ جُنُبٍ كَانَ، فَإِنَّهُ ثَبَتَ أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ كَانَ يَنَامُ بِاللَّيْلِ وَهُوَ جُنُبٌ إِلَى مَا بَعْدَ الْفَجْرِ.»
  • The angels (lām al-ʿahd) = those of mercy/blessing/visitation/hearing dhikr — not the recording angels (الكتبة), who never leave the mukallaf for a blink.
  • Image (ṣūra): an animal-image on something raised (wall/ceiling), not on a carpet/floor — barred for resembling idol-houses (مضاهاة). Fine: soulless images; headless (no longer dhū rūḥ); images on what is trodden/leaned on (also: so small the features can't be made out).
  • Dog: barred as najis — except hunting/farming/herding dogs (lawful to keep out of need).
  • Junub: specifically one who habitually delays ghusl out of negligence (تهاوناً) until a prayer-time passes — not every junub (the Prophet ﷺ slept junub till after Fajr).
  • Your aside on photography (image as "transferred light" vs hand-fashioned imitation) — kept as your reflection.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 463); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦٥
«وَعَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ أَبِي بَكْرِ بْنِ مُحَمَّدِ بْنِ عَمْرِو بْنِ حَزْمٍ: أَنَّ فِي الْكِتَابِ الَّذِي كَتَبَهُ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ لِعَمْرِو بْنِ حَزْمٍ: «أَنْ لَا يَمَسَّ الْقُرْآنَ إِلَّا طَاهِرٌ»»
Narrated by ʿAbdullāh b. Abī Bakr b. Ḥazm (mursal)  ·  Grade: (lam tatimm dirāsatuh)
Translation
'Abdallah b. Abu Bakr b. Muhammad b. 'Amr b. Hazm told that in the letter God's messenger wrote to 'Amr b. Hazm there was the statement, "Only one who is pure must touch the Qur'an."
Class Notes
  • The jamhūr/Ḥanafī proof under 461 that the impure may not touch the Qurʾān.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 465); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦٧
«وَعَنِ الْمُهَاجِرِ بْنِ قُنْفُذٍ أَنَّهُ أَتَى النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ وَهُوَ يَبُولُ فَسَلَّمَ عَلَيْهِ فَلَمْ يَرُدَّ عَلَيْهِ حَتَّى تَوَضَّأَ، ثُمَّ اعْتَذَرَ إِلَيْهِ فَقَالَ: «إِنِّي كَرِهْتُ أَنْ أَذْكُرَ اللَّهَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ إِلَّا عَلَى طُهْرٍ»»
Narrated by al-Muhājir ibn Qunfudh  ·  Riwayah: Abū Dāwūd; al-Nasāʾī (partial, with an addition)
Daleel For
  • Not madhhab-specific in the notes — this is the hadith behind the "no dhikr except in purity" discussion at hadith 456, reconciled there as khilāf al-awlā (a dislike, not a prohibition), not tied to one school.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 467). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 456's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦٨
«وَعَنْ أُمِّ سَلَمَةَ … كَانَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ يُجْنِبُ ثُمَّ يَنَامُ ثُمَّ يَنْتَبِهُ ثُمَّ يَنَامُ»
Narrated by Umm Salama  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
Umm Salama said that God's messenger would become defiled, then go to sleep, then awake, then go to sleep again.
Class Notes
  • Jamhūr's proof (under 453) that the junub's wuḍūʾ is mustaḥabb, not obligatory.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 468); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٦٩
«وَعَنْ شُعْبَةَ … إِنَّ ابْنَ عَبَّاسٍ كَانَ إِذَا اغْتَسَلَ مِنَ الْجَنَابَةِ يُفْرِغُ بِيَدِهِ الْيُمْنَى عَلَى يَدِهِ الْيُسْرَى سَبْعَ مِرَارٍ ثُمَّ يَغْسِلُ فَرْجَهُ … ثُمَّ يَتَوَضَّأُ وُضُوءَهُ لِلصَّلَاةِ ثُمَّ يُفِيضُ عَلَى جِلْدِهِ الْمَاءَ، ثُمَّ يَقُولُ: هَكَذَا كَانَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ يَتَطَهَّرُ»
Narrated by Shuʿba ← Ibn ʿAbbās  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
Shu'ba said that when Ibn 'Abbas washed because of seminal defilement he emptied water seven times from his right hand over his left hand, then washed his private parts. Once he forgot how often he had emptied it, so he asked Shu'ba, but he replied that he did not know, so he said, "You should be ashamed of yourself. What prevents you from knowing?" Then he would perform his ablution for prayer, then pour water over his body and say, "Thus was God's messenger accustomed to purify himself." Abu Dawud transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • The abrogated ruling = the seven pourings — ties to 450 (seven to one).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 469); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٧١
«وَعَنِ الْحَكَمِ بْنِ عَمْرٍو قَالَ: نَهَى رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ أَنْ يَتَوَضَّأَ الرَّجُلُ بِفَضْلِ طَهُورِ الْمَرْأَةِ»
Narrated by al-Ḥakam b. ʿAmr  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ (Tirmidhī: ḥasan ṣaḥīḥ)
Translation
God's messenger forbade that a man should perform ablution with the water left over by his wife after purifying herself.
Class Notes
  • Read as makrūh tanzīhan (slight dislike), reconciled with 457 (where he used a wife's leftover water, showing permissibility).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 471); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ مُخَالَطَةِ الْجُنُبِ وَمَا يُبَاحُ لَهُ
حديث ٤٧٢ (٤٧٣)
«وَعَنْ حُمَيْدٍ الْحِمْيَرِيِّ قَالَ: لَقِيتُ رَجُلًا صَحِبَ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ أَرْبَعَ سِنِينَ كَمَا صَحِبَهُ أَبُو هُرَيْرَةَ، قَالَ: نَهَى رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ أَنْ تَغْتَسِلَ الْمَرْأَةُ بِفَضْلِ الرَّجُلِ أَوْ يَغْتَسِلَ الرَّجُلُ بِفَضْلِ الْمَرْأَةِ. زَادَ مُسَدَّدٌ: وَلْيَغْتَرِفَا جَمِيعًا. … وَزَادَ أَحْمَدُ فِي أَوَّلِهِ: نَهَى أَنْ يَمْتَشِطَ أَحَدُنَا كُلَّ يَوْمٍ أَوْ يَبُولَ فِي مُغْتَسَلٍ، وَرَوَاهُ ابْنُ مَاجَهْ عَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ سَرْجِسَ»
Narrated by Ḥumayd al-Ḥimyarī (← an unnamed Companion)  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Humaid al-Himyari said, 'I met a man who was a companion of the Prophet for four years, as was Abu Huraira. He said that God's messenger forbade that a woman should wash in water left over by a man, or that a man should wash in water left over by a woman.' Musaddad added that they should use it together. Abu Dawud and Nasa'i transmitted it, and Ahmad added at the beginning, 'He forbade any of us to use a comb every day, or pass water in a bathing place.' Ibn Majah transmitted it from 'Abdallah b. Sarjis.
Class Notes
  • The unnamed Companion (accompanied the Prophet four years, like Abū Hurayra): candidates include ʿAbdullāh b. Mughaffal (+ two more). Principle: an unnamed Ṣaḥābī does no harm (all are upright).
  • Combing (التَّرَجُّل / يمتشط) — don't do it every day: a sign of those obsessed with their own beauty, unless there's a need. (Read «كل يوم» literally, or "all the time.")
  • Urinating in the bathing place (مُغتَسَل) — see 474–475.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 472); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٧٤
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ … لَا يَبُولَنَّ أَحَدُكُمْ فِي الْمَاءِ الدَّائِمِ الَّذِي لَا يَجْرِي ثُمَّ يَغْتَسِلُ فِيهِ. مُتَّفَقٌ عَلَيْهِ»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Class Notes
  • Extreme Ẓāhirism: we (the jamhūr) extend the ruling to all filth, not just urine; the Ẓāhirīs literally restrict it to urine (so feces would be "fine") — their extreme literalism.
  • Your scooping/handful note (scooping doesn't necessarily make it mustaʿmal) + "if no intention" — kept; to confirm from teacher's source.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 474); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٧٥
«وَعَنْ جَابِرٍ قَالَ: نَهَى رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ أَنْ يُبَالَ فِي الْمَاءِ الرَّاكِدِ. رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Jābir b. ʿAbdullāh  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Jabir said that God's messenger forbade passing water in motionless water.
Class Notes
  • Urinating in the bathing place / motionless water — prohibited / makrūh taḥrīman; if the water flows away it's still disliked, because of waswasa (whisperings).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 475); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٧٦
«وَعَنِ السَّائِبِ بْنِ يَزِيدَ قَالَ: ذَهَبَتْ بِي خَالَتِي إِلَى النَّبِيِّ ﷺ فَقَالَتْ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ إِنَّ ابْنَ أُخْتِي وَجِعٌ، فَمَسَحَ رَأْسِي وَدَعَا لِي بِالْبَرَكَةِ، ثُمَّ تَوَضَّأَ فَشَرِبْتُ مِنْ وَضُوئِهِ، ثُمَّ قُمْتُ خَلْفَ ظَهْرِهِ فَنَظَرْتُ إِلَى خَاتَمِ النُّبُوَّةِ بَيْنَ كَتِفَيْهِ مِثْلَ زِرِّ الْحَجَلَةِ»
Narrated by al-Sāʾib b. Yazīd  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Class Notes
  • A young child (he drank from the wuḍūʾ water). Your note on his age ("less than eight") — to confirm.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 476); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٧٧
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ … «إِذَا كَانَ الْمَاءُ قُلَّتَيْنِ لَمْ يَحْمِلِ الْخَبَثَ»»
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • iḍṭirāb (instability) weakens it (your teacher's detail — not in our cache): the narrator al-Walīd b. Kathīr reports from four different people (sanad); the amount varies (1 / 3 qullas, 30 / 40 buckets) (matn); and qulla itself is ambiguous (قامة a man's height, قلة الجبل a mountain-top, a bucket — which size? by weight — which?). Anwar Shāh al-Kashmīrī commented on it (point not recoverable from your notes).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 477); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٧٨
«وَعَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ الْخُدْرِيِّ قَالَ: قِيلَ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ أَنَتَوَضَّأُ مِنْ بِئْرِ بُضَاعَةَ وَهِيَ بِئْرٌ يُلْقَى فِيهَا الْحِيَضُ وَلُحُومُ الْكِلَابِ وَالنَّتْنُ؟ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ: «إِنَّ الْمَاءَ طَهُورٌ لَا يُنَجِّسُهُ شَيْءٌ»»
Narrated by Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • The water-quantity masʾala (477 ↔ 478):
  • Agreed: water is intrinsically pure even if some impurity mixes in, so long as it keeps fluidity (سيلان) and thinness (رقّة); dispute is over what happens when impurity falls in (and the qulla amount).
  • Mālik & the Ẓāhirīs: impure only if a quality (taste/color/smell) changes, regardless of quantity. (Lamaʿāt: «فَمَا تَغَيَّرَ لَوْنُهُ أَوْ طَعْمُهُ أَوْ رِيحُهُ فَهُوَ قَلِيلٌ، وَمَا لَمْ يَتَغَيَّرْ فَكَثِيرٌ».) They cite 478 (and the Mālik riwāya «إلا ما غلب على ريحه وطعمه ولونه»).
  • Abū Ḥanīfa, al-Shāfiʿī, Aḥmad: depends on abundance vs scarcity; they differ on the threshold.
  • Ḥanafī gauges (Lamaʿāt):
    • raʾy al-mubtalā bih (the judgment of the afflicted person) — Shams al-Aʾimma: «الْمَذْهَبُ الظَّاهِرُ التَّحَرِّي وَالتَّفْوِيضُ إِلَى رَأْيِ الْمُبْتَلَى مِنْ غَيْرِ حُكْمٍ بِالتَّقْدِيرِ … وَهَذَا هُوَ الْأَصَحُّ.»
    • taḥrīk (تحريك): «يَكُونُ بِحَيْثُ لَا يَتَحَرَّكُ طَرَفُهُ عِنْدَ تَحْرِيكِ الْآخَرِ» — if agitating one side doesn't move the other, it's "much."
    • Area (مساحة): «وَاعْتَبَرَ أَبُو سُلَيْمَانَ الْجَوْزَجَانِيُّ الْكَثْرَةَ بِالْمَسَاحَةِ … فَقَوْمٌ ثَمَانِيَةً فِي ثَمَانِيَةٍ، وَقَوْمٌ خَمْسَةَ عَشَرَ … وَنُقِلَ عَنْ مُحَمَّدٍ حِينَ سُئِلَ … إِنْ كَانَ مِثْلَ مَسْجِدِي هَذَا فَكَثِيرٌ، فَقِيسَ … اثْنَيْ عَشَرَ فِي مِثْلِهَا … وَثَمَانِيَةً فِي ثَمَانٍ … وَقَالَ أَبُو عِصْمَةَ: كَانَ مُحَمَّدٌ يُوَقِّتُ عَشَرَةً فِي عَشَرَةٍ ثُمَّ رَجَعَ … وَعَامَّةُ الْمَشَايِخِ عَلَى عَشَرٍ فِي عَشَرٍ … وَقَالَ أَبُو اللَّيْثِ: وَعَلَيْهِ الْفَتْوَى.»— Correction to the lesson: the "ten-by-ten" area view is Abū Sulaymān al-Jawzajānī (الجوزجاني), not al-Jurjānī; Imām Muḥammad gauged it by his masjid (12×12 / 8×8) and fixed 10×10 then retracted; later mashāyikh settled on 10×10 (Abū al-Layth: the fatwā is on it).
  • Flowing water (الماء الجاري): exempt (by consensus); and whatever is in the status of flowing water is exempt.
  • Three proofs (Ḥanafī, that impurity affects water even without a quality changing): (1) 474 (still water — no "quality change" condition, so any impurity affects it); (2) the dog's lick requiring washing seven times; (3) "when one wakes from sleep, wash the hand thrice."
  • Response to Mālik: the «الـ» in 478 is li-l-ʿahd, meaning it refers to the well of Buḍāʿa, which was flowing; al-Ṭaḥāwī: «كَانَتْ بِئْرُ بُضَاعَةَ طَرِيقًا إِلَى الْبَسَاتِينِ فَهُوَ كَالنَّهْرِ» (it was a channel to the gardens, like a river). (Your note added al-Wāqidī also reported it was flowing — al-Wāqidī not in our Lamaat passage.) Rational proof: impure things were thrown in, so a quality would have changed if it were still — since it stayed pure, it must have been flowing.
  • Response to al-Shāfiʿī: the qullatayn hadith is weak for iḍṭirāb — see 477.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 478); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٧٩
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: سَأَلَ رَجُلٌ رَسُولَ اللهِ ﷺ فَقَالَ: يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ إِنَّا نَرْكَبُ الْبَحْرَ وَنَحْمِلُ مَعَنَا الْقَلِيلَ مِنَ الْمَاءِ، فَإِنْ تَوَضَّأْنَا بِهِ عَطِشْنَا، أَفَنَتَوَضَّأُ مِنْ مَاءِ الْبَحْرِ؟ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ: هُوَ الطَّهُورُ مَاؤُهُ، الْحِلُّ مَيْتَتُهُ»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Abu Huraira said that a man told God's messenger that he sailed on the sea taking a small quantity of water with him. As he would suffer from thirst if he used this for ablution, he asked whether he might use sea water for that purpose, and received the reply, "Its water is pure, and what dies a natural death in it is lawful food." Malik, Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud, Nasa'i, Ibn Majah and Darimi transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • Why the Companions were unsure about seawater: (1) the narration «تحت البحر نار» (Mirqāt: «خَبَرُ "تَحْتَ الْبَحْرِ نَارٌ وَتَحْتَ النَّارِ بَحْرٌ" حَتَّى عَدَّ سَبْعَةً ضَعِيفٌ اتِّفَاقًا» — agreed weak); (2) it's salty, unlike sweet water; (3) carcasses in the sea.
  • Sea animals: Mālik: all permissible. al-Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad: (likewise broad). Ḥanafīs (Lamaʿāt): only fish — «وَالْمُرَادُ بِالْمَيْتَةِ السَّمَكُ … وَلَيْسَ الْمُرَادُ الَّذِي مَاتَ فِي الْبَحْرِ وَهُوَ حَرَامٌ عِنْدَنَا، وَعِنْدَ مَالِكٍ وَالشَّافِعِيِّ وَأَحْمَدَ لَا بَأْسَ بِهِ.»
    • al-ṭāfī (fish that died and float): ḥarām for Ḥanafīs. Jābir's hadith (Lamaʿāt): «مَا أَلْقَاهُ الْبَحْرُ وَجَزَرَ عَنْهُ الْمَاءُ فَكُلُوهُ، وَمَا مَاتَ فِيهِ وَطَفَا فَلَا تَأْكُلُوا» (Abū Dāwūd, Ibn Mājah).
    • Specifier: «أُحِلَّتْ لَنَا مَيْتَتَانِ وَدَمَانِ: الْحُوتُ وَالْجَرَادُ، وَالْكَبِدُ وَالطِّحَالُ» — the lawful "dead" = fish & locusts (the two "bloods" = liver & spleen).
    • Their «صيد البحر» (al-Māʾida 5:96): they read it as mafʿūl (the hunted thing); we read ṣayd = the act of hunting (maṣdar), so the activity is permitted, not every creature.
    • Shaykh al-Hind: the hadith is about purity (ṭahāra), while the opponents argue eating — two matters; and a riwāya of the whale hadith has it as a big fish (حوت).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 479); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٨٠
«وَعَنْ أَبِي زَيْدٍ عَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ مَسْعُودٍ: أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ قَالَ لَهُ لَيْلَةَ الْجِنِّ: مَا فِي إِدَاوَتِكَ؟ قَالَ: قُلْتُ: نَبِيذٌ. فَقَالَ: تَمْرَةٌ طَيِّبَةٌ وَمَاءٌ طَهُورٌ. … وَزَادَ أَحْمَدُ وَالتِّرْمِذِيُّ: فَتَوَضَّأَ مِنْهُ. وَقَالَ التِّرْمِذِيُّ: أَبُو زَيْدٍ مَجْهُولٌ. وَصَحَّ عَنْ عَلْقَمَةَ عَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ مَسْعُودٍ قَالَ: لَمْ أَكُنْ لَيْلَةَ الْجِنِّ مَعَ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ. رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Ibn Masʿūd (via Abū Zayd; with the contrasting ʿAlqama report)  ·  Grade: (first part ḍaʿīf; the ʿAlqama report ṣaḥīḥ)
Class Notes
  • Lamaʿāt: Abū Ḥanīfa & Sufyān allowed wuḍūʾ with nabīdh when no pure water; al-Shāfiʿī, Aḥmad, Abū Yūsuf (and a riwāya from Abū Ḥanīfa) require tayammum — and Abū Ḥanīfa's retraction (رجوع) to this is reported. The dispute concerns a thin, sweet nabīdh that flows like water (your "thin then later thick" point).
  • Defects: Abū Zayd is majhūl (al-Tirmidhī; al-Bukhārī in the weak; al-Ḥākim: a majhūl with one hadith); and Ibn Masʿūd «لم أكن ليلة الجن».
  • Reconciliation (Lamaʿāt): «إِنَّ لَيْلَةَ الْجِنِّ مُتَعَدِّدَةٌ، وَكَانَتْ بِالْمَدِينَةِ كَمَا كَانَتْ بِمَكَّةَ» — the night of the jinn happened multiple times (Medina as well as Mecca), so Ibn Masʿūd could be absent on one and present on another.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 480); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمِيَاهِ
حديث ٤٩٤
«وَعَنْ سُلَيْمَانَ بْنِ يَسَارٍ قَالَ: سَأَلْتُ عَائِشَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهَا عَنِ الْمَنِيِّ يُصِيبُ الثَّوْبَ، فَقَالَتْ: كُنْتُ أَغْسِلُهُ مِنْ ثَوْبِ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَيَخْرُجُ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ وَأَثَرُ الْغَسْلِ فِي ثَوْبِهِ»
Narrated by Sulaymān b. Yasār ← ʿĀʾishah (R.A.)  ·  Riwayah: al-Bukhārī and Muslim (Muttafaq ʿAlayh)
Daleel For
  • Commonly cited in fiqh discussions on whether manī (semen) is najis — ʿĀʾisha's own practice of washing it from the garment. (The teacher's note at hadith 504 only flags a placement correction; the fuller madhhab dispute over manī's purity isn't spelled out in the cached notes.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 494). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 504's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ
حديث ٤٩٧
«وَعَنْ أُمِّ قَيْسٍ بِنْتِ مِحْصَنٍ: أَنَّهَا أَتَتْ بِابْنٍ لَهَا صَغِيرٍ لَمْ يَأْكُلِ الطَّعَامَ إِلَى رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ فَأَجْلَسَهُ فِي حِجْرِهِ فَبَالَ عَلَى ثَوْبِهِ، فَدَعَا بِمَاءٍ فَنَضَحَهُ وَلَمْ يَغْسِلْهُ»
Narrated by Umm Qays bint Miḥṣan  ·  Grade: muttafaq ʿalayh
Class Notes
  • The child's urine is impure; the dispute is over how to purify it.
  • al-Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad: a milk-only male infant: urine sprinkled (naḍḥ/rashsh); a female infant: washed (ghasl). (Lamaʿāt: «الْمَشْهُورُ مِنْ مَذْهَبِ الشَّافِعِيِّ وَأَحْمَدَ أَنَّهُ يَكْفِي فِي بَوْلِ الطِّفْلِ الَّذِي لَا يَطْعَمُ … إِلَّا اللَّبَنَ الرَّشُّ بِالْمَاءِ، وَيَتَعَيَّنُ فِي بَوْلِ الصَّبِيَّةِ الْغَسْلُ … مِنْ أَجْلِ التَّخْفِيفِ» — the concession is a lightening (تخفيف), not because the boy's urine is pure.)
  • Abū Ḥanīfa & Mālik: both must be washed. (Mirqāt, al-Ṭaḥāwī: «النَّضْحُ … الْمُرَادُ بِهِ الصَّبُّ … حُكْمُ بَوْلِ الْغُلَامِ الْغَسْلُ إِلَّا أَنَّهُ يُجْزِئُ فِيهِ الصَّبُّ … وَبَوْلُ الْجَارِيَةِ … لَا يَكْفِي فِيهِ الصَّبُّ؛ لِأَنَّ بَوْلَ الْغُلَامِ يَكُونُ فِي مَوْضِعٍ وَاحِدٍ لِضِيقِ مَخْرَجِهِ، وَبَوْلُ الْجَارِيَةِ يَتَفَرَّقُ … لِسَعَةِ مَخْرَجِهَا» — i.e. the boy/girl rule you didn't follow: the boy's outlet is narrow (urine collects in one spot, so a pour suffices), the girl's is wide (it spreads, so it must be scrubbed). For Ḥanafīs the naḍḥ here = a real washing.)
  • Our (Ḥanafī) proof: the muṭlaq (unqualified) urine-prohibition; "he washed his clothes from urine"; «اسْتَنْزِهُوا مِنَ الْبَوْلِ»; sprinkling merely spreads the urine; if impure, treat like any impurity.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 497); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ
حديث ٤٩٨
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عَبَّاسٍ … سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللهِ ﷺ يَقُولُ: «إِذَا دُبِغَ الْإِهَابُ فَقَدْ طَهُرَ». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • The jamhūr's proof that tanning makes a carcass skin pure. (Mirqāt, Ibn al-Malak: «حُجَّةٌ عَلَى مَالِكٍ فِي قَوْلِهِ جِلْدُ الْمَيْتَةِ لَا يَطْهُرُ بِالدِّبَاغِ، وَعَلَى الشَّافِعِيِّ فِي … جِلْدُ الْكَلْبِ … وَاسْتَثْنَى الْآدَمِيَّ تَكْرِيمًا، وَالْخِنْزِيرَ لِنَجَاسَةِ عَيْنِهِ».)
  • Positions: Mālik: carcass skin never becomes pure even tanned. Jamhūr: every skin except pig (and human, out of honor) becomes pure by tanning. al-Shāfiʿī: also excludes dog.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 498); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ
حديث ٥٠٣
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ … «إِذَا وَطِئَ أَحَدُكُمْ بِنَعْلِهِ الْأَذَى فَإِنَّ التُّرَابَ لَهُ طَهُورٌ»»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Abu Huraira reported God's messenger as saying, "When any of you treads with his sandal on something unclean, earth should be used to purify it."
Class Notes
  • Walking through filth (dung, etc.) — covers impurities and other than impurities (the naʿl/khuff alike).
  • Dry vs wet (Lamaʿāt): Abū Ḥanīfa — the hadith is about dry najāsa that clings and is removed by rubbing on the ground (دَلْك); wet najāsa won't rub off, so dryness is required. Abū Yūsuf, al-Shāfiʿī (qadīm), the jamhūr — any najāsa, wet or dry, on grounds of ease (رفع الحرج). Muḥammad: the khuff isn't purified (except dry semen) without washing.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 503); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ
حديث ٥٠٤
«وَعَنْ أُمِّ سَلَمَةَ … قَالَتْ لَهَا امْرَأَةٌ: إِنِّي امْرَأَةٌ أُطِيلُ ذَيْلِي وَأَمْشِي فِي الْمَكَانِ الْقَذِرِ، قَالَتْ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ: «يُطَهِّرُهُ مَا بَعْدَهُ»»
Narrated by Umm Salama  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
Umm Salama said that a woman told her she had a long skirt and walked in filthy places, so she told her that God's messenger had said, "What comes after it cleanses it."
Class Notes
  • (Mirqāt: «يُطَهِّرُهُ مَا بَعْدَهُ … بِزَوَالِ مَا يَتَشَبَّثُ بِالذَّيْلِ مِنَ الْقَذَرِ يَابِسًا») — the dry dirt on the hem is rubbed off by the clean ground after it.
  • Your "earlier 504 note about removing the devil's whispering (waswasa)" — the commentary does NOT tie waswasa to this hadith (it appears at 494, about rinsing blood "to settle the soul"). Re-read your note if you still want it placed here. Also the khuff disagreement (dry can be rubbed off vs wet must be washed) is the 503/504 point above.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 504); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ
حديث ٥٠٦
«وَعَنْ أَبِي الْمَلِيحِ بْنِ أُسَامَةَ عَنْ أَبِيهِ عَنِ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ: نَهَى عَنْ جُلُودِ السِّبَاعِ» — وزاد الترمذي والدارمي: «أَنْ تُفْتَرَشَ» (to be spread out and sat on)
Narrated by Usāma (b. ʿUmayr)  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) prohibited the use of the skins of beasts of prey. Ahmad, Abu Dawud and Nasa'i transmitted it; Tirmidhi and Darimi added the words "that they may be used as rugs."
Class Notes
  • (Your "a kind of sitting that is prohibitively disliked" = sitting on / spreading out predator-skins.) (Lamaʿāt: «إِنَّمَا نَهَى … لِأَنَّ ذَلِكَ مِنْ سِيرِ الْجَبَابِرَةِ وَدَأْبِ الْمُتَكَبِّرِينَ وَالْمُتْرَفِينَ، فَالنَّهْيُ لِلتَّنْزِيهِ، وَأَمَّا مَنْ يَذْهَبُ إِلَى … أَنَّ جُلُودَ الْمَيْتَةِ لَا تَطْهُرُ بِالدِّبَاغِ فَالنَّهْيُ عِنْدَهُ لِلتَّحْرِيمِ».) The prohibition is for dislike (tanzīh) — it's the way of tyrants/the arrogant; it becomes taḥrīm for those who hold maytah hair/skin isn't purified by tanning.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 506); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ
حديث ٥٠٨
«وَعَنْ عَبْدِ اللهِ بْنِ عُكَيْمٍ … أَتَانَا كِتَابُ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ: «أَنْ لَا تَنْتَفِعُوا مِنَ الْمَيْتَةِ بِإِهَابٍ وَلَا عَصَبٍ»»
Narrated by ʿAbdullāh b. ʿUkaym  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
The letter of God's Messenger came to us telling us not to make use of the skin or sinew of an animal which had died a natural death.
Class Notes
