  • Note (corrects the lesson): this (508), not the tanning hadith, is the apparent counter-evidence; the jamhūr's "tanning purifies" proof is 498.
  • Response (Mirqāt, al-Tūribishtī): «(مِنَ الْمَيْتَةِ بِإِهَابٍ): أَيْ قَبْلَ الدِّبَاغِ … قِيلَ إِنَّ هَذَا الْحَدِيثَ نَاسِخٌ … وَالْجُمْهُورُ عَلَى خِلَافِهِ؛ لِأَنَّهُ لَا يُقَاوِمُ تِلْكَ الْأَحَادِيثَ صِحَّةً وَاشْتِهَارًا، ثُمَّ إِنَّ ابْنَ عُكَيْمٍ لَمْ يَلْقَ النَّبِيَّ … وَلَوْ ثَبَتَ فَحَقُّهُ أَنْ يُحْمَلَ عَلَى نَهْيِ الِانْتِفَاعِ قَبْلَ الدِّبَاغِ» — «ihāb» = skin before tanning, so the prohibition is on benefiting from untanned skin; the claim that it abrogates the tanning hadith is rejected (it can't match them in soundness/fame; Ibn ʿUkaym never met the Prophet ﷺ).
  • "Type of leaf" you mentioned = the tanning agents qaraẓ / ʿafṣ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 508); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ — الفصل الثالث
حديث ٥١٤
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: كَانَتِ الْكِلَابُ تُقْبِلُ وَتُدْبِرُ فِي الْمَسْجِدِ فِي زَمَانِ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ فَلَمْ يَكُونُوا يَرُشُّونَ شَيْئًا مِنْ ذَلِكَ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ (al-Bukhārī)
Translation
Ibn 'Umar said that the dogs used to go here and there in the mosque in the time of God's messenger and that they did not sprinkle any place where they had been.
Class Notes
  • Your point — purification by drying: the ground needs no washing because its purification is its drying (Lamaʿāt: «هَذَا إِذَا لَمْ تَكُنِ الْكِلَابُ رَطْبَةً وَلَمْ تَنْفَصِلْ عَنْهَا نَجَاسَةٌ … لَمْ يَكُونُوا يَرُشُّونَ … لِمُجَرَّدِ إِقْبَالِ الْكِلَابِ وَإِدْبَارِهَا») — they didn't sprinkle, since the dogs weren't wet and no najāsa fell. (The exact maxim «ذكاة الأرض يبسها» isn't in our cache, but the concept is here.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 514); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ تَطْهِيرِ النَّجَاسَاتِ — الفصل الثالث
حديث ٥١٥
«وَعَنِ الْبَرَاءِ بْنِ عَازِبٍ … «لَا بَأْسَ بِبَوْلِ مَا يُؤْكَلُ لَحْمُهُ»»
Narrated by al-Barāʾ b. ʿĀzib  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
Al-Bara' b. 'Azib reported God's messenger as saying, "There is no harm in the urine of an animal whose flesh may be eaten." Ahmad and Daraqutni transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • Pure — Mālik, Aḥmad, al-Iṣṭakhrī (Shāfiʿī), and Muḥammad of the Ḥanafīs (Mirqāt, from al-Nawawī's Rawḍa). Proof includes the ʿUraniyyīn (العُرَنِيِّين) who drank camels' urine (أبوال الإبل) — (Mirqāt: «وَحَمَلَهُ أَبُو يُوسُفَ عَلَى التَّدَاوِي لِحَدِيثِ الْعُرَنِيِّينَ» — Abū Yūsuf reads it as for medicine).
  • We (Ḥanafīs — Abū Ḥanīfa & Abū Yūsuf): the urine of edible animals is impure (a light najāsa) — (Mirqāt: «وَلِلْجُمْهُورِ عُمُومُ حَدِيثِ "اسْتَنْزِهُوا مِنَ الْبَوْلِ؛ فَإِنَّ عَامَّةَ عَذَابِ الْقَبْرِ مِنْهُ"»; Lamaʿāt: «نَجِسٌ نَجَاسَةً خَفِيفَةً لِتَعَارُضِ الْآثَارِ، وَلَعَلَّ تَأْوِيلَ … أَنَّ الْمُرَادَ لَا بَأْسَ عَظِيمٌ») — the muṭlaq wording «استنزهوا من البول» is our proof; the permissive reports conflict / are weak, read as "no great harm."
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 515); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ
حديث ٥١٧
«وَعَنْ شُرَيْحِ بْنِ هَانِئٍ قَالَ: سَأَلْتُ عَلِيَّ بْنَ أَبِي طَالِبٍ عَنِ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ، فَقَالَ: جَعَلَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ ثَلَاثَةَ أَيَّامٍ وَلَيَالِيَهُنَّ لِلْمُسَافِرِ وَيَوْمًا وَلَيْلَةً لِلْمُقِيمِ. رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Shurayḥ b. Hāniʾ (← ʿAlī)  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Translation
I asked 'Ali b. Abu Talib about wiping over the shoes and he replied that God's messenger had appointed three days and nights as the period when a traveller may do it and one day and night as the period for one who is not travelling.
Class Notes
  • Mass-transmission & the marks of Ahl al-Sunna:
  • Wiping over the khuffs is mutawātir. (Mirqāt: «قَالَ الْحَسَنُ الْبَصْرِيُّ: أَدْرَكْتُ سَبْعِينَ نَفَرًا مِنَ الصَّحَابَةِ يَرَوْنَ الْمَسْحَ … وَلِذَا قَالَ أَبُو حَنِيفَةَ: مَا قُلْتُ بِالْمَسْحِ حَتَّى جَاءَنِي فِيهِ مِثْلُ ضَوْءِ النَّهَارِ. وَقَالَ الْكَرْخِيُّ: أَخَافُ الْكُفْرَ عَلَى مَنْ لَا يَرَى الْمَسْحَ … لِأَنَّ الْآثَارَ … فِي حَيِّزِ التَّوَاتُرِ».)
    • al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī: seventy Companions practiced it. Abū Ḥanīfa: "I did not hold wiping until proof came like the light of day." al-Karkhī: "I fear kufr for one who rejects it."
  • Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr: the narrators reached two hundred, and ijmāʿ was claimed; he says only Ibn ʿAbbās, ʿĀʾisha, and Abū Hurayra are reported to have denied it — and they retracted / it's confirmed against them. The report from ʿĀʾisha «لَأَنْ أَقْطَعَ رِجْلِي … أَحَبُّ إِلَيَّ مِنْ أَنْ أَمْسَحَ» is bāṭil (false).
  • The creedal marks (Mirqāt — Anas, asked the signs of Ahl al-Sunna wa-l-Jamāʿa): «أَنْ تُحِبَّ الشَّيْخَيْنِ، وَلَا تَطْعَنَ الْخَتَنَيْنِ، وَتَمْسَحَ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ» — love the two Shaykhs (Abū Bakr & ʿUmar), don't malign the two sons-in-law (ʿUthmān & ʿAlī), and wipe over the two socks.
  • Deniers: the Khawārij and the Rāfiḍa/Twelvers (Lamaʿāt: «لِأَجْلِ مَنْ طَعَنَ فِيهِ مِنْ أَهْلِ الْبِدَعِ مِنَ الْخَوَارِجِ وَالرَّوَافِضِ») — they argue a Qurʾānic farḍ (washing) can't be abrogated by an (āḥād) hadith. Our response: we're not abrogating, but adding (and it's near-tawātur); and the āya's washing applies when not wearing a khuff — no conflict. (Lamaʿāt notes masḥ is even held preferable to spite the innovators.)
  • Time limits:
  • Resident: one day & night; traveler: three days & nights. Mālik & some: no limit — (Mirqāt: «اتَّفَقُوا أَنَّهُ غَيْرُ مُضْطَرِبٍ لَا يُحْتَجُّ بِهِ» — the unrestricting report is unusable; ʿUmar's «أَصَبْتَ السُّنَّةَ» is opposed by the sound time-limit report, or he retracted).
  • Your teacher's extra points — not in our cache: the Ibn Abī ʿUmāra «سبعاً / ما بدا لك» hadith (= 518 in your numbering) being maʿlūl; the «لو استزدناه لزادنا» report; Ibn al-Mubārak's statement on the retraction. Kept as your notes.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 517); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ
حديث ٥١٨
«وَعَنْ عُرْوَةَ بْنِ الْمُغِيرَةِ بْنِ شُعْبَةَ عَنْ أَبِيهِ … أَنَّهُ غَزَا مَعَ رَسُولِ اللهِ ﷺ غَزْوَةَ تَبُوكَ … فَتَبَرَّزَ رَسُولُ اللهِ ﷺ قِبَلَ الْغَائِطِ فَحَمَلْتُ مَعَهُ إِدَاوَةً … فَغَسَلَ كَفَّيْهِ وَوَجْهَهُ، وَعَلَيْهِ جُبَّةٌ مِنْ صُوفٍ، فَذَهَبَ يَحْسِرُ عَنْ ذِرَاعَيْهِ فَضَاقَ كُمُّ الْجُبَّةِ فَأَخْرَجَ يَدَهُ مِنْ تَحْتِ الْجُبَّةِ … وَغَسَلَ ذِرَاعَيْهِ وَمَسَحَ بِنَاصِيَتِهِ وَعَلَى الْعِمَامَةِ وَعَلَى خُفَّيْهِ، ثُمَّ رَكِبَ … وَقَدْ صَلَّى بِهِمْ عَبْدُ الرَّحْمَنِ بْنُ عَوْفٍ رَكْعَةً …»
Narrated by al-Mughīra b. Shuʿba (via ʿUrwa)  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Set in the Battle of Tabūk (9 AH, the Prophet's ﷺ last great expedition, toward the Byzantine frontier). He wiped his forelock, turban, and khuffs.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 518); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ
حديث ٥٢١
«وَعَنِ الْمُغِيرَةِ بْنِ شُعْبَةَ قَالَ: وَضَّأْتُ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ فِي غَزْوَةِ تَبُوكَ فَمَسَحَ أَعْلَى الْخُفِّ وَأَسْفَلَهُ»
Narrated by al-Mughīra b. Shuʿba  ·  Grade: ḍaʿīf
Translation
I assisted the Prophet to perform ablution on the expedition to Tabuk, and he wiped the top and bottom of the shoe.
Class Notes
  • Weak (مَعْلُول) (both commentaries: «قَالَ التِّرْمِذِيُّ: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ مَعْلُولٌ، وَسَأَلْتُ أَبَا زُرْعَةَ وَمُحَمَّدًا — يَعْنِي الْبُخَارِيَّ — فَقَالَا: لَيْسَ بِصَحِيحٍ، وَكَذَا ضَعَّفَهُ أَبُو دَاوُدَ»): no rulings established from it.
  • The defect (Lamaʿāt): it is munqaṭiʿ — «لَمْ يَثْبُتِ اتِّصَالُهُ بِالْمُغِيرَةِ بَلْ بِالْوَرَّادِ كَاتِبِهِ … يَرْوِيهِ ثَوْرُ بْنُ يَزِيدَ عَنْ رَجَاءِ بْنِ حَيْوَةَ عَنْ كَاتِبِ الْمُغِيرَةِ عَنِ الْمُغِيرَةِ، وَثَوْرٌ لَمْ يَسْمَعْ هَذَا مِنْ رَجَاءٍ» (the chain is Thawr b. Yazīd ← Rajāʾ b. Ḥaywa ← al-Warrād, the scribe of al-Mughīra; Thawr didn't hear it from Rajāʾ; al-Dāraquṭnī: this riwāya has no mention of the underside). (So your "two weak narrators" = Thawr / Rajāʾ via the scribe al-Warrād — not "Abū Mūsā.")
  • Ruling: wipe only the top (Mirqāt: Shāfiʿī & Mālik — top wājib, bottom sunna; Aḥmad — top only; «إِنِ اقْتَصَرَ عَلَى أَعْلَاهُ أَجْزَأَهُ بِالِاتِّفَاقِ، وَإِنِ اقْتَصَرَ عَلَى أَسْفَلِهِ لَمْ يُجْزِئْهُ») — no benefit in wiping the bottom (it touches the ground).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 521); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ
حديث ٥٢٣
«وَعَنِ الْمُغِيرَةِ بْنِ شُعْبَةَ قَالَ: تَوَضَّأَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ وَمَسَحَ عَلَى الْجَوْرَبَيْنِ وَالنَّعْلَيْنِ. رَوَاهُ أَحْمَدُ وَالتِّرْمِذِيُّ وَأَبُو دَاوُدَ وَابْنُ مَاجَهْ»
Narrated by al-Mughīra b. Shuʿba  ·  Grade: ṣaḥīḥ
Class Notes
  • Conditions for wiping a cloth sock (Mirqāt / Lamaʿāt): it must be thick (ثَخِين, not شِفاف — water can't seep through), stand on the leg without tying (مُسْتَمْسِك), and be walkable («بِحَيْثُ يُمْكِنُ مُتَابَعَةُ الْمَشْيِ عَلَيْهِمَا»).
  • The split: al-Ṣāḥibān (Abū Yūsuf & Muḥammad) permit wiping the jawrab absolutely; Abū Ḥanīfa only if thick / munaʿʿal / mujallad — and he came to this later (آخِرًا), "and the fatwā is on it" (your "Abū Ḥanīfa retracted" point). al-Shāfiʿī forbids wiping a jawrab even if munaʿʿal.
  • Your "three Sharʿī miles = one farsakh" walkability figure isn't in our cache (the commentary just says "walkable"); kept as your note — and the equivalence 3 mīl = 1 farsakh is itself correct.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 523); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ
حديث ٥٢٤
«وَعَنِ الْمُغِيرَةِ بْنِ شُعْبَةَ قَالَ: مَسَحَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ، فَقُلْتُ: يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ، أَنَسِيتَ؟ قَالَ: بَلْ أَنْتَ نَسِيتَ، بِهَذَا أَمَرَنِي رَبِّي عَزَّ وَجَلَّ»
Narrated by al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah (R.A.)  ·  Riwayah: Aḥmad, Abū Dāwūd
Daleel For
  • Daleel for Imām Aḥmad & Abū Ḥanīfa — the base khuff-wiping report; hadith 525 extends the same reasoning (tanqīḥ al-manāṭ) to wiping over the cotton jawrab.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 524). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 525's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ
حديث ٥٢٥
«وَعَن عَليّ رَضِي الله عَنهُ قَالَ: لَوْ كَانَ الدِّينُ بِالرَّأْيِ لَكَانَ أَسْفَلُ الْخُفِّ أَوْلَى بِالْمَسْحِ مِنْ أَعْلَاهُ وَقَدْ رَأَيْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَمْسَحُ على ظَاهر خفيه»
Narrated by Ali (رضي الله عنه)  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud (al-Darimi similar)  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
'Ali said, "If the religion were based on opinion, it would be more important to wipe the under part of the shoe than the upper, but I have seen God's messenger wiping over the upper part of his shoes."
Class Notes
  • This continues the discussion of wiping over the cotton sock (jawrab) carried over from 523–524 (the leather khuff).
  • The Ṣaḥīḥayn and three of the imams held you may wipe over the jawrab; there is an established retraction attributed to one of the Ḥanafī authorities, after which there is effectively consensus on wiping over the cotton sock.
  • Objection: the hadith is weak — how can wiping over socks be established from it?
  • Response: the mujtahid works by extracting the ʿilla (effective cause) — this is tanqīḥ al-manāṭ (تنقيح المناط), an ijtihād that isolates the manāṭ/ʿilla and transfers the qualities of the leather sock onto the cotton sock.
  • See Mirqāt on the jawrab and the leather-faced jawrab (jawrab mujallad): wiping is fine for footwear (niʿāl) one can walk in. This answers the objection that "you are wiping over a sandal" — we are wiping over a sock, not a sandal.
  • Because this is a taʿabbudī (تعبدي) matter — a matter of pure worship — we do not override it with bare reason. This is also a proof relied on by Imām Aḥmad and Imām Abū Ḥanīfa.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 525); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ التَّيَمُّمِ
حديث ٥٢٦
«عَنْ حُذَيْفَةَ قَالَ قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «فُضِّلْنَا عَلَى النَّاسِ بِثَلَاثٍ جُعِلَتْ صُفُوفُنَا كَصُفُوفِ الْمَلَائِكَةِ وَجُعِلَتْ لَنَا الْأَرْضُ كلهَا مَسْجِدا وَجعلت تربَتهَا لنا طَهُورًا إِذَا لَمْ نَجِدِ الْمَاءَ»»
Narrated by Hudhaifa  ·  Riwayah: Muslim  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
We have been made superior to mankind in three respects: our ranks are formed like those of the angels; all the earth has been appointed as a mosque for us; and our earth has been appointed for us as a means of cleansing when we do not find water.
Class Notes
  • This hadith opens the chapter and states a special favour for this nation: the earth/soil was made a means of purification (the word here = تُرَاب / صَعِيد, soil/dust).
  • Tayammum — definition & terminology:
  • Linguistically: tayammum = intention, to intend — al-qaṣd (قصد). Cf. Sūrat al-Baqara 2:267, "wa-lā tayammamū…" (do not intend/aim at the bad to spend from it).
  • In the Sharīʿa: applying/wiping the pure earth in a specific manner.
  • Why intention (niyya) is obligatory in tayammum:
  • Because we carry over the linguistic sense (qaṣd), niyya is obligatory — unlike its origin (wuḍūʾ / ghusl).
    • Wuḍūʾ is physical purification, so (on the Ḥanafī view) intention is not a condition for its validity, only for reward.
    • Tayammum is ḥukmī (legal/effective) purification, not physical — so you need intention to make dust, which ordinarily soils, into a means of purity.
  • The word qaṣd sits inside the very name ta-yammum. It works for both minor (ḥadath aṣghar) and major (ḥadath akbar) impurity.
  • Number of strikes (ḍarba):
  • Imām Aḥmad & Imām Isḥāq: one strike — wipe the face and the palms.
  • The other three imams (Abū Ḥanīfa, al-Shāfiʿī, Mālik): two strikes — to the elbows.
  • Evidence:
  • Aḥmad's proof: hadiths 527, 528, 529.
  • Ḥanafī proof: the Qurʾānic verse names two limbs, each taking its own ruling — wipe the face and the hands/arms; plus the narration of Jābir: "Tayammum is two strikes — a strike for the face and a strike for the [arms to the] elbows." The like is related from Umāma, ʿAmmār & al-Bazzār, ʿĀʾisha, Ibn ʿUmar and Abū Hurayra.
  • On the one-strike reports: we read them as summarised (mukhtaṣar) narrations — the same narrator (e.g. ʿAmmār) also relates two strikes elsewhere; the fuller report governs the abridged one.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 526); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ التَّيَمُّمِ
حديث ٥٢٧
«وَعَن عمرَان بن حُصَيْن الْخُزَاعِيِّ أَنَّ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: رأى رجلا مُعْتَزِلا لم يصل فِي الْقَوْم فَقَالَ: «يَا فلَان مَا مَنعك أَن تصلي فِي الْقَوْم فَقَالَ يَا رَسُول الله أَصَابَتْنِي جَنَابَةٌ وَلَا مَاءَ قَالَ عَلَيْكَ بِالصَّعِيدِ فَإِنَّهُ يَكْفِيك»»
Narrated by 'Imran ibn Husayn al-Khuzai'i  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim (Muttafaq Alayh)
Translation
While we were on a journey with the Prophet he led the people in the prayer, and when he turned away after the prayer he saw a man apart who had not prayed along with the people. He asked him what had prevented him from praying along with the company, and when the man replied that he was affected by seminal defilement and had no water, he said, "Make use of earth, for it is enough for you."
Class Notes
  • What counts as ṣaʿīd? (the material of tayammum):
  • al-Shāfiʿī: ṣaʿīd = turāb specifically (dust/soil only).
  • Ḥanafī & Mālik: ṣaʿīd = wajh al-arḍ — the face of the earth, i.e. anything of the earth's surface (sand, stone, etc.).
  • Imām Aḥmad: turāb munbit — soil that grows vegetation.
  • Imām Abū Yūsuf: the above plus raml (رمل) — sand.
  • Evidence: the jamhūr / Abū Yūsuf / Aḥmad rest on H526; the Ḥanafīs point to the variant wording with turāb.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 527); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ التَّيَمُّمِ
حديث ٥٢٨
«وَعَن عمار قَالَ: جَاءَ رَجُلٌ إِلَى عُمَرَ بْنِ الْخَطَّابِ فَقَالَ: إِنِّي أَجْنَبْتُ فَلَمْ أُصِبِ الْمَاءَ فَقَالَ عمار بن يَاسر لعمر بن الْخطاب أَمَا تَذْكُرُ أَنَّا كُنَّا فِي سَفَرٍ أَنَا وَأَنْتَ فَأَمّا أَنْتَ فَلَمْ تُصَلِّ وَأَمَّا أَنَا فتمعكت فَصليت فَذكرت للنَّبِي صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَقَالَ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسلم إِنَّمَا كَانَ يَكْفِيكَ هَكَذَا فَضَرَبَ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى الله عَلَيْهِ وَسلم بكفيه الأَرْض وَنفخ فيهمَا ثمَّ مسح بهما وَجْهَهُ وَكَفَّيْهِ. رَوَاهُ الْبُخَارِيُّ وَلِمُسْلِمٍ نَحْوُهُ وَفِيهِ قَالَ: إِنَّمَا يَكْفِيكَ أَنْ تَضْرِبَ بِيَدَيْكَ الْأَرْضَ ثمَّ تنفخ ثمَّ تمسح بهما وَجهك وكفيك»
Narrated by 'Ammar (عمار بن ياسر)  ·  Riwayah: al-Bukhari (Muslim has a similar narration)  ·  Grade: Sahih
Translation
A man came to 'Umar ibn al-Khattab and said, "I have had a sexual emission and cannot find water." 'Ammar ibn Yasir said to 'Umar ibn al-Khattab, "Do you not remember that you and I were on a journey and you did not pray, but I rolled myself on the ground, then prayed? I mentioned that afterwards to the Prophet, and he said, 'It would have been enough for you to do thus' — and the Prophet struck the ground with the palms of his hands, blew into them, then wiped his face and the palms of his hands with them." Bukhari transmitted it, and Muslim has a similar version which states: "It is enough for you to strike the ground with your hands, then blow, then wipe your face and the palms of your hands with them."
Class Notes
  • تَمَعَّكْتُ = ʿAmmār rolled in the dust (as an animal rolls) when he thought the whole body had to be covered for the major impurity — the Prophet corrected him: it is enough to strike the earth, blow off the excess, and wipe.
  • Bears on the one-vs-two strike debate (see H526). Al-Nawawī: one strike is closer to the Sunna; al-Khaṭṭābī: limiting it to the palms has the stronger riwāya, while extending to the forearms is the more analogically consistent (since tayammum is a badal, substitute, for wuḍūʾ).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 528); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ التَّيَمُّمِ
حديث ٥٢٩
«وَعَنْ أَبِي الْجُهَيْمِ بْنِ الْحَارِثِ بْنِ الصِّمَّةِ قَالَ: مَرَرْتُ عَلَى النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ وَهُوَ يَبُولُ فَسَلَّمْتُ عَلَيْهِ فَلَمْ يَرُدَّ عَلَيَّ حَتَّى قَامَ إِلَى جِدَارٍ فَحَتَّهُ بِعَصًى كَانَتْ مَعَهُ ثُمَّ وَضَعَ يَدَيْهِ عَلَى الْجِدَارِ فَمَسَحَ وَجْهَهُ وَذِرَاعَيْهِ ثُمَّ رَدَّ عَلَيَّ»
Narrated by Abul Juhaim b. al-Harith b. as-Simma  ·  Riwayah: al-Baghawi (Sharh al-Sunna, graded sound)  ·  Grade: Da'if (al-Albani)
Class Notes
  • This is, in effect, a rebuke of a muftī: an ignorant fatwa caused the wounded man's death (he was told he must do ghusl despite his head-wound, bathed, and died).
  • "شِفَاءُ الْعِيِّ السُّؤَالُ" — "the cure for ignorance is to ask." They should have asked rather than given a reckless ruling.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 529); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ التَّيَمُّمِ
حديث ٥٣٣
«وَعَن أبي سعيد الْخُدْرِيّ قَالَ: خَرَجَ رَجُلَانِ فِي سَفَرٍ فَحَضَرَتِ الصَّلَاةُ وَلَيْسَ مَعَهُمَا مَاءٌ فَتَيَمَّمَا صَعِيدًا طَيِّبًا فَصَلَّيَا ثُمَّ وَجَدَا الْمَاءَ فِي الْوَقْتِ فَأَعَادَ أَحَدُهُمَا الصَّلَاةَ وَالْوُضُوءَ وَلَمْ يَعُدِ الْآخَرُ ثُمَّ أَتَيَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَذَكَرَا ذَلِكَ لَهُ فَقَالَ لِلَّذِي لَمْ يُعِدْ: «أَصَبْتَ السُّنَّةَ وَأَجْزَأَتْكَ صَلَاتُكَ» وَقَالَ لِلَّذِي تَوَضَّأَ وَأَعَادَ: «لَكَ الْأَجْرُ مَرَّتَيْنِ»»
Narrated by Abu Sa'id al-Khudri  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud, al-Darimi (Nasa'i similar)  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
Two men went out on a journey, and when prayer time arrived with no water available, they performed tayammum with good earth and prayed. They subsequently found water within the prayer period, and one of them repeated the prayer with ablution while the other did not. They then came to God's messenger and mentioned that to him. He said to the one who had not repeated: "You observed the Sunnah and your prayer sufficed you," and he said to the one who had performed ablution and repeated: "You will have twofold reward."
Class Notes
  • The safest course is to act on caution (al-iḥtiyāṭ) when water is doubtful or scarce.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 533); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ التَّيَمُّمِ
حديث ٥٣٦
«وَعَن عمار بن يَاسر: أَنَّهُ كَانَ يُحَدِّثُ أَنَّهُمْ تَمَسَّحُوا وَهُمْ مَعَ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ بِالصَّعِيدِ لِصَلَاةِ الْفَجْرِ فَضَرَبُوا بِأَكُفِّهِمُ الصَّعِيدَ ثُمَّ مَسَحُوا وُجُوههم مَسْحَةً وَاحِدَةً ثُمَّ عَادُوا فَضَرَبُوا بِأَكُفِّهِمُ الصَّعِيدَ مَرَّةً أُخْرَى فَمَسَحُوا بِأَيْدِيهِمْ كُلِّهَا إِلَى الْمَنَاكِبِ وَالْآبَاطِ مِنْ بُطُونِ أَيْدِيهِمْ»
Narrated by 'Ammar b. Yasir  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
'Ammar b. Yasir used to tell that when they were with God's messenger they wiped themselves with earth for the dawn prayer, striking the palms of their hands on the earth, then wiping their faces once. They struck the palms of their hands again on the earth and wiped their arms completely up to the shoulders, and up to the armpits on the inside of their arms.
Class Notes
  • This hadith is a proof for the jamhūr, but the jamhūr do not take the clause taking the wiping all the way up to the shoulders and the armpits.
  • It leads into the next discussion — what may be used for tayammum (see the material debate noted at H527: Aḥmad = turāb munbit; Abū Yūsuf = + raml; Abū Ḥanīfa = anything of the genus of earth / ṣaʿīd; the Ḥanafīs also cite the turāb variant).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 536); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٣٧
«إِذَا جَاءَ أَحَدُكُمُ الْجُمُعَةَ فَلْيَغْتَسِلْ»
Narrated by Ibn 'Umar (رضي الله عنهما)  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim (Muttafaq Alayh)
Translation
When any of you comes to Friday prayer, let him bathe.
Class Notes
  • A command implies obligation. The order to perform ghusl every Friday was obligatory in early Islam; it was later relaxed to mustaḥabb (preferable).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 537); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٣٨
«وَعَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ الْخُدْرِيِّ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «غُسْلُ يَوْمِ الْجُمُعَةِ وَاجِبٌ عَلَى كُلِّ مُحْتَلِمٍ»»
Narrated by Abu Sa'id al-Khudri  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon (Bukhari and Muslim) — متفق عليه
Translation
Bathing on Friday is obligatory on everyone who has reached puberty.
Class Notes
  • Again set in early Islam — continuing the context of H537 (the Friday ghusl as originally obligatory).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 538); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٣٩
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «حَقُّ عَلَى كُلِّ مُسْلِمٍ أَنْ يَغْتَسِلَ فِي كُلِّ سَبْعَةِ أَيَّامٍ يَوْمًا يَغْسِلُ فِيهِ رَأسه وَجَسَده»»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim (Muttafaq Alayh)
Class Notes
  • The wording here means it is binding / necessary / required upon every Muslim (wājib ʿalā kull muslim) — read in light of the abrogation discussion (see H540, H544).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 539); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٤٠
«مَنْ تَوَضَّأَ يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ فَبِهَا وَنِعْمَتْ وَمَنِ اغْتَسَلَ فَالْغُسْلُ أَفْضَلُ»
Narrated by Samura b. Jundub  ·  Riwayah: Ahmad, Abu Dawud, Tirmidhi, Nasa'i, al-Darimi  ·  Grade: Hasan (al-Albani)
Translation
If anyone performs ablution on Friday, well and good; but if anyone bathes, bathing is more excellent.
Class Notes
  • Proof that the obligation was abrogated: "If he performs wuḍūʾ on Friday that is fine, but ghusl is better." — i.e. ghusl moved from obligation to superiority/preference.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 540); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٤١
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «مَنْ غَسَّلَ مَيِّتًا فَلْيَغْتَسِلْ» . رَوَاهُ ابْنُ مَاجَهْ وَزَادَ أَحْمَدُ وَالتِّرْمِذِيُّ وَأَبُو دَاوُدَ: «وَمَنْ حمله فَليَتَوَضَّأ»»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Ibn Majah (addition: Ahmad, Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud)  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
Abu Huraira reported God's messenger as saying, "Anyone who has washed a dead body must bathe himself." Ibn Majah transmitted it, and Ahmad, Tirmidhi and Abu Dawud added, "And he who carries it must perform ablution."
Class Notes
  • On the Sunnahs of the (Friday) washing — related via Ubayy.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 541); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٤٢
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهَا أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: كَانَ يَغْتَسِلُ مِنْ أَربع: من الْجَنَابَة وَمن يَوْم الْجُمُعَة وَمن الْحجام وَمن غسل الْمَيِّت»
Narrated by 'A'isha (عَائِشَةَ)  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud; graded ضَعِيفٌ (weak) by al-Albani
Translation
The Prophet bathed for four reasons: after seminal defilement, on Friday, after being cupped, and after washing the dead.
Class Notes
  • The Prophet never literally washed a dead body. This is figurative speech — kināya (كناية): like saying "the king built a palace" (he commanded it built). So "the Prophet washed" = he commanded it to be washed.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 542); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٤٣
«وَعَن قيس بن عَاصِم: أَنَّهُ أَسْلَمَ فَأَمَرَهُ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ أَنْ يَغْتَسِلَ بِمَاءٍ وَسِدْرٍ»
Narrated by Qais b. 'Asim  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud, Nasa'i  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
When he accepted Islam the Prophet commanded him to bathe with water and lote-tree leaves.
Class Notes
  • Ghusl for a new Muslim:
  • Imām Aḥmad & Imām Mālik: obligatory.
  • Imām al-Shāfiʿī: requires intention; so a wash done while still a kāfir would not count — but if he had had a wet dream / was in major impurity, ghusl is due.
  • Ḥanafī (us): we do not require intention; ghusl is obligatory only if in a state of major ritual impurity (جَنَابَة janāba), and only after reaching maturity. In practice this lands close to Aḥmad and Mālik (ghusl after maturity).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 543); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْغُسْلِ الْمَسْنُونِ
حديث ٥٤٤
«عَن عِكْرِمَة: إِنَّ نَاسًا مِنْ أَهْلِ الْعِرَاقِ جَاءُوا فَقَالُوا يَا ابْنَ عَبَّاسٍ أَتَرَى الْغُسْلَ يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ وَاجِبًا قَالَ لَا وَلَكِنَّهُ أَطْهَرُ وَخَيْرٌ لِمَنِ اغْتَسَلَ وَمَنْ لَمْ يَغْتَسِلْ فَلَيْسَ عَلَيْهِ بِوَاجِبٍ. وَسَأُخْبِرُكُمْ كَيْفَ بَدْءُ الْغُسْلِ: كَانَ النَّاسُ مَجْهُودِينَ يَلْبَسُونَ الصُّوفَ وَيَعْمَلُونَ عَلَى ظُهُورِهِمْ وَكَانَ مَسْجِدُهُمْ ضَيِّقًا مُقَارِبَ السَّقْفِ إِنَّمَا هُوَ عَرِيشٌ فَخَرَجَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فِي يَوْمٍ حَارٍّ وَعَرِقَ النَّاسُ فِي ذَلِكَ الصُّوفِ حَتَّى ثَارَتْ مِنْهُمْ رِيَاحٌ آذَى بِذَلِكَ بَعْضُهُمْ بَعْضًا. فَلَمَّا وَجَدَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ تِلْكَ الرّيح قَالَ: «أَيُّهَا النَّاسُ إِذَا كَانَ هَذَا الْيَوْمُ فَاغْتَسِلُوا وَلْيَمَسَّ أَحَدُكُمْ أَفْضَلَ مَا يَجِدُ مِنْ دُهْنِهِ وَطِيبِهِ» . قَالَ ابْنُ عَبَّاسٍ: ثُمَّ جَاءَ اللَّهُ بِالْخَيْرِ وَلَبِسُوا غَيْرَ الصُّوفِ وَكُفُوا الْعَمَلَ وَوُسِّعَ مَسْجِدُهُمُ وَذَهَبَ بَعْضُ الَّذِي كَانَ يُؤْذِي بَعْضُهُمْ بَعْضًا مِنَ الْعَرَقِ.»
Narrated by 'Ikrima (from Ibn 'Abbas)  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud  ·  Grade: Hasan (al-Albani)
Translation
'Ikrima said that some people from al-'Iraq came and asked Ibn 'Abbas whether he considered bathing on Friday obligatory. He replied that, while not obligatory, it had a more cleansing effect and was better for the one who observed it; but if anyone did not bathe there was no obligation for him to do so. He then said that he would tell them how the practice of bathing on Friday began. The people were in difficult circumstances, their clothing being of wool and their work being the carrying of loads on their backs. Their mosque was rather small and had a low roof which was only made of trellis work. God's messenger came out one hot day when the people were sweating in their woollen garments to such an extent that they annoyed one another by the odours which were spread around. When God's messenger noticed those odours he said, "When this day comes, you people must wash, and each of you should apply the best grease and perfume you can find." Ibn 'Abbas added that when God afterwards brought prosperity, they wore clothes not made of wool, they did not need to do manual labour, their mosque was extended, and some of the annoyance caused to one another by the sweat came to an end.
Class Notes
  • Shows it was originally obligatory, then became a Sunna — i.e. abrogation of the obligation.
  • A proof against the Ẓāhirīs (Ahl al-Ẓāhir), the literalists, who held the Friday ghusl to remain obligatory.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 544); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٤٥
«عَن أنس: إِنَّ الْيَهُودَ كَانُوا إِذَا حَاضَتِ الْمَرْأَةُ فِيهِمْ لَمْ يُؤَاكِلُوهَا وَلَمْ يُجَامِعُوهُنَّ فِي الْبُيُوتِ فَسَأَلَ أَصْحَاب النَّبِي صلى الله عَلَيْهِ وَسلم النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَأَنْزَلَ اللَّهُ تَعَالَى (ويسألونك عَن الْمَحِيض قُلْ هُوَ أَذًى فَاعْتَزِلُوا النِّسَاءَ فِي الْمَحِيضِ) الْآيَة. فَبَلَغَ ذَلِكَ الْيَهُودَ. فَقَالُوا: مَا يُرِيدُ هَذَا الرَّجُلُ أَنْ يَدَعَ مِنْ أَمْرِنَا شَيْئًا إِلَّا خَالَفَنَا فِيهِ فَجَاءَ أُسَيْدُ بْنُ حَضَيْرٍ وَعَبَّادُ بْنُ بِشْرٍ فَقَالَا يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِنَّ الْيَهُودَ تَقُولُ كَذَا وَكَذَا أَفَلَا نُجَامِعُهُنَّ؟ فَتَغَيَّرَ وَجْهُ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ حَتَّى ظَنَنَّا أَنْ قَدْ وَجَدَ عَلَيْهِمَا. فَخَرَجَا فَاسْتَقْبَلَتْهُمَا هَدِيَّةٌ مِنْ لَبَنٍ إِلَى النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَأَرْسَلَ فِي آثَارِهِمَا فَسَقَاهُمَا فعرفا أَن لم يجد عَلَيْهِمَا.»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Riwayah: Muslim; Sahih (al-Albani)
Class Notes
  • Definition of ḥayḍ:
  • The speaker uses the cause and intends the effect. Ḥayḍ is from سَالَ (sāla) — to flow.
  • In the Sharīʿa: blood that flows from the womb of a healthy woman — free of (the blood of) sickness or of childbirth.
  • When menstruation ends, that end is the cause for the obligation of ghusl.
  • Ruling: it prevents fasting and prayer; the fasts are made up later (not the prayers — see H556).
  • Qurʾānic source: "They ask you about menstruation…" (2:222). Some said it was written upon the daughters of Ādam. (al-Bukhārī)
  • Ibn al-Qayyim's rebuttal of those who claim menstruation began with the women of Banī Isrāʾīl — he holds it goes back earlier (the Mirqāt discussion, possibly to Ḥawwāʾ).
  • Intimate contact with a menstruating wife (without a barrier):
  • Imām Aḥmad & Imām Muḥammad (al-Shaybānī): permissible — proof = H545.
  • The other three imams: not permissible (direct contact of that area).
  • Ḥanafī reading: the licence covers everything except intercourse and what leads to it; the permitted zone is below the navel and above the knees is excluded — i.e. one enjoys what is above the knee and below the navel is the forbidden band (to double-check the exact wording: the prohibited area is between navel and knee; contact is via what is over the izār).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 545); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٤٧
«وَعَنْهَا قَالَتْ: كُنْتُ أَشْرَبُ وَأَنَا حَائِضٌ ثُمَّ أُنَاوِلُهُ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَيَضَعُ فَاهُ عَلَى مَوْضِعٍ فِيَّ فَيَشْرَبُ وَأَتَعَرَّقُ الْعَرْقَ وَأَنَا حَائِضٌ ثُمَّ أُنَاوِلُهُ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسلم فَيَضَع فَاه على مَوضِع فِيَّ»
Narrated by Aisha (رضي الله عنها)  ·  Riwayah: Muslim (رَوَاهُ مُسلم); Sahih per Al-Albani
Translation
She also said, "I would drink when I was menstruating, then hand it to the Prophet, and he would put his mouth where mine had been and drink; and I would eat flesh from a bone when I was menstruating, then hand it to the Prophet, and he would put his mouth where mine had been."
Class Notes
  • Word: the gloss here is "to bite the meat" — ʿĀʾisha would bite from a bone/meat and the Prophet would put his mouth on the same spot.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 547); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٥١
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «مَنْ أَتَى حَائِضًا أَوِ امْرَأَةً فِي دُبُرِهَا أَوْ كَاهِنًا فَقَدْ كَفَرَ بِمَا أُنْزِلَ عَلَى مُحَمَّدٍ»»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Ibn Majah, al-Darimi  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
If anyone has intercourse with a woman who is menstruating, or uses preposterous venery with a woman, or visits a kahin, he has disbelieved in what has been sent down to Muhammad.
Class Notes
  • It means they would commit kufr (disbelief) — i.e. the Jews' practice of shunning menstruating women entirely; or those who would deem lawful what is forbidden.
  • Needs commentary + a clean translation — flagged in class to revisit.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 551); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٥٢
«وَعَنْ مُعَاذِ بْنِ جَبَلٍ قَالَ: قُلْتُ: يَا رَسُول الله مَا تحل لِي مِنِ امْرَأَتِي وَهِيَ حَائِضٌ؟ قَالَ: «مَا فَوْقَ الْإِزَارِ وَالتَّعَفُّفُ عَنْ ذَلِكَ أَفْضَلُ»»
Narrated by Mu'adh b. Jabal  ·  Riwayah: Razin (Muhyi as-Sunna: chain not strong)  ·  Grade: Da'if (al-Albani)
Translation
What is above the waist-wrapper, but to abstain from that is better.
Class Notes
  • A proof for the jamhūr (do everything except intercourse).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 552); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٥٣
«إِذَا وَقَعَ الرَّجُلُ بِأَهْلِهِ وَهِيَ حَائِضٌ فَلْيَتَصَدَّقْ بِنِصْفِ دِينَارٍ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbas  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud, Nasaʾi, al-Darimi, Ibn Majah  ·  Grade: Sahih (al-Albani)
Translation
When a man has intercourse with his wife while she is menstruating, he must give half a dinar in alms.

Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٥٤
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عَبَّاسٍ عَنِ النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ قَالَ: «إِذَا كَانَ دَمًا أَحْمَرَ فَدِينَارٌ وَإِذَا كَانَ دَمًا أَصْفَرَ فَنِصْفُ دِينَارٍ» . رَوَاهُ التِّرْمِذِيّ»
Narrated by Ibn Abbas (Abdullah ibn Abbas)  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi  ·  Grade: Da'if (al-Albani)
Class Notes
  • Ruling if intercourse occurs during menstruation:
  • Imām Aḥmad: repent to God and give charity (a dīnār or half).
  • Imām Abū Ḥanīfa & Imām Mālik: repentance only; charity is preferable (to reinforce the repentance), not obligatory.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 554); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٥٥
«عَن زيد بن أسلم قَالَ: أَنَّ رَجُلًا سَأَلَ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَقَالَ: مَا يَحِلُّ لِي مِنَ امْرَأَتِي وَهِيَ حَائِضٌ؟ فَقَالَ لَهُ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «تَشُدُّ عَلَيْهَا إِزَارَهَا ثُمَّ شَأْنُكَ بِأَعْلَاهَا» . رَوَاهُ مَالِكٌ وَالدَّارِمِيُّ مُرْسلا»
Narrated by Zaid b. Aslam  ·  Riwayah: Malik, al-Darimi (mursal)  ·  Grade: Sahih
Class Notes
  • Word: the gloss is "bed / bedding." (My note: "I don't see it there — couldn't locate the word; to check.")
  • On the prohibition, Imām Aḥmad treats it as permissible, which is the safer/more cautious position.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 555); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْحَيْضِ
حديث ٥٥٦
«كُنْتُ إِذَا حِضْتُ نَزَلْتُ عَن الْمِثَال على الْحَصِير فَلم نقرب رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ وَلَمْ ندن مِنْهُ حَتَّى نطهر»

When I menstruated I left the bed and lay on the reed-mat and did not approach or come near God's messenger till I was purified.

Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمُسْتَحَاضَةِ
حديث ٥٦٢
«عَن أَسمَاء بنت عُمَيْس قَالَتْ: قُلْتُ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِنَّ فَاطِمَةَ بِنْتَ أَبِي حُبَيْشٍ اسْتُحِيضَتْ مُنْذُ كَذَا وَكَذَا فَلَمْ تُصَلِّ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «سُبْحَانَ اللَّهِ إِنَّ هَذَا مِنَ الشَّيْطَانِ لِتَجْلِسَ فِي مِرْكَنٍ فَإِذَا رَأَتْ صُفَارَةً فَوْقَ الْمَاءِ فَلْتَغْتَسِلْ لِلظُّهْرِ وَالْعَصْرِ غُسْلًا وَاحِدًا وَتَغْتَسِلْ لِلْمَغْرِبِ وَالْعِشَاءِ غُسْلًا وَاحِدًا وَتَغْتَسِلْ لِلْفَجْرِ غُسْلًا وَاحِدًا وَتَوَضَّأُ فِيمَا بَيْنَ ذَلِكَ»»
Narrated by Asma' daughter of 'Umais  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud  ·  Grade: Sahih, mawquf (al-Albani)
Translation
Asma' daughter of 'Umais said she told God's messenger that Fatima daughter of Abu Hubaish had had a flow of blood for a certain period and had not prayed. God's messenger said, "Glory be to God! This comes from the devil. She should sit in a tub, and when she sees yellowness appearing on the top of the water she should wash once for the noon and afternoon prayer, once for the sunset and night prayer, once for the dawn prayer, and in between times she should perform ablution." Abu Dawud transmitted it, saying Mujahid transmitted from Ibn 'Abbas that when the washing became too much for her he ordered her to combine the two prayers.
Class Notes
  • Word: mirkan = a wash-tub / large washing basin.
  • My note: the ruling here is only about what is preferable — (flagged: "I don't fully get this either" — to revisit.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 562); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الطَّهَارَةِ بَابُ الْمُسْتَحَاضَةِ
حديث ٥٦٣
«عَن أَسمَاء بنت عُمَيْس قَالَتْ: قُلْتُ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِنَّ فَاطِمَةَ بِنْتَ أَبِي حُبَيْشٍ اسْتُحِيضَتْ مُنْذُ كَذَا وَكَذَا فَلَمْ تُصَلِّ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ: «سُبْحَانَ اللَّهِ إِنَّ هَذَا مِنَ الشَّيْطَانِ لِتَجْلِسَ فِي مِرْكَنٍ فَإِذَا رَأَتْ صُفَارَةً فَوْقَ الْمَاءِ فَلْتَغْتَسِلْ لِلظُّهْرِ وَالْعَصْرِ غُسْلًا وَاحِدًا وَتَغْتَسِلْ لِلْمَغْرِبِ وَالْعِشَاءِ غُسْلًا وَاحِدًا وَتَغْتَسِلْ لِلْفَجْرِ غُسْلًا وَاحِدًا وَتَوَضَّأُ فِيمَا بَيْنَ ذَلِكَ»»
Narrated by Asma' daughter of 'Umais  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud — Dha'if
Translation
Asma' reported that Fatima bint Abu Hubaish suffered prolonged bleeding for so long that she did not pray. The Prophet said: 'Glory be to God! This is from Satan. Let her sit in a basin; when she sees yellowness above the water, let her perform one ritual washing for Zuhr and 'Asr together, one for Maghrib and 'Isha together, and one for Fajr, performing ablution in between.'
Class Notes
  • Continues 562; my notes here are incomplete.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 563); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٦٤
«الصَّلَوَاتُ الْخَمْسُ وَالْجُمُعَةُ إِلَى الْجُمُعَةِ وَرَمَضَانُ إِلَى رَمَضَانَ مُكَفِّرَاتٌ لِمَا بَيْنَهُنَّ إِذَا اجْتُنِبَتِ الْكَبَائِرُ»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Muslim — Sahih
Translation
The five daily prayers, and from one Friday to the next, and from one Ramadan to the next are expiations for the sins between them, so long as major sins are avoided.
Class Notes
  • Word: "إِذَا" (idhā) here is read in the sense of "إِلَّا" (illā) — "so long as / provided that the major sins are avoided" — that is the sound reading.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 564); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٦٧
«جَاءَ رَجُلٌ فَقَالَ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِنِّي أَصَبْتُ حَدًّا فَأَقِمْهُ عَلَيَّ. قَالَ: وَحَضَرَتِ الصَّلَاةُ فَصَلَّى مَعَ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ، فَلَمَّا قَضَى الصَّلَاةَ قَالَ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِنِّي أَصَبْتُ حَدًّا فَأَقِمْ فِيَّ كِتَابَ اللَّهِ. قَالَ: هَلْ حَضَرْتَ مَعَنَا الصَّلَاةَ؟ قَالَ: نَعَمْ. قَالَ: قَدْ غُفِرَ لَكَ»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
A man came and said: 'Messenger of God, I have committed a sin liable to punishment, so inflict it on me.' The time for prayer came and he prayed with the Prophet. When the prayer ended he repeated his request. The Prophet asked: 'Did you attend the prayer with us?' He said yes. He said: 'You have been forgiven.'
Class Notes
  • Three explanations of this hadith:
  • 1. The Prophet knew the ḥadd (prescribed penalty) was not actually established by what the man did.
  • 2. By "ḥadd" he meant taʿzīr (تعزير) — a discretionary punishment, not a fixed capital penalty; some chastisement, not the ḥadd proper.
  • 3. Some say it was a special concession of the Prophet — he could choose not to punish for it.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 567); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٦٨
«سَأَلْتُ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ أَيُّ الْأَعْمَالِ أَحَبُّ إِلَى اللَّهِ؟ قَالَ: الصَّلَاةُ لِوَقْتِهَا. قُلْتُ ثُمَّ أَيٌّ؟ قَالَ: بِرُّ الْوَالِدَيْنِ. قُلْتُ ثُمَّ أَيٌّ؟ قَالَ: الْجِهَادُ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ»
Narrated by Ibn Mas'ud  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
I asked the Prophet which deed is dearest to God. He said: 'Prayer at its proper time.' I asked what next; he said: 'Kindness to parents.' I asked what next; he said: 'Jihad in God's path.'
Class Notes
  • "Which deed is best?" — why the answers differ:
  • Across the hadiths the Prophet was repeatedly asked "what is the best action?" and gave different answers, tailored to:
    • the questioner, his state/circumstances, and his deficiencies/needs.
  • Here the answer is prayer at its time, then kindness to parents, then jihād.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 568); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٦٩
«بَيْنَ الرَّجُلِ وَبَيْنَ الشِّرْكِ وَالْكُفْرِ تَرْكُ الصَّلَاةِ»
Narrated by Jabir  ·  Riwayah: Muslim — Sahih
Translation
Between a man and polytheism and disbelief lies the abandonment of prayer.
Class Notes
  • Three opinions on "between a man and disbelief…":
  • 1. Severe warning and rebuke only.
  • 2. "Disbelief" = he does the deeds of the disbelievers (not literal kufr).
  • 3. He deems abandoning the prayer lawful in the religion (a juristic stance, not actual apostasy).
  • Positions on the one who abandons prayer:
  • The Khārijites: he is a disbeliever by leaving it (literal kufr).
  • The Muʿtazilites: he is in a manzila bayna l-manzilatayn (a station between belief and disbelief).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 569); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٧٠
«خَمْسُ صَلَوَاتٍ افْتَرَضَهُنَّ اللَّهُ تَعَالَى مَنْ أَحْسَنَ وُضُوءَهُنَّ وَصَلَّاهُنَّ لِوَقْتِهِنَّ وَأَتَمَّ رُكُوعَهُنَّ وَخُشُوعَهُنَّ كَانَ لَهُ عَلَى اللَّهِ عَهْدٌ أَنْ يَغْفِرَ لَهُ وَمَنْ لَمْ يَفْعَلْ فَلَيْسَ لَهُ عَلَى اللَّهِ عَهْدٌ إِنْ شَاءَ غَفَرَ لَهُ وَإِنْ شَاءَ عَذَّبَهُ»
Narrated by 'Ubada b. as-Samit  ·  Riwayah: Ahmad and Abu Dawud — Sahih
Translation
God has prescribed five prayers; whoever performs their ablution well, prays them at their times, and completes their bowing and humility, has a covenant from God to forgive him. Whoever does not has no covenant — if God wills He forgives him, and if He wills He punishes him.
Class Notes
  • Sleeping arrangements: applies to both same and different genders — separate the beds.
  • It is makrūh taḥrīman (prohibitively disliked) for them to be under one blanket; other narrations give a clear prohibition.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 570); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٧٢
«مُرُوا أَوْلَادَكُمْ بِالصَّلَاةِ وَهُمْ أَبْنَاءُ سَبْعِ سِنِينَ وَاضْرِبُوهُمْ عَلَيْهَا وَهُمْ أَبْنَاءُ عَشْرِ سِنِينَ وَفَرِّقُوا بَيْنَهُمْ فِي الْمَضَاجِعِ»
Narrated by 'Amr b. Shu'aib (from father, from grandfather)  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud — Hasan
Translation
Command your children to pray when they are seven years old, and discipline them for it when they are ten, and separate them in their beds.
Class Notes
  • On separating children's beds — for both same and different genders (the closing clause "and separate them in their beds").
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 572); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٧٥
«جَاءَ رَجُلٌ إِلَى النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَقَالَ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِنِّي عَالَجْتُ امْرَأَةً فِي أَقْصَى الْمَدِينَةِ وَإِنِّي أَصَبْتُ مِنْهَا مَا دُونَ أَنْ أَمَسَّهَا فَأَنَا هَذَا فَاقْضِ فِيَّ مَا شِئْتَ. فَنَزَلَتْ: وَأَقِمِ الصَّلَاةَ طَرَفَيِ النَّهَارِ وَزُلَفًا مِنَ اللَّيْلِ إِنَّ الْحَسَنَاتِ يُذْهِبْنَ السَّيِّئَاتِ»
Narrated by 'Abdullah b. Mas'ud  ·  Riwayah: Muslim — Sahih
Translation
A man came to the Prophet and said: 'Messenger of God, I dallied with a woman in the far part of the city and got from her everything short of intercourse; here I am, judge me as you wish.' Then was revealed: 'And establish prayer at the two ends of the day and in the near hours of the night; good deeds drive away evil deeds.'
Class Notes
  • Word: "عَالَجْتُ" (ʿālajtu) = to dally / be intimate short of intercourse — here kissing/fondling, not intercourse.
  • Word: "زُلَفًا / الزُّلَف" (al-zulaf) = the near hours (the early parts of the night). (I had it as "parts / nearness" — to confirm.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 575); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٧٨
«مَنْ حَافَظَ عَلَيْهَا كَانَتْ لَهُ نُورًا وَبُرْهَانًا وَنَجَاةً يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ وَمَنْ لَمْ يُحَافِظْ عَلَيْهَا لَمْ تَكُنْ لَهُ نُورٌ وَلَا بُرْهَانٌ وَلَا نَجَاةٌ وَكَانَ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ مَعَ قَارُونَ وَفِرْعَوْنَ وَهَامَانَ وَأُبَيِّ بْنِ خَلَفٍ»
Narrated by 'Abdullah b. 'Amr b. al-'As  ·  Riwayah: Ahmad, Darimi, Bayhaqi — Sahih
Translation
Whoever maintains the prayer will have light, proof, and salvation on the Day of Resurrection; whoever does not maintain it will have no light, proof, or salvation, and will be on that Day with Qarun, Pharaoh, Haman, and Ubayy b. Khalaf.
Class Notes
  • Word: "بُرْهَان" (burhān) = a proof / clear evidence that he is a true believer.
  • Meaning: there is proof he is a believer — he is not a disbeliever — yet abandoning the prayer can lead to disbelief, and he will be punished as the disbelievers are.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 578); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَرْضِ الصَّلَاةِ وَفَضْلِهَا
حديث ٥٨٠
«أَوْصَانِي خَلِيلِي أَنْ لَا تُشْرِكَ بِاللَّهِ شَيْئًا وَإِنْ قُطِّعْتَ وَحُرِّقْتَ وَلَا تَتْرُكْ صَلَاةً مَكْتُوبَةً مُتَعَمِّدًا فَمَنْ تَرَكَهَا مُتَعَمِّدًا فَقَدْ بَرِئَتْ مِنْهُ الذِّمَّةُ وَلَا تَشْرَبِ الْخَمْرَ فَإِنَّهَا مِفْتَاحُ كُلِّ شَرٍّ»
Narrated by Abu Darda'  ·  Riwayah: Ibn Majah — Hasan
Translation
My intimate friend enjoined me: do not associate anything with God even if you are cut to pieces and burned; do not intentionally abandon a prescribed prayer, for whoever abandons it intentionally has the protection withdrawn from him; and do not drink wine, for it is the key to every evil.
Class Notes
  • A proof that steadfastness is better — even under compulsion, where some leeway is technically permitted, holding firm is superior. (My note trails off here.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 580); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ مَوَاقِيتِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٥٨٥
«إِنَّ أَهَمَّ أُمُورِكُمْ عِنْدِي الصَّلَاةُ فَمَنْ حَفِظَهَا وَحَافَظَ عَلَيْهَا حَفِظَ دِينَهُ وَمَنْ ضَيَّعَهَا فَهُوَ لِمَا سِوَاهَا أَضْيَعُ … (وفيه ذكر أوقات الظهر والعصر)»
Narrated by 'Umar b. al-Khattab  ·  Riwayah: Malik — Dha'if
Translation
The most important of your affairs in my view is prayer; whoever guards it and maintains it guards his religion, and whoever neglects it will be more neglectful of all else. (The letter then specifies the times of Zuhr and 'Asr.)
Class Notes
  • ʿUmar's letter; one of the texts cited by al-Shāfiʿī on the Ẓuhr/ʿAsr boundary (see H581). (Graded weak.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 585); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٥٩٠
«إِذَا اشْتَدَّ الْحَرُّ فَأَبْرِدُوا بِالصَّلَاةِ فَإِنَّ شِدَّةَ الْحَرِّ مِنْ فَيْحِ جَهَنَّمَ»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
When the heat is intense, delay the prayer until it cools, for the intensity of heat is from the exhalation of Hell.
Class Notes
  • "إِذَا اشْتَدَّ الْحَرُّ فَأَبْرِدُوا بِالصَّلَاةِ" — the Ḥanafī anchor for delaying Ẓuhr in the heat (see H587). "When it cools" ≈ when the shadow is twice the object's length.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 590); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٥٩١
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «إِذَا اشْتَدَّ الْحَرُّ فَأَبْرِدُوا بِالصَّلَاةِ» — وَفِي رِوَايَةٍ لِلْبُخَارِيِّ عَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ: «فَإِنَّ شِدَّةَ الْحَرِّ مِنْ فَيْحِ جَهَنَّمَ»»
Narrated by Abū Hurayrah (R.A.)  ·  Riwayah: al-Bukhārī and Muslim (Muttafaq ʿAlayh)
Daleel For
  • Cited (numbering uncertain in the teacher's own notes, flagged at hadith 606) among the Ḥanafī proofs for the principle that a specific text (khuṣūṣiyya) overrides a general one — feeding the argument that delaying ʿAṣr in extreme heat can be preferable.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 591). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 606's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٥٩٦
«كُنَّا نُصَلِّي الْمَغْرِبَ مَعَ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَيَنْصَرِفُ أَحَدُنَا وَإِنَّهُ لَيُبْصِرُ مَوَاقِعَ نَبْلِهِ»
Narrated by Rafi' b. Khadij  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
We used to pray Maghrib with God's Messenger, and one of us would depart still able to see where his arrows fell.
Class Notes
  • Cited by Abū Ḥanīfa on ʿAsr timing (delaying before the yellowing). (Flagged in class to explain why.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 596); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦٠٥
«يَا عَلِيُّ ثَلَاثٌ لَا تُؤَخِّرْهَا: الصَّلَاةُ إِذَا أَتَتْ وَالْجِنَازَةُ إِذَا حَضَرَتْ وَالْأَيِّمُ إِذَا وَجَدْتَ لَهَا كُفُؤًا»
Narrated by 'Ali  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi — Hasan
Translation
O 'Ali, three things you must not postpone: the prayer when its time comes, the funeral when it is ready, and the unmarried woman when you find a suitable match for her.
Class Notes
  • Word: "الأَيِّم" (al-ayyim) = an unmarried woman.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 605); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦٠٦
«الْوَقْتُ الْأَوَّلُ مِنَ الصَّلَاةِ رِضْوَانُ اللَّهِ وَالْوَقْتُ الْآخِرُ عَفْوُ اللَّهِ»
Narrated by Ibn 'Umar  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi — Da'if (some grade it fabricated)
Translation
The earliest time of prayer earns the good pleasure of God, and the latest time earns His pardon.
Class Notes
  • Hastening vs delaying — the broader argument:
  • Agreed: hasten Maghrib; delay ʿIshāʾ to about the third of the night.
  • Opponents' evidence: reports praising the first times — e.g. this hadith ("the first time earns God's pleasure"), and the answer "prayer at its time" to "which deed is best?"
  • Ḥanafī principle: where there are specific texts (khuṣūṣiyyāt) you don't fall back on the general (ʿumūmiyyāt) — Ḥanafī proofs cited as H590–591 (numbers uncertain in my notes).
  • (Note: H606 is graded very weak / fabricated by some — the point survives on the other reports.)
  • ʿAsr — preferred time:
  • al-Shāfiʿī, Mālik, Aḥmad: hastening is better.
  • Abū Ḥanīfa: delaying (before the yellowing) is better — proof H596, and H610. (I lost track of the exact hadith numbers — to pull from the commentary.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 606); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦٠٩
«لَا تَزَالُ أُمَّتِي بِخَيْرٍ أَوْ قَالَ عَلَى الْفِطْرَةِ مَا لَمْ يُؤَخِّرُوا الْمَغْرِبَ إِلَى أَنْ تَشْتَبِكَ النُّجُومُ»
Narrated by Abu Ayyub  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud — Hasan
Translation
My people will continue in good — or he said, upon the natural way — so long as they do not delay the Maghrib prayer until the stars are abundant.
Class Notes
  • Gap in my notes (missed this class): H609 is said to carry two parts — the opponents' evidence first, then the Ḥanafī evidence. To pull the Mirqāt explanation of what is meant.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 609); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ تَعْجِيلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦١٤
«أَسْفِرُوا بِالْفَجْرِ فَإِنَّهُ أَعْظَمُ لِلْأَجْرِ»
Narrated by Rafi' b. Khadij  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud, Darimi — Hasan
Translation
Observe the dawn prayer at daybreak, for it is more productive of reward.
Class Notes
  • Notes missing for 614–616 — to ask classmates.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 614); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَضَائِلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦٢٥
«مَنْ صَلَّى الْبَرْدَيْنِ دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ»
Narrated by Abu Musa  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
Whoever prays the two cool prayers (Fajr and 'Asr) will enter Paradise.
Class Notes
  • This can also refer to the prayer itself. (Context to clarify.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 625); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَضَائِلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦٢٨
«لَوْ يَعْلَمُ النَّاسُ مَا فِي النِّدَاءِ وَالصَّفِّ الْأَوَّلِ ثُمَّ لَمْ يَجِدُوا إِلَّا أَنْ يَسْتَهِمُوا عَلَيْهِ لَاسْتَهَمُوا وَلَوْ يَعْلَمُونَ مَا فِي التَّهْجِيرِ لَاسْتَبَقُوا إِلَيْهِ»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
If people knew what blessing lies in the call to prayer and the first row, and could find no other way than to draw lots for it, they would draw lots; and if they knew what merit lies in going early to prayer, they would race to it.
Class Notes
  • "Going early to the prayer" & the Middle Prayer:
  • This hadith is about going early to the prayer (al-tahjīr) — and the worth of the call and the first row.
  • al-Ṣalāt al-Wusṭā (الصلاة الوسطى) — the "Middle Prayer":
    • Imām Aḥmad: it is ʿAsr.
    • Imām Mālik & Imām al-Shāfiʿī: it is Fajr.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 628); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ فَضَائِلِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٦٢٩
«لَيْسَ صَلَاةٌ أَثْقَلَ عَلَى الْمُنَافِقِينَ مِنَ الْفَجْرِ وَالْعِشَاءِ وَلَوْ يَعْلَمُونَ مَا فِيهِمَا لَأَتَوْهُمَا وَلَوْ حَبْوًا»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim — Muttafaq
Translation
No prayer is more burdensome to the hypocrites than the dawn and night prayers; if they knew what was in them, they would come to them even crawling.
Class Notes
