Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 629); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١٠
«إِذَا قَامَ أَحَدُكُمْ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ فَلَا يَبْصُقْ أَمَامَهُ فَإِنَّمَا يُنَاجِي اللَّهَ مَا دَامَ فِي مُصَلَّاهُ وَلَا عَنْ يَمِينِهِ فَإِنَّ عَنْ يَمِينِهِ مَلَكًا وَلْيَبْصُقْ عَنْ يَسَارِهِ أَوْ تَحْتَ قَدَمِهِ فَيَدْفِنُهَا»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Sahih al-Bukhari and Sahih Muslim
Translation
When any of you stands for prayer, he must not spit in front of him, for he is conversing with God as long as he is in his place of prayer; nor towards his right, for there is an angel on his right. He may spit to his left or under his foot and bury it.
Class Notes
  • Also #711: Abū Saʿīd's variant adds «تَحْتَ قَدَمِهِ الْيُسْرَى» — "or under his left foot" (Bukhārī & Muslim).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 710); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١١
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «إِذَا قَامَ أَحَدُكُمْ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ فَلَا يَبْصُقْ أَمَامَهُ فَإِنَّمَا يُنَاجِي اللَّهَ مَا دَامَ فِي مُصَلَّاهُ، وَلَا عَنْ يَمِينِهِ فَإِنَّ عَنْ يَمِينِهِ مَلَكًا، وَلْيَبْصُقْ عَنْ يَسَارِهِ أَوْ تَحْتَ قَدَمِهِ فَيَدْفِنُهَا» — وَفِي رِوَايَةِ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ: «تَحْتَ قَدَمِهِ الْيُسْرَى»»
Narrated by Abū Hurayrah (R.A.)  ·  Riwayah: al-Bukhārī and Muslim (Muttafaq ʿAlayh)
Daleel For
  • Not a madhhab dispute — Abū Saʿīd's variant (Bukhārī & Muslim) adding «تحت قدمه اليسرى», cited as an added detail to hadith 710's ruling on where to spit during prayer.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 711). Reference-only entry — cited as a dalīl in hadith 710's class notes; no separate class notes exist for this hadith itself.
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١٢
لَعَنَ اللَّهُ الْيَهُودَ وَالنَّصَارَى اتَّخَذُوا قُبُورَ أَنْبِيَائِهِمْ مَسَاجِدَ
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • Two opinions on the meaning: (1) they took it [intending] to worship the grave itself; (2) they worshiped there, facing the grave out of honoring it, while still intending Allah.
  • Praying in a graveyard is forbidden as long as the graves are visible; if you are far enough that they are not visible it is fine — unless there is an area dedicated for prayer (makān muʿadd li-l-ṣalāh).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 712); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١٤
اجْعَلُوا فِي بُيُوتِكُمْ مِنْ صَلَاتِكُمْ وَلَا تَتَّخِذُوهَا قُبُورًا
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • Grammar: "fī buyūtikum" = the second predicate (khabar); "min ṣalātikum" = the first predicate.
  • Refers to optional/voluntary prayer (nafl).
  • The comparison reads two ways: (1) the house itself empty of prayer is likened to the graveyard, or (2) the person himself — the one heedless of his prayer (ghāfil).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 714); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١٦
خَرَجْنَا وَفْدًا إِلَى رَسُولِ اللَّهِ ﷺ … «اخْرُجُوا فَإِذَا أَتَيْتُمْ أَرْضَكُمْ فَاكْسِرُوا بِيعَتَكُمْ وَانْضَحُوا مَكَانَهَا بِهَذَا الْمَاءِ وَاتَّخِذُوهَا مَسْجِدًا» …
Narrated by Talq b. ʿAli
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Narrated from a Companion (Talq b. ʿAli) who went out as a delegation to the Messenger ﷺ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 716); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١٨
«مَا أُمِرْتُ بِتَشْيِيدِ الْمَسَاجِدِ» . قَالَ ابْنُ عَبَّاسٍ: لَتُزَخْرِفُنَّهَا كَمَا زَخْرَفَتِ الْيَهُودُ وَالنَّصَارَى
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • tashyīd = either making it big/tall or adorning it.
  • The prohibition concerns pride (tafākhur) or being preoccupied with the matter (shughl al-ṭalab).
  • It does not forbid the permissibility of beautifying — it only negates that it is obligatory (nahy ʿan al-wujūb, not al-ibāḥa).
  • ʿUthmān decorated the masjid (added baked bricks); when Companions complained he cited the Prophet's ﷺ saying — whoever builds a masjid for Allah, Allah builds for him a house in Paradise.
  • Ibn ʿAbbās's statement applies when the purpose shifts from worship to mere beautification.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 718); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧١٩
مِنْ أَشْرَاطِ السَّاعَةِ أَنْ يَتَبَاهَى النَّاسُ فِي الْمَسَاجِدِ
Narrated by Anas
Class Notes
  • Your notes: This clarifies Ibn ʿAbbās's statement in 718: boasting over mosques is a sign of the Last Hour.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 719); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٢١ (٧٢٢)
بَشِّرِ الْمَشَّائِينَ فِي الظُّلَمِ إِلَى الْمَسَاجِدِ بِالنُّورِ التَّامِّ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ
Narrated by Buraida (Ibn Majah also from Sahl b. Saʿd & Anas)
Class Notes
  • Your notes: al-ẓulam = the ʿIshāʾ and Maghrib prayers; some add Fajr. (Just those prayers.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 721); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٢٣
إِذَا رَأَيْتُمُ الرَّجُلَ يَتَعَاهَدُ الْمَسْجِدَ فَاشْهَدُوا لَهُ بِالْإِيمَانِ … (إِنَّمَا يَعْمُرُ مَسَاجِدَ اللَّهِ مَنْ آمَنَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ …)
Narrated by Abu Saʿid al-Khudri
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Shows the virtue of those who habituate / frequent the masjid.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 723); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٢٥
رَأَيْتُ رَبِّيَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ فِي أَحْسَنِ صُورَةٍ … فَوَضَعَ كَفَّهُ بَيْنَ كَتِفَيَّ فَوَجَدْتُ بَرْدَهَا … فَعَلِمْتُ مَا فِي السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالْأَرْضِ …
Narrated by ʿAbd al-Raḥmān b. ʿĀʾish
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • "He saw" = the seeing of sleep / a dream (ruʾyā) — taken as majāz (metaphor). If taken as reality (ḥaqīqa), the meaning is consigned to Allah (tafwīḍ).
  • Placing the hand on the shoulder = the manner of addressing one you love and who is junior; a metaphor.
  • Some use it as proof of the Prophet's ﷺ knowledge of the unseen (ʿilm al-ghayb) — not correct: at most it shows he knew what is between the heavens and earth at that moment, not all until the Last Day; and there are cases where he ﷺ did not know something would happen.
  • What were they disputing? — competing to record the [good] deeds; or discussing the virtue of humans; or wanting what humans have.
  • A metaphor / allusion (ishāra) to the increase of mercy and virtue.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 725); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٢٦
هَلْ تَدْرِي فِيمَ يَخْتَصِمُ الْمَلَأُ الْأَعْلَى؟ … فِي الْكَفَّارَاتِ … الْمُكْثُ فِي الْمَسَاجِدِ بَعْدَ الصَّلَوَاتِ وَالْمَشْيُ عَلَى الْأَقْدَامِ إِلَى الْجَمَاعَاتِ وَإِبْلَاغُ الْوُضُوءِ فِي الْمَكَارِهِ … وَالدَّرَجَاتُ: إِفْشَاءُ السَّلَامِ وَإِطْعَامُ الطَّعَامِ وَالصَّلَاةُ بِاللَّيْلِ وَالنَّاسُ نِيَامٌ
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās & Muʿādh b. Jabal
Class Notes
  • Your notes: This is the "other thing they were disputing about" you mentioned at 726 — the kaffārāt (staying in the mosque after prayer, walking to congregation, thorough wuḍūʾ in adversity) — plus the darajāt. (Fully spelled out in 748 below.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 726); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٣٢
نَهَى رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ عَنْ تَنَاشُدِ الْأَشْعَارِ فِي الْمَسْجِدِ وَعَنِ الْبَيْعِ وَالِاشْتِرَاءِ فِيهِ وَأَنْ يَتَحَلَّقَ النَّاسُ يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ قَبْلَ الصَّلَاةِ فِي الْمَسْجِدِ
Narrated by ʿAmr b. Shuʿayb, from his father, from his grandfather
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • What is forbidden is ugly/repulsive poetry and poetry of ignorance (jāhiliyya). Islamic / praiseworthy poetry — e.g. calling to Allah — is allowed.
  • Proof: the Prophet ﷺ would summon the poet, set up a place [a minbar] for him, and ask him to recite. (Poet: Ḥassān b. Thābit — commentary, to verify.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 732); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٤٢
مَنْ جَاءَ مَسْجِدِي هَذَا لَمْ يَأْتِهِ إِلَّا لِخَيْرٍ يَتَعَلَّمُهُ أَوْ يُعَلِّمُهُ فَهُوَ بِمَنْزِلَةِ الْمُجَاهِدِ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ …
Narrated by Abu Huraira
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Refers to any masjid being for learning or teaching (taʿlīm / taʿallum) — taken unrestrictedly (muṭlaq).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 742); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْمَسَاجِدِ وَمَوَاضِعِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٤٨
احْتَبَسَ عَنَّا رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ … «فِيمَ يَخْتَصِمُ الْمَلَأُ الْأَعْلَى» … «الْكَفَّارَاتُ: مَشْيُ الْأَقْدَامِ إِلَى الْجَمَاعَاتِ وَالْجُلُوسُ فِي الْمَسَاجِدِ بَعْدَ الصَّلَوَاتِ وَإِسْبَاغُ الْوُضُوءِ حِينَ الْكَرِيهَاتِ … الدَّرَجَاتُ: إِطْعَامُ الطَّعَامِ وَلِينُ الْكَلَامِ وَالصَّلَاةُ وَالنَّاسُ نِيَامٌ» …
Narrated by Muʿādh b. Jabal
Class Notes
  • Your notes: This is the full version of the "highest assembly" matter referenced at 725–726 — the kaffārāt and the darajāt spelled out.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 748); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٥٤
رَأَيْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ يُصَلِّي فِي ثَوْبٍ وَاحِدٍ مُشْتَمِلًا بِهِ فِي بَيْتِ أُمِّ سَلَمَةَ وَاضِعًا طَرَفَيْهِ عَلَى عَاتِقَيْهِ
Narrated by ʿUmar b. Abī Salama
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • The thobe must cover the ʿawra — a condition for the validity of the prayer.
  • The sunnah is three garments: izār (lower), ridāʾ (upper), and the head covering; anything extravagant was prohibited by the Messenger ﷺ.
  • The narrator ʿUmar b. Abī Salama was the Prophet's rabīb (ربيب) — stepson (son of Umm Salama).
  • The cloth must wrap around the shoulder (ʿātiq) to keep it from falling.
  • Imām Aḥmad: a single thobe not on the shoulder means prayer not accepted (also from 755). Jamhūr: valid; the prohibition is only minor dislike (tanzīh).
  • If a single wide cloth, tie something around your waist.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 754); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٥٥
لَا يُصَلِّيَنَّ أَحَدُكُمْ فِي الثَّوْبِ الْوَاحِدِ لَيْسَ عَلَى عَاتِقَيْهِ مِنْهُ شَيْءٌ
Narrated by Abu Huraira
Class Notes
  • Your notes: This is Imām Aḥmad's evidence (with 754) that the garment must be on the shoulder.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 755); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٦٠
إِنِّي رَجُلٌ أَصِيدُ أَفَأُصَلِّي فِي الْقَمِيصِ الْوَاحِدِ؟ قَالَ: «نَعَمْ وَازْرُرْهُ وَلَوْ بِشَوْكَةٍ»
Narrated by Salama b. al-Akwaʿ
Class Notes
  • Your notes: The main goal is to fully cover the ʿawra — that is why you tie/fasten it tight; otherwise the cloth itself is fine.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 760); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٦١
بَيْنَمَا رَجُلٌ يُصَلِّي مُسْبِلًا إِزَارَهُ … «اذْهَبْ فَتَوَضَّأْ» … «إِنَّ اللَّهَ تَعَالَى لَا يَقْبَلُ صَلَاةَ رَجُلٍ مُسْبِلٍ إِزَارَهُ»
Narrated by Abu Huraira
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • "Allah will not accept" = not accepted in preference / reward — the prayer is technically valid.
  • The repeated wuḍūʾ was to break the bad habit of dragging the garment, because outward purification leads to inward purification.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 761); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٦٣
أَتُصَلِّي الْمَرْأَةُ فِي دِرْعٍ وَخِمَارٍ لَيْسَ عَلَيْهَا إِزَارٌ؟ قَالَ: «إِذَا كَانَ الدِّرْعُ سَابِغًا يُغَطِّي ظُهُورَ قَدَمَيْهَا»
Narrated by Umm Salama
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Approved for those who hold the top of the feet is part of a woman's ʿawra (al-Shāfiʿī). To confirm on commentary fetch: (a) the "not due to hardship" point; (b) the other scholar who also holds the feet are ʿawra.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 763); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٦٤
نَهَى عَنِ السَّدْلِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ وَأَنْ يُغَطِّيَ الرَّجُلُ فَاهُ
Narrated by Abu Huraira
Class Notes
  • Your notes (this is the hadith for your sadl / covering-mouth points):
  • sadl / isbāl — letting clothes hang below the ankles, any cloth; two opinions: (1) prohibited with or without arrogance; (2) prohibited only with arrogance, otherwise minor dislike (tanzīh).
  • Sadl in prayer — so there is no preoccupation/distraction with the garment; implies continuous covering.
  • Covering the mouth (yughaṭṭī fāhu) — i.e. mouth/nose (turban riding up over the face); prohibited; means continuous covering; does not apply to yawning.
  • Yawning (tathāʾub) in prayer: cover the mouth — generally the left hand, but while standing in prayer, the right hand (less movement).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 764); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٦٥
خَالِفُوا الْيَهُودَ فَإِنَّهُمْ لَا يُصَلُّونَ فِي نِعَالِهِمْ وَلَا خِفَافِهِمْ
Narrated by Shaddād b. Aws
Class Notes
  • Your notes: This is the basis for the permissibility of praying in sandals, which the next hadith (766) qualifies (provided they are clean).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 765); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السَّتْرِ
حديث ٧٦٦
خَلَعَ نَعْلَيْهِ فَوَضَعَهُمَا عَنْ يَسَارِهِ … «إِنَّ جِبْرِيلَ أَتَانِي فَأَخْبَرَنِي أَنَّ فِيهِمَا قَذَرًا … فَإِنْ رَأَى فِي نَعْلَيْهِ قَذَرًا أَوْ أَذًى فَلْيَمْسَحْهُ وَلْيُصَلِّ فِيهِمَا»
Narrated by Abu Saʿid al-Khudri
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • What was on the sandal was not najāsa — just qadhar/adhā, ordinary dirt.
  • If dry with body/substance (jirm), wipe; if wet, wash.
  • Establishes the permissibility of praying in sandals even with dirt — but still sinful, because you leave a sunnah (you cannot bend the foot properly in them).
  • In the masjid take them off — you'd be intentionally dirtying the masjid, which is prohibited.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 766); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٧٢
كَانَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ يَغْدُو إِلَى الْمُصَلَّى وَالْعَنَزَةُ بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ تُحْمَلُ وَتُنْصَبُ بِالْمُصَلَّى بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ فَيُصَلِّي إِلَيْهَا
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar
Class Notes
  • Your notes:
  • Obligatory? The Ẓāhirīs say yes; we and the rest of the schools say it is not necessary.
  • Length ≈ a cubit (dhirāʿ); thickness ≈ the width of the index finger.
  • Passing in front of one praying is prohibited; beside (left/right) is fine.
    • Small masjid (≈ 40–60 dhirāʿ): cannot pass in front at all.
    • Large masjid: limit without a sutra = the place of prostration ≈ three rows including your own; past that you may pass.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 772); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٧٣
رَأَيْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ بِمَكَّةَ وَهُوَ بِالْأَبْطَحِ فِي قُبَّةٍ حَمْرَاءَ … وَرَأَيْتُ النَّاسَ يَبْتَدِرُونَ ذَاكَ الْوَضُوءَ … ثُمَّ رَأَيْتُ بِلَالًا أَخَذَ عَنَزَةً فَرَكَزَهَا … صَلَّى إِلَى الْعَنَزَةِ بِالنَّاسِ رَكْعَتَيْنِ …
Narrated by Abu Juḥayfa
Class Notes
  • Your notes: (You couldn't recall the word that "means quickly" — it is yabtadirūn, "they hastened/raced.")
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 773); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٧٤
كَانَ يُعَرِّضُ رَاحِلَتَهُ فَيُصَلِّي إِلَيْهَا … «كَانَ يَأْخُذُ الرَّحْلَ فَيُعَدِّلُهُ فَيُصَلِّي إِلَى آخِرَتِهِ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar (via Nāfiʿ)
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Shows a sutra can be many things (a riding-beast, a saddle — and, by extension, a wall): anything placed as a screen serves.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 774); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٧٨
تَقْطَعُ الصَّلَاةَ الْمَرْأَةُ وَالْحِمَارُ وَالْكَلْبُ وَيَقِي ذَلِكَ مِثْلُ مُؤَخِّرَةِ الرَّحْلِ
Narrated by Abu Huraira
Class Notes
  • Your notes (the three positions):
  • Ẓāhirīs: all three break it — woman, donkey, dog.
  • Aḥmad & Isḥāq: only the black dog (al-kalb al-aswad) (proof: 779, 784).
  • Jamhūr: none break it (proof: 779, 780, 784).
  • Reply to this ḥadīth: (1) al-Ṭaḥāwī — abrogated (mansūkh); (2) "cuts off" = the khushūʿ is broken, not the prayer itself.
  • Why these three? They were common in that place.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 778); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٧٩
كَانَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ يُصَلِّي مِنَ اللَّيْلِ وَأَنَا مُعْتَرِضَةٌ بَيْنَهُ وَبَيْنَ الْقِبْلَةِ كَاعْتِرَاضِ الْجَنَازَةِ
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha
Class Notes
  • Your notes: A proof (in the 778 discussion) that a woman's presence does not break the prayer.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 779); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٨٠
أَقْبَلْتُ رَاكِبًا عَلَى أَتَانٍ وَأَنَا … قَدْ نَاهَزْتُ الِاحْتِلَامَ وَرَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ يُصَلِّي بِالنَّاسِ بِمِنًى إِلَى غَيْرِ جِدَارٍ … فَلَمْ يُنْكِرْ ذَلِكَ عَلَيَّ أَحَدٌ
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Why no one rebuked him: (1) he was a child; (2) it was at Minā and crowded; (3) the imām had a sutra, and the imām's sutra serves all those following (maʾmūm).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 780); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٨١
إِذَا صَلَّى أَحَدُكُمْ فَلْيَجْعَلْ تِلْقَاءَ وَجْهِهِ شَيْئًا فَإِنْ لَمْ يَجِدْ فَلْيَنْصِبْ عَصَاهُ فَإِنْ لَمْ يَكُنْ مَعَهُ عَصًا فَلْيَخْطِطْ خَطًّا ثُمَّ لَا يَضُرُّهُ مَا مَرَّ أَمَامَهُ
Narrated by Abu Huraira
Class Notes
  • Your notes: This is the hadith of "drawing the line." You asked for the Lamaat footnote verbatim — below.
  • Lamaat al-Tanqih (Fasl 2, 781) — full footnote:
  • English (Lamaat footnote, rendered):
  • "Let him place in front of his face" — i.e. opposite and beside him — something: a tree, a stone, a wall, or the like.
  • "If he finds nothing" — this indicates an order (tartīb); apparently it is recommended (mandūb).
  • "Let him set up his staff" — and if the ground is hard so he cannot plant/fix it, he simply lays it down — but lengthwise, not crosswise, so it resembles the planted form.
  • "Let him draw a line" — al-Shāfiʿī held this in his old view (qadīm) but rejected it in his new view (jadīd) because of the iḍṭirāb (inconsistency) and weakness of the hadith — so in the Shaykh's commentary. In our (Ḥanafī) view the line counts for nothing — thus narrated from [Imām] Muḥammad [al-Shaybānī]. Some of our later scholars adopted it and said he draws a line — except that we say the line is not considered a barrier between him and the passer-by, so its presence and absence are equal; so said al-Sughnāqī.
  • Ibn al-Humām (Fatḥ al-Qadīr): they differed over the line just as they differed over laying down [the staff] when one has nothing to plant or lay. The one who forbids says the objective is not achieved, since it is not visible from afar. The one who permits says the report came with it (Abu Dawud's hadith). The author of al-Hidāya chose the first (placing an object), and the sunnah is more deserving of being followed — though the line "shows in general," since the aim is to gather one's attention by tying the imagination to it so it does not scatter. End.
  • They then differed on the shape of the line: some say it is made like a crescent (hilāl); some say it is extended long toward the qibla, and it may be extended right and left; the chosen view is the first (crescent).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 781); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٨٣
مَا رَأَيْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ يُصَلِّي إِلَى عُودٍ وَلَا عَمُودٍ وَلَا شَجَرَةٍ إِلَّا جَعَلَهُ عَلَى حَاجِبِهِ الْأَيْمَنِ أَوِ الْأَيْسَرِ وَلَا يَصْمُدُ لَهُ صَمْدًا
Narrated by al-Miqdād b. al-Aswad
Class Notes
  • Your notes: It is better (afḍal) to shift slightly to the left or right of the sutra rather than stand directly behind it.

Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٨٤
صَلَّى فِي صَحْرَاءَ لَيْسَ بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ سُتْرَةٌ وَحِمَارَةٌ لَنَا وَكَلْبَةٌ تَعْبَثَانِ بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ فَمَا بَالَى ذَلِكَ

Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٨٥
لَا يَقْطَعُ الصَّلَاةَ شَيْءٌ وَادْرَؤُوا مَا اسْتَطَعْتُمْ فَإِنَّمَا هُوَ شَيْطَانٌ

Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ السُّتْرَةِ
حديث ٧٨٩
إِذَا صَلَّى أَحَدُكُمْ إِلَى غَيْرِ السُّتْرَةِ فَإِنَّهُ يَقْطَعُ صَلَاتَهُ الْحِمَارُ وَالْخِنْزِيرُ وَالْيَهُودِيُّ وَالْمَجُوسِيُّ وَالْمَرْأَةُ وَتُجْزِئُ عَنْهُ إِذَا مَرُّوا بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ عَلَى قَذْفَةٍ بِحَجَرٍ
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās
Class Notes
  • Your notes: Distance to the sutra ≈ the length of a flick/throw of a stone; and it is prohibitively disliked (makrūh taḥrīman) to pray in a spot that blocks a passage/exit — pray in the right spot.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 789); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٩٠
«… ارْجِعْ فَصَلِّ، فَإِنَّكَ لَمْ تُصَلِّ … إِذَا قُمْتَ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ فَأَسْبِغِ الْوُضُوءَ، ثُمَّ اسْتَقْبِلِ الْقِبْلَةَ فَكَبِّرْ، ثُمَّ اقْرَأْ بِمَا تَيَسَّرَ مَعَكَ مِنَ الْقُرْآنِ، ثُمَّ ارْكَعْ حَتَّى تَطْمَئِنَّ رَاكِعًا …»
Narrated by Abu Huraira  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon (Bukhari & Muslim)
Translation
"Go back and pray, for you have not prayed… When you stand for prayer perform the ablution fully, then face the qibla and say the takbīr, then recite what is easy for you of the Qurʾān, then bow until you are at rest bowing…"
Class Notes
  • Subject: taʿdīl al-arkān — performing the pillars properly. Minimum ṭumaʾnīna in rukūʿ/sujūd = the time of one tasbīḥ.
  • Farḍ or wājib?
  • Farḍ camp — the three imams (Mālik, Shāfiʿī, Aḥmad) + Abu Yūsuf: if missed, the prayer is invalid.
  • Wājib camp — Abu Hanifa & Muḥammad b. al-Ḥasan: leaving it = sin only (not invalid).
  • Farḍ camp's proofs:
  • 1. "lam tuṣalli" — read as negating the foundation (aṣl) of the prayer, so it's invalid.
  • 2. Abu Masʿūd al-Badrī: «لَا تُجْزِئُ صَلَاةٌ لَا يُقِيمُ الرَّجُلُ فِيهَا صُلْبَهُ فِي الرُّكُوعِ وَالسُّجُودِ» — a prayer in which a man does not straighten his back in rukūʿ and sujūd does not suffice. (Abu Dāwūd/Tirmidhī wording.)
  • Our (Ḥanafī) reply — negation of completeness (kamāl), not validity:
  • "lam tuṣalli" = not the complete prayer; "lā tujziʾ" = does not suffice for completeness — really our proof.
  • Abu Qatāda (Aḥmad & Ṭabarānī): «أَسْوَأُ النَّاسِ سَرِقَةً الَّذِي يَسْرِقُ مِنْ صَلَاتِهِ، قَالُوا: وَكَيْفَ يَسْرِقُ مِنْ صَلَاتِهِ؟ قَالَ: لَا يُتِمُّ رُكُوعَهَا وَلَا سُجُودَهَا» — meaning "lā yutimm" (does not complete) = itmām = kamāl, so the foundation is there but not complete.
  • al-Tirmidhī's addition: «وَإِنِ انْتَقَصْتَ مِنْهُ شَيْئًا انْتَقَصْتَ مِنْ صَلَاتِكَ»: negation of completeness — the prayer exists but is deficient.
  • He was told to repeat only because it was makrūh — a missed wājib is repeated within its time.
  • Why corrected ~3 times / silent at first? He didn't stay silent — he corrected him each time, but only explained after the third, to show him the importance of the prayer.
  • Why Abu Hanifa says wājib, not farḍ:
  • Rukūʿ & sujūd are commanded unrestricted (muṭlaq): ﴿ارْكَعُوا وَاسْجُدُوا﴾.
  • Rukūʿ = mere bending (muṭlaq al-inḥināʾ); sujūd = placing part of the head on the ground.
  • A solitary (āḥād) report cannot restrict (taqyīd) the definitive (qaṭʿī), unrestricted command of the verse, so ṭumaʾnīna is wājib, not farḍ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 790); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٩١
«كَانَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ يَسْتَفْتِحُ الصَّلَاةَ بِالتَّكْبِيرِ وَالْقِرَاءَةَ بِـ﴿الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ﴾ … وَكَانَ يَنْهَى عَنْ عُقْبَةِ الشَّيْطَانِ، وَيَنْهَى أَنْ يَفْتَرِشَ الرَّجُلُ ذِرَاعَيْهِ افْتِرَاشَ السَّبُعِ، وَكَانَ يَخْتِمُ الصَّلَاةَ بِالتَّسْلِيمِ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Translation
"The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to open the prayer with the takbīr and the recitation with al-ḥamdu lillāhi rabbil-ʿālamīn… he used to forbid the devil's heel-rest, and forbid that a man spread his forearms like a beast of prey, and he would end the prayer with the taslīm."
Class Notes
  • This is the proof of those who say the basmala is NOT part of the Qurʾān [as a verse of al-Fātiḥa]. Two matters:
  • (1) Is the basmala part of the Qurʾān?:
  • All agree the basmala in Sūrat al-Naml is a verse — revelation.
  • Mālik: not part of the Qurʾān — only for separation (faṣl).
  • Shāfiʿī: part of al-Fātiḥa (and the other suras — the correct view in his school).
  • Abu Hanifa: not a specific verse (āya khāṣṣa) of any sūra — only for faṣl — but it is Qurʾān, recited revelation (waḥy matlū).
  • (2) Aloud (jahr) or silent (sirr)?:
  • Mālik: don't even read it. Shāfiʿī: aloud. Abu Hanifa & Aḥmad: silent.
  • Ḥanafī proofs (silent; and the basmala not a verse of al-Fātiḥa):
  • 1. Ḥadīth qudsī: «قَسَمْتُ الصَّلَاةَ بَيْنِي وَبَيْنَ عَبْدِي نِصْفَيْنِ … فَإِذَا قَالَ الْعَبْدُ: ﴿الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ﴾ …» — the division starts at al-ḥamdu lillāh, not the basmala — meaning the basmala isn't counted in al-Fātiḥa; he began with the sūra.
  • 2. The Anas and Ibn Mughaffal narrations are really Mālik's proofs, but we use them too — they only negate saying it aloud, not that it's Qurʾān:
    • Anas (Muslim): «صَلَّيْتُ خَلْفَ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ وَأَبِي بَكْرٍ وَعُمَرَ، فَكُلُّهُمْ يُخْفُونَ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِيمِ».
    • ʿAbdullāh b. Mughaffal (Tirmidhī): «إِيَّاكَ وَالْحَدَثَ، فَإِنِّي صَلَّيْتُ مَعَ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ وَأَبِي بَكْرٍ وَعُمَرَ وَعُثْمَانَ، فَلَمْ أَسْمَعْ أَحَدًا مِنْهُمْ يَقُولُهُ».
  • 3. Ibn ʿAbbās (Abu Dāwūd): the Prophet ﷺ did not know the separator (faṣl) between the suras until ﴿بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِيمِ﴾ was revealed to him.
  • 4. Abu Hurayra: Sūrat al-Mulk = 30 verses — only works if the basmala is not counted a verse of it.
  • 5. Same logic — al-Fātiḥa = 7 verses (works only without counting the basmala).
  • 6. Consensus that al-Kawthar = 3 verses — if the basmala were part of it, it'd be 4 (against ijmāʿ).
  • Shāfiʿī's proofs (aloud; and the basmala is a verse):
  • Tirmidhī [Ibn ʿAbbās]: the Prophet ﷺ used to begin the prayer with the basmala — you'd only know that if it was said aloud. Many other jahr narrations — see Muṣannaf Ibn Abī Shayba.
  • Some weak narrations that the basmala is part of the Qurʾān: Abu Hurayra (al-Fātiḥa is 7 verses, one being the basmala); Umm Salama (counted the basmala as part of al-Fātiḥa); and the report «أُنْزِلَتْ عَلَيَّ آنِفًا سُورَةٌ» then he read the basmala + Sūrat al-Kawthar — "unzila ʿalayya" shows it was part of it.
  • Word glosses (from the matn):
  • «لَمْ يُشْخِصْ رَأْسَهُ» — yushkhiṣ = to raise [the head].
  • «وَلَمْ يُصَوِّبْهُ» — yuṣawwib = to lower / hang [the head].
  • «عُقْبَةِ الشَّيْطَانِ» = the devil's heel-sitting = sitting on the heels with the buttocks against the heels, which is makrūh per the Ḥanafīs.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 791); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٩٢
«أَنَا أَحْفَظُكُمْ لِصَلَاةِ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ ﷺ، رَأَيْتُهُ إِذَا كَبَّرَ جَعَلَ يَدَيْهِ حِذَاءَ مَنْكِبَيْهِ، وَإِذَا رَكَعَ أَمْكَنَ يَدَيْهِ مِنْ رُكْبَتَيْهِ ثُمَّ هَصَرَ ظَهْرَهُ، فَإِذَا رَفَعَ رَأْسَهُ اسْتَوَى حَتَّى يَعُودَ كُلُّ فَقَارٍ مَكَانَهُ … وَإِذَا جَلَسَ فِي الرَّكْعَةِ الْآخِرَةِ قَدَّمَ رِجْلَهُ الْيُسْرَى وَنَصَبَ الْأُخْرَى وَقَعَدَ عَلَى مَقْعَدَتِهِ»
Narrated by Abu Ḥumayd al-Sāʿidī  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari
Class Notes
  • Gloss: «هَصَرَ ظَهْرَهُ» — haṣara = to bend / lower the back.
  • Raising the hands is a matter of preference — it doesn't invalidate the prayer either way.
    • Mālik & Abu Hanifa: don't raise [at rukūʿ / rising from it]. Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad: yes.
  • iḍṭirāb (discordance) in the wording for how high: level of the shoulders / middle of the ears / furūʿ (tops) of the ears. Best (fits all narrations): palms level with the shoulders, fingertips reaching the ears — preferred per Ḥanafīs & Shāfiʿīs. (Practical cue: just bring your fingertips up toward your ears.)
  • The Shāfiʿīs have many proofs, mostly from [young] Companions, raising before & after bowing.
    • Ḥanafī response (a): why not also act on the narrations of raising before & after prostration? (b) whatever your reason for not taking those, the same applies to not raising at bowing.
  • Daleel of the Aḥnāf:
    1. Ibn Masʿūd: "Shall I not show you the prayer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?" — he prayed and did not raise except at the beginning.
    2. Ibn Masʿūd: he prayed with the Prophet ﷺ, Abu Bakr & ʿUmar — they didn't raise except at the first takbīr.
    3. al-Barāʾ b. ʿĀzib (Tirmidhī): he saw it only at the first takbīra; in Abu Dāwūd's wording, he did not repeat it.
    4. Jābir b. Samura (Muslim): «مَا لِي أَرَاكُمْ رَافِعِي أَيْدِيكُمْ كَأَنَّهَا أَذْنَابُ خَيْلٍ شُمْسٍ، اسْكُنُوا فِي الصَّلَاةِ» — (shums = restive/unruly horses). (quoted in Mirqat.)
  • Responses to their proof:
    1. It was abrogated (raising was early, then abrogated).
    2. Most of their proof is Ibn ʿUmar's narrations — but his student Mujāhid says he prayed behind Ibn ʿUmar ten years and he never raised — so take his ʿamal (practice) over his narration.
    3. iḍṭirāb in the narrations: only at the start / [twice] / three times / at every movement.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 792); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٩٦
«أَنَّهُ رَأَى النَّبِيَّ ﷺ يُصَلِّي، فَإِذَا كَانَ فِي وِتْرٍ مِنْ صَلَاتِهِ لَمْ يَنْهَضْ حَتَّى يَسْتَوِيَ قَاعِدًا»
Narrated by Mālik b. al-Huwayrith  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari
Class Notes
  • Aḥmad & Shāfiʿī: it's a sunna. Abu Hanifa & Mālik: permissible if done quickly/normally, but pausing too long may break the prayer.
  • Ḥanafī proof — Abu Hurayra: «كَانَ يَنْهَضُ فِي الصَّلَاةِ عَلَى صُدُورِ قَدَمَيْهِ» — he rose on the fronts/balls of his feet (ṣudūr al-qadamayn). (Tirmidhī; in Mirqat.)
  • Response to their proof (that he sat before standing): (1) it was to show permissibility; (2) or for an excuse (old age, pain).
  • Also: the prayer is about being energetic, not lax.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 796); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٧٩٧
«رَفَعَ يَدَيْهِ حِينَ دَخَلَ فِي الصَّلَاةِ كَبَّرَ، ثُمَّ الْتَحَفَ بِثَوْبِهِ، ثُمَّ وَضَعَ يَدَهُ الْيُمْنَى عَلَى الْيُسْرَى»
Narrated by Wāʾil b. Ḥujr  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Class Notes
  • Three imams: crossing the hands (right on left) is sunna. Mālik: keeping them down (irsāl) is better — he has no authentic narration, only qiyās and the practice of the people of Medina.
  • Where to place them: Shāfiʿī (& an opinion from Aḥmad): above the navel. Abu Hanifa: below the navel. Aḥmad: your choice.
  • Shāfiʿī proof (Ṣaḥīḥ Ibn Khuzayma, from Wāʾil b. Ḥujr): he placed his right hand on the left «عَلَى صَدْرِهِ» (on the chest).
  • Ḥanafī proof: the same Companion (Wāʾil b. Ḥujr) in Muṣannaf Ibn Abī Shayba = below the navel; also Abu Dāwūd + a report from ʿAlī: «تَحْتَ السُّرَّةِ» (Nawawī: they agreed it is weak).
  • Back-and-forth: each side calls the other's proof weak.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 797); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٠٠
«أَفْضَلُ الصَّلَاةِ طُولُ الْقُنُوتِ»
Narrated by Jābir  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Translation
"The best prayer is that with the longest qunūt (standing)."
Class Notes
  • Shāfiʿī (& Aḥmad): sujūd (prostration) is better. Mālik & Abu Hanifa: qiyām (standing) is better.
  • Our proof: this hadith; there is more difficulty in standing, meaning more reward; and you recite the Qurʾān while standing (more merit too).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 800); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٠٥
«الصَّلَاةُ مَثْنَى مَثْنَى، تَشَهُّدٌ فِي رَكْعَتَيْنِ، وَتَخَشُّعٌ وَتَضَرُّعٌ وَتَمَسْكُنٌ، ثُمَّ تُقْنِعُ يَدَيْكَ — يَقُولُ: تَرْفَعُهُمَا — إِلَى رَبِّكَ … وَتَقُولُ: يَا رَبِّ يَا رَبِّ، وَمَنْ لَمْ يَفْعَلْ ذَلِكَ فَهُوَ كَذَا وَكَذَا»
Narrated by al-Faḍl b. ʿAbbās  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhī
Translation
"Prayer is two by two: a tashahhud after each two rakʿas; and humility, supplication, and self-abasement; then you raise your hands — he says: lift them — toward your Lord… saying 'O Lord, O Lord'; and whoever does not do that, it is so-and-so [deficient]."
Class Notes
  • Shāfiʿī: two by two. Abu Hanifa: four is always better. Abu Yūsuf & Imām Muḥammad: four by day, two at night — their view is best, most in accordance with the narrations, and the least interpretive (least muʾawwal).
  • Abu Hanifa's proof: an athar from Aisha that he prayed four at night — but al-Kashmīrī explains a fuller narration shows it was with two salāms, i.e. actually done two-by-two (so: four at night, but in pairs).
  • Word glosses:
    • takhashshuʿ (تَخَشُّع) = inner humility / inner focus.
    • tadarruʿ (تَضَرُّع) = physical (outward) humility / focus.
    • tamaskun (تَمَسْكُن) = showing neediness / begging (an augmented form — the root letters fitted into a longer pattern).
    • «تُقْنِعُ يَدَيْكَ» (raising the hands) is a proof of making duʿāʾ / asking of God after the prayer.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 805); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ صِفَةِ الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨١١
«صَلَّى بِنَا رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ الظُّهْرَ، وَفِي مُؤَخَّرِ الصُّفُوفِ رَجُلٌ فَأَسَاءَ الصَّلَاةَ … يَا فُلَانُ أَلَا تَتَّقِي اللَّهَ؟ أَلَا تَرَى كَيْفَ تُصَلِّي؟ … وَاللَّهِ إِنِّي لَأَرَى مِنْ خَلْفِي كَمَا أَرَى مِنْ بَيْنِ يَدَيَّ»
Narrated by Abu Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Aḥmad
Class Notes
  • Ibn Ḥajar: the Prophet's ﷺ seeing behind his back during the prayer was because Allah willed it (divinely enabled).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 811); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٢٤
«أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ وَأَبَا بَكْرٍ وَعُمَرَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُمَا كَانُوا يَفْتَتِحُونَ الصَّلَاةَ بِـ﴿الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ﴾»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Translation
"The Prophet ﷺ, Abū Bakr and ʿUmar used to begin the prayer with al-ḥamdu lillāhi rabbi l-ʿālamīn."
Class Notes
  • Masʾala: Do you recite Qur'ān behind the imam? (partial — full notes tomorrow):
  • View 1 — reciting al-Fātiḥa is obligatory behind the imam (you recite it quietly, even in the silent/sirrī prayers, and in the loud/jahrī prayers):
  • 1. Ḥadīth of ʿUbāda b. al-Ṣāmit (see #854): they were reciting behind the Messenger ﷺ, the recitation became heavy on him, and he said — do not recite [along] except al-Fātiḥa: «لَا تَفْعَلُوا إِلَّا بِفَاتِحَةِ الْكِتَابِ، فَإِنَّهُ لَا صَلَاةَ لِمَنْ لَمْ يَقْرَأْ بِهَا».
  • 2. The famous hadith: "there is no prayer for the one who does not recite the Opening of the Book" — «لَا صَلَاةَ لِمَنْ لَمْ يَقْرَأْ بِفَاتِحَةِ الْكِتَابِ» (ʿUbāda; Bukhārī & Muslim).
  • Our view (Ḥanafī) — no recitation behind the imam. Five proofs:
  • 1. The āya (al-Aʿrāf 7:204): «وَإِذَا قُرِئَ الْقُرْآنُ فَاسْتَمِعُوا لَهُ وَأَنْصِتُوا لَعَلَّكُمْ تُرْحَمُونَ» — "And when the Qur'ān is recited, listen to it and stay silent." Some say it is about the Friday khuṭba, others about the prayer; even if it is the khuṭba, we still take proof from the generality (ʿumūm) of the āya.
  • 2. Jābir relates from the Messenger ﷺ: "whoever has an imam, the imam's recitation is a recitation for him" — «مَنْ كَانَ لَهُ إِمَامٌ فَقِرَاءَةُ الْإِمَامِ لَهُ قِرَاءَةٌ» (Ibn Mājah, Aḥmad, al-Dāraquṭnī; mursal in the Muwaṭṭaʾ). Mirqat classes it as al-shādh al-maqbūl (the accepted anomalous), and notes Ibn al-Humām elaborates its many chains (ṭuruq) in Fatḥ al-Qadīr (Sharḥ al-Hidāya).
  • 3. The munāzaʿa hadith (see #855): he ﷺ led a loud prayer, then asked whether anyone recited behind him, and said «مَا لِي أُنَازَعُ الْقُرْآنَ؟» — after which the people stopped reciting. Three reasons this is a proof for us:
    1. The Prophet ﷺ asked as a rebuke, which shows we are not to recite.
    2. Only one man said "yes" — not everyone — showing most already knew to stay silent.
    3. His word munāzaʿa = to contend / infringe on another's right; here you would be infringing the imam's right to be the sole reciter.
  • 4. «وَإِذَا قَرَأَ فَأَنْصِتُوا» ("and when he recites, be silent") (see #857).
  • 5. al-ʿAynī cited the narrations of eighty Companions who rejected reciting behind the imam.
  • Our response to the opposing proofs:
  • One of their two ḥadīths (unsure which — likely the ʿUbāda "do not recite except al-Fātiḥa") has a hidden defect: it is maʿlūl (مَعْلُول). (Supporting this, Lamaat flags an internal tension in the ʿUbāda hadith — see #854.)
  • "No prayer for the one who does not recite the Opening of the Book" refers to the one praying alone (munfarid) or the imam himself — not the one praying behind an imam.
  • The madhhab positions in full (from Lamaat):
  • Abū Ḥanīfa: the muqtadī recites nothing behind the imam — neither in the silent (sirrī) nor the loud (jahrī) prayers. (Imām Muḥammad reports reciting the Fātiḥa is mustaḥabb as a precaution (iḥtiyāṭ); it is makrūh to the two companions because of the threat (waʿīd) in the texts.)
  • al-Shāfiʿī: the muqtadī recites the Fātiḥa silently, even in the loud prayers.
  • There is ijmāʿ that it is makrūh for the muqtadī to recite aloud, even if he cannot hear his imam.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 824); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٢٥
«إِذَا أَمَّنَ الْإِمَامُ فَأَمِّنُوا، فَإِنَّهُ مَنْ وَافَقَ تَأْمِينُهُ تَأْمِينَ الْمَلَائِكَةِ غُفِرَ لَهُ مَا تَقَدَّمَ مِنْ ذَنْبِهِ»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon (Bukhārī & Muslim)
Translation
"Say āmīn when the imam says it, for whoever's saying of āmīn coincides with that of the angels will have his past sins forgiven."
Class Notes
  • Masʾala: who says āmīn — the imam, the follower, or both?:
  • Jamhūr + Abū Ḥanīfa's main view: both the imam and the follower say āmīn. Two proofs: (1) this hadith — «إِذَا أَمَّنَ الْإِمَامُ فَأَمِّنُوا» shows the imam himself says it; (2) hadith 845 (Wāʾil).
  • Opinion from Imām Aḥmad: only the followers say āmīn. Proof: the narration that separates "[the imam] says…" then "[the followers] say āmīn" — this division negates sharing, so only the one side says it.
  • A further discussion: do you say āmīn with the imam or after him? — two sides, detail later.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 825); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٢٦ (٨٢٧)
«إِذَا صَلَّيْتُمْ فَأَقِيمُوا صُفُوفَكُمْ، ثُمَّ لِيَؤُمَّكُمْ أَحَدُكُمْ، فَإِذَا كَبَّرَ فَكَبِّرُوا، وَإِذَا قَالَ ﴿غَيْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا الضَّالِّينَ﴾ فَقُولُوا آمِينَ يُجِبْكُمُ اللَّهُ، فَإِذَا كَبَّرَ وَرَكَعَ فَكَبِّرُوا وَارْكَعُوا، فَإِنَّ الْإِمَامَ يَرْكَعُ قَبْلَكُمْ وَيَرْفَعُ قَبْلَكُمْ، فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: فَتِلْكَ بِتِلْكَ…» — وَفِي رِوَايَةٍ عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ وَقَتَادَةَ: «وَإِذَا قَرَأَ فَأَنْصِتُوا»
Narrated by Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī (riwāya: Abū Hurayra & Qatāda)  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Class Notes
  • Note: the riwāya «وَإِذَا قَرَأَ فَأَنْصِتُوا» is part of the Ḥanafī evidence for staying silent behind the imam (cf. #824, #857). When he ﷺ made them hear the recitation [in a silent prayer], it was a form of teaching the Companions — not the normal practice.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 826); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٢٨
«كَانَ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَقْرَأُ فِي الظُّهْرِ فِي الْأُولَيَيْنِ بِأُمِّ الْكِتَابِ وَسُورَتَيْنِ، وَفِي الرَّكْعَتَيْنِ الْأُخْرَيَيْنِ بِأُمِّ الْكِتَابِ، وَيُسْمِعُنَا الْآيَةَ أَحْيَانًا، وَيُطَوِّلُ فِي الرَّكْعَةِ الْأُولَى مَا لَا يُطَوِّلُ فِي الرَّكْعَةِ الثَّانِيَةِ، وَهَكَذَا فِي الْعَصْرِ، وَهَكَذَا فِي الصُّبْحِ»
Narrated by Abū Qatāda  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon
Translation
"In the first two rakʿas of Ẓuhr he recited the Fātiḥa and two sūras, and in the last two the Fātiḥa, sometimes letting us hear a verse; he prolonged the first rakʿa more than the second, and likewise in ʿAṣr and in Fajr."
Class Notes
  • Commentary (Mirqat): in the last two rakʿas only the Fātiḥa is recited — no sūra; this is our (Ḥanafī) madhhab («وَهَذَا مَذْهَبُنَا»).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 828); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٣٢
«سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَقْرَأُ فِي الْمَغْرِبِ بِ﴿الْمُرْسَلَاتِ عُرْفًا﴾»
Narrated by Umm al-Faḍl bint al-Ḥārith  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon
Translation
"I heard God's Messenger reciting al-Mursalāt at the sunset prayer."
Class Notes
  • Commentary (Mirqat): this was occasional, to show permissibility (li-bayān al-jawāz) — the recommended recitation at Maghrib is the short mufaṣṣal (qiṣār al-mufaṣṣal).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 832); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٣٣
«كَانَ مُعَاذٌ يُصَلِّي مَعَ النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ ثُمَّ يَأْتِي فَيَؤُمُّ قَوْمَهُ، فَصَلَّى لَيْلَةً مَعَ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ الْعِشَاءَ، ثُمَّ أَتَى قَوْمَهُ فَأَمَّهُمْ فَافْتَتَحَ بِسُورَةِ الْبَقَرَةِ، فَانْحَرَفَ رَجُلٌ فَسَلَّمَ ثُمَّ صَلَّى وَحْدَهُ وَانْصَرَفَ… فَأَقْبَلَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ عَلَى مُعَاذٍ فَقَالَ: يَا مُعَاذُ أَفَتَّانٌ أَنْتَ؟ اقْرَأْ ﴿الشَّمْسِ وَضُحَاهَا﴾ و﴿وَاللَّيْلِ إِذَا يَغْشَى﴾ و﴿سَبِّحِ اسْمَ رَبِّكَ الْأَعْلَى﴾»
Narrated by Jābir  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon
Class Notes
  • Note (length of recitation): an imam should lighten the prayer for those behind him.
  • Masʾala: can a person praying nafl (optional) lead people praying farḍ (obligatory)?:
  • View: No — Abū Ḥanīfa, Mālik, and an opinion from Aḥmad.
  • Proof: the Prophet ﷺ said "the imam is responsible / a guarantor (ḍāmin)" — «الْإِمَامُ ضَامِنٌ» — and the imam carries something equal or higher, so a nafl imam cannot carry the followers' farḍ.
  • The opposing (Shāfiʿī) proof — and this very hadith (833): al-Qāḍī said this hadith shows the permissibility of a muftariḍ following a mutanaffil (اقتداء المفترض بالمتنفل), since whoever performs a farḍ then repeats it, the repeat falls as nafl — and the Prophet ﷺ rebuked Muʿādh only for lengthening, not for leading a farḍ while himself praying nafl. The famous phrase «هِيَ لَهُ تَطَوُّعٌ وَلَهُمْ فَرِيضَةٌ» ("it was voluntary for him and obligatory for them") is from al-Shāfiʿī's own ijtihād (known only through his transmission) — not part of the hadith's wording.
  • Ṣalāt al-khawf — the part I didn't follow, now from the commentary (Lamaat / Ibn al-Humām): at Dhāt al-Riqāʿ the Prophet ﷺ prayed two rakʿas with each of the two groups — so it was four rakʿas for him and two for each group — which also looks like a muftariḍ following a mutanaffil (his second pair being extra). The Ḥanafī replies:
  • (a) perhaps that is a special feature (khaṣīṣa) of ṣalāt al-khawf («لَعَلَّ ذَلِكَ مِنْ خَصَائِصِ صَلَاةِ الْخَوْفِ») — not generalizable to ordinary prayer.
  • (b) it only binds us as an ilzām (because we hold the traveler's farḍ is two rakʿas, so the extra pair is nafl); but it is no proof for the Shāfiʿī's own madhhab, since for him all four rakʿas fall as farḍ.
  • Our response to the Muʿādh hadith:
  • Muʿādh prayed nafl with the Prophet ﷺ and farḍ when leading his people, showing matching status, not nafl-for-farḍ.
  • Imām al-Ṭaḥāwī (via Ibn al-Humām): the ruling is abrogated — it was when the farḍ used to be prayed twice, then abrogated — citing Ibn ʿUmar: «نُهِيَ أَنْ تُصَلَّى فَرِيضَةٌ فِي يَوْمٍ مَرَّتَيْنِ» ("a farḍ is not to be prayed twice in one day"), since "a prohibition only comes after a prior permission" («النَّهْيُ لَا يَكُونُ إِلَّا بَعْدَ الْإِبَاحَةِ») — proof of naskh.
  • (Aside from the commentary: the man who broke off behind Muʿādh was named Sulaym — said also Ḥaram/Ḥarām b. Abī Kaʿb al-Anṣārī.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 833); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٣٨
«كَانَ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَقْرَأُ فِي الْفَجْرِ يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ بِ﴿الم تَنْزِيلُ﴾ فِي الرَّكْعَةِ الْأُولَى، وَفِي الثَّانِيَةِ ﴿هَلْ أَتَى عَلَى الْإِنْسَانِ﴾»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Agreed upon
Translation
"The Prophet ﷺ used to recite at the dawn prayer on Friday Alif Lām Mīm — the Sajda in the first rakʿa, and in the second Has there come upon man…"
Class Notes
  • Note: reciting these specific portions in these specific prayers is only recommended (mustaḥabb) — not a sunna or emphasized sunna (sunna muʾakkada).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 838); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٤٤
«كَانَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَفْتَتِحُ صَلَاتَهُ بِ﴿بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِيمِ﴾»
Narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhī (who said its chain "is not approved")
Translation
"God's Messenger used to begin his prayer with Bismillāhi r-raḥmāni r-raḥīm."
Class Notes
  • Commentary (Mirqat, Faṣl 2): understood as silently (sirran) — so it does not contradict the earlier reports that he ﷺ did not say the basmala aloud.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 844); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٤٥
«سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَقْرَأُ ﴿غَيْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا الضَّالِّينَ﴾ فَقَالَ: آمِينَ، مَدَّ بِهَا صَوْتَهُ»
Narrated by Wāʾil b. Ḥujr  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhī, Abū Dāwūd, Dārimī, Ibn Mājah
Translation
"I heard God's Messenger recite …nor of those who go astray and say āmīn, prolonging his voice with it."
Class Notes
  • Masʾala: is āmīn said out loud (jahran) or silently (sirran)?:
  • We say: either is fine/valid — the dispute is only over which is better.
  • al-Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad: out loud, by both imam and follower.
  • Abū Ḥanīfa, Sufyān al-Thawrī, Mālik: silently in all prayers, by both imam and follower.
  • Shāfiʿī/Aḥmad's proof: this hadith — they read madd (مَدّ, "prolonging") as rafʿ (رَفْع, "raising" the voice).
  • Counter: the chain runs through Sufyān al-Thawrī, who himself held āmīn should be said silently — so the very narrator did not understand "madd" as saying it aloud.
  • The other narration (via Shuʿba b. al-Ḥajjāj): «خَفَضَ بِهَا صَوْتَهُ» — he lowered his voice with it (vs al-Thawrī's «مَدَّ»). So madd here just means elongating/stretching the word — said silently but drawn out.
  • Why we prefer Shuʿba's chain — four reasons:
  • 1. al-Thawrī's own practice was to say āmīn silently — so his own narration «madd» must be reinterpreted (not "aloud").
  • 2. It agrees with the Qur'ān: «ادْعُوا رَبَّكُمْ تَضَرُّعًا وَخُفْيَةً» (al-Aʿrāf 7:55 — "call upon your Lord in humility and silently"), and āmīn is a type of duʿāʾ, so it should be silent.
  • 3. Shuʿba was strict against tadlīs (تدليس — concealing a chain defect), whereas al-Thawrī, though reliable, did tadlīs at times, so Shuʿba's chain is stronger.
  • 4. We can combine both narrations by taking madd in its literal linguistic meaning — he stretched the word, not that he raised his voice (so «madd» and «khafaḍa» agree).
  • What the commentary adds (verify / be aware — the muḥaddith view differs from our fiqh argument):
  • Mirqat (citing Mīrak): Shuʿba narrated «خَفَضَ بِهَا صَوْتَهُ», but "the ḥuffāẓ agreed he erred (ghalaṭ) in it, and the correct, well-known wording is «مَدَّ وَرَفَعَ بِهَا صَوْتَهُ»" (prolonged and raised). The «madd» version is from Tirmidhī, Aḥmad, Ibn Abī Shayba; the «rafaʿa» version from Abū Dāwūd.
  • Tirmidhī cited both the raising and the lowering narrations and preferred the aloud (jahr) one, quoting al-Bukhārī likewise: "this is the practice of most scholars among the Companions and Tābiʿīn."
  • So saying āmīn aloud is the Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad madhhab; Abū Ḥanīfa: say it silently absolutely (yusirru l-taʾmīn muṭlaqan); Mālik has two reports.
  • But the silent side is not without support: some scholars also authenticated the khafḍ (lowering) narration, and it is reported from ʿUmar that the imam keeps four things quiet — the taʿawwudh, the basmala, āmīn, and «سُبْحَانَكَ اللَّهُمَّ وَبِحَمْدِكَ» — and the like from Ibn Masʿūd.
  • Note: when he ﷺ asked "what is your opinion", it was to show the importance of the action.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 845); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٤٧
«أَنَّ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ صَلَّى الْمَغْرِبَ بِسُورَةِ الْأَعْرَافِ فَرَّقَهَا فِي رَكْعَتَيْنِ»
Narrated by ʿĀʾisha  ·  Riwayah: Nasāʾī
Translation
"God's Messenger prayed Maghrib with sūrat al-Aʿrāf, dividing it between the two rakʿas."
Class Notes
  • Commentary (al-Tūrbishtī): reciting a long sūra at Maghrib — the tightest prayer in time — was to show what is permissible / the fullest form, not the regular practice (cf. #832).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 847); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٥٣
«مَا صَلَّيْتُ وَرَاءَ أَحَدٍ أَشْبَهَ صَلَاةً بِرَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ مِنْ فُلَانٍ. قَالَ سُلَيْمَانُ: صَلَّيْتُ خَلْفَهُ فَكَانَ يُطِيلُ الرَّكْعَتَيْنِ الْأُولَيَيْنِ مِنَ الظُّهْرِ وَيُخَفِّفُ الْأُخْرَيَيْنِ، وَيُخَفِّفُ الْعَصْرَ، وَيَقْرَأُ فِي الْمَغْرِبِ بِقِصَارِ الْمُفَصَّلِ، وَيَقْرَأُ فِي الْعِشَاءِ بِوَسَطِ الْمُفَصَّلِ، وَيَقْرَأُ فِي الصُّبْحِ بِطِوَالِ الْمُفَصَّلِ»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra (via Sulaymān b. Yasār)  ·  Riwayah: Nasāʾī (ḥasan)
Class Notes
  • Commentary (Mirqat): the unnamed "so-and-so" (fulān) was said to be ʿAlī, or ʿAmr b. Salama, or ʿUmar b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz — though al-Tūrbishtī notes that last is chronologically unreliable (ʿUmar b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz was born 61 AH, after Abū Hurayra's death).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 853); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٥٤
«كُنَّا خَلْفَ النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فِي صَلَاةِ الْفَجْرِ فَقَرَأَ، فَثَقُلَتْ عَلَيْهِ الْقِرَاءَةُ، فَلَمَّا فَرَغَ قَالَ: لَعَلَّكُمْ تَقْرَؤُونَ خَلْفَ إِمَامِكُمْ؟ قُلْنَا: نَعَمْ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ. قَالَ: لَا تَفْعَلُوا إِلَّا بِفَاتِحَةِ الْكِتَابِ، فَإِنَّهُ لَا صَلَاةَ لِمَنْ لَمْ يَقْرَأْ بِهَا» — وَفِي رِوَايَةٍ لِأَبِي دَاوُدَ: «وَأَنَا أَقُولُ: مَا لِي يُنَازِعُنِي الْقُرْآنُ؟ فَلَا تَقْرَؤُوا بِشَيْءٍ مِنَ الْقُرْآنِ إِذَا جَهَرْتُ إِلَّا بِأُمِّ الْقُرْآنِ»
Narrated by ʿUbāda b. al-Ṣāmit  ·  Riwayah: Abū Dāwūd, Tirmidhī (ḥasan)
Class Notes
  • Note: this is the obligatory-Fātiḥa camp's main proof (see the masʾala under #824).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 854); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٥٥
«أَنَّ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ انْصَرَفَ مِنْ صَلَاةٍ جَهَرَ فِيهَا بِالْقِرَاءَةِ فَقَالَ: هَلْ قَرَأَ مَعِي أَحَدٌ مِنْكُمْ آنِفًا؟ فَقَالَ رَجُلٌ: نَعَمْ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ. قَالَ: إِنِّي أَقُولُ: مَا لِي أُنَازَعُ الْقُرْآنَ؟ قَالَ: فَانْتَهَى النَّاسُ عَنِ الْقِرَاءَةِ مَعَ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فِيمَا جَهَرَ فِيهِ بِالْقِرَاءَةِ مِنَ الصَّلَوَاتِ حِينَ سَمِعُوا ذَلِكَ»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Mālik, Aḥmad, Abū Dāwūd, Tirmidhī, Nasāʾī (Ibn Mājah similar)
Class Notes
  • Note: our daleel (the munāzaʿa proof — see the three reasons under #824).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 855); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٥٦
«إِنَّ الْمُصَلِّيَ يُنَاجِي رَبَّهُ، فَلْيَنْظُرْ مَا يُنَاجِيهِ بِهِ، وَلَا يَجْهَرْ بَعْضُكُمْ عَلَى بَعْضٍ بِالْقُرْآنِ»
Narrated by Ibn ʿUmar & al-Bayāḍī  ·  Riwayah: Aḥmad (ṣaḥīḥ)
Translation
"The one who prays is in intimate converse with his Lord, so let him consider how he addresses Him; and none of you should recite the Qur'ān more loudly than another."
Class Notes
  • Note: Abū Ḥanīfa's proof for silent recitation.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 856); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ
حديث ٨٥٧
«إِنَّمَا جُعِلَ الْإِمَامُ لِيُؤْتَمَّ بِهِ، فَإِذَا كَبَّرَ فَكَبِّرُوا، وَإِذَا قَرَأَ فَأَنْصِتُوا»
Narrated by Abū Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Abū Dāwūd, Nasāʾī, Ibn Mājah (ṣaḥīḥ)
Translation
"The imam is appointed only to be followed: when he says the takbīr, say it; and when he recites, be silent."
Class Notes
  • Note: Abū Ḥanīfa's proof — the fourth Ḥanafī daleel for staying silent behind the imam (see #824).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 857); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٦٨
«عَنْ أَنَسٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «أَقِيمُوا الرُّكُوعَ وَالسُّجُودَ فَوَاللَّهِ إِنِّي لَأَرَاكُمْ مِنْ بَعْدِي»»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim
Class Notes
  • The hadith's point:
  • The Prophet ﷺ reminds the companions that he can see behind himself in the prayer (Allah enables it for him) — and if he can see them, then Allah sees them all the more. (A motive for perfecting the prayer.)
  • What rukūʿ means:
  • Rukūʿ = inclining / bowing — inḥināʾ (الانحناء).
  • Why two prostrations but only one bowing:
  • The bowing is like an introduction — a muqaddima wa wasīla (مقدمة ووسيلة), a preliminary/means leading to the maqṣad (the goal), which is the sujūd itself. So the sujūd, being the objective, is the part that is repeated.
  • Mirqat footnote (full vocalized Arabic to be grabbed from the commentary):
  • Mirqat, explaining the chapter title (rukūʿ):
  • It is a pillar (rukn) by the Book, the Sunnah, and the ijmāʿ of the umma.
  • Linguistically: inclining (inḥināʾ).
  • It is said to be among the special characteristics of this nation (khaṣāʾiṣ) — based on some scholars' reading of «wa-rkaʿū maʿa l-rākiʿīn» (al-Baqara 2:43, "and bow with those who bow"): this is only said because the previous nations' prayer had no rukūʿ in it; the ones who do bow are Muhammad ﷺ and his umma — so the verse effectively means "pray with those who pray."
  • Wisdom of repeating the sujūd:
    • It is the greatest sign of humility (khuḍūʿ) — placing the most noble part (the face/forehead) onto what the feet and slippers tread; so it is fitting to repeat it.
    • The first sujūd is mutakaffil (متكفّل — guaranteeing/fulfilling) the objective, as it is narrated: "the closest the slave is to his Lord is while he is in sujūd."
    • Said it is repeated as a sign that man was created from earth, to it he returns, and from it he is brought forth: the 1st sujūd = from it you were created, the 2nd = to it you return, and the raising up = from it you will be brought forth a second time.
    • Also said: when the angels were commanded to prostrate and did so, they saw that Iblīs / the cursed ones had not prostrated — so they prostrated a second time in gratitude (shukran) to Allah Most High for enabling them to prostrate.
    • What is most apparent (al-aẓhar): it is purely an act of worship (taʿabbud).
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat — exact Arabic of the chapter-title footnote):
  • Chapter title / rukn: «(١٣) بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ. هُوَ رَكْنٌ بِالْكِتَابِ وَالسُّنَّةِ وَإِجْمَاعِ الْأُمَّةِ، وَهُوَ لُغَةً الِانْحِنَاءُ، وَقَدْ يُرَادُ بِهِ الْخُضُوعُ، وَقِيلَ: هُوَ مِنْ خَصَائِصِنَا لِقَوْلِ بَعْضِ الْمُفَسِّرِينَ فِي قَوْلِهِ تَعَالَى ﴿وَارْكَعُوا مَعَ الرَّاكِعِينَ﴾ إِنَّمَا قَالَ لَهُمْ ذَلِكَ؛ لِأَنَّ صَلَاتَهُمْ لَا رُكُوعَ فِيهَا، وَالرَّاكِعُونَ مُحَمَّدٌ ﷺ وَأُمَّتُهُ».
  • Wisdom of repeating the sujūd: «وَقِيلَ: حِكْمَةُ تَكْرِيرِ السُّجُودِ دُونَهُ أَنَّهُ وَسِيلَةٌ وَمُقَدِّمَةٌ لِلسُّجُودِ الَّذِي هُوَ الْخُضُوعُ الْأَعْظَمُ، لِمَا فِيهِ مِنْ مُبَاشَرَةِ أَشْرَفِ مَا فِي الْإِنْسَانِ لِمَوَاطِئِ الْأَقْدَامِ وَالنِّعَالِ، فَنَاسَبَ تَكْرِيرَهُ؛ لِأَنَّهُ الْمُتَكَفِّلُ بِالْمَقْصُودِ، حَيْثُ وَرَدَ: أَقْرَبُ مَا يَكُونُ الْعَبْدُ مِنْ رَبِّهِ وَهُوَ سَاجِدٌ. وَقِيلَ: إِنَّمَا كُرِّرَ إِشَارَةً إِلَى أَنَّ الْإِنْسَانَ خُلِقَ مِنَ الْأَرْضِ وَإِلَيْهَا يَعُودُ وَمِنْهَا يَخْرُجُ، فَكَأَنَّهُ يَقُولُ فِي السَّجْدَةِ الْأُولَى: مِنْهَا خَلَقْتَنِي، وَفِي الثَّانِيَةِ: وَفِيهَا تُعِيدُنِي، وَفِي الرَّفْعِ الثَّانِي: وَمِنْهَا تُخْرِجُنِي تَارَةً أُخْرَى. وَقِيلَ: لِأَنَّ الْمَلَائِكَةَ أُمِرُوا بِالسُّجُودِ وَسَجَدُوا، رَأَوْا بَعْدَ السُّجُودِ أَنَّ اللَّعِينَ لَمْ يَسْجُدْ، فَسَجَدُوا سَجْدَةً ثَانِيَةً شُكْرًا لِلَّهِ تَعَالَى. وَالْأَظْهَرُ أَنَّهُ تَعَبُّدٌ مَحْضٌ».
  • (matn gloss: «أَقِيمُوا» = أَتِمُّوهُمَا; «إِنِّي لَأَرَاكُمْ مِنْ بَعْدِي» = a khāriqa/miracle — real sight, peculiar to him ﷺ.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 868); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٦٩
«وَعَنِ الْبَرَاءِ قَالَ: كَانَ رُكُوعُ النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ وَسُجُودُهُ وَبَيْنَ السَّجْدَتَيْنِ وَإِذَا رَفَعَ مِنَ الرُّكُوعِ مَا خَلَا الْقِيَامَ وَالْقُعُودَ قَرِيبًا مِنَ السَّوَاءِ»
Narrated by Al-Bara' b. 'Azib  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim
Class Notes
  • The components of the prayer should take about the same length of time — the sujūd, the sitting between the two prostrations, and the bowing.
  • Only sometimes should the bowing be made a little longer, and only sometimes the prostration after rising from the bowing be made a little longer.
  • Otherwise they would all be roughly equal in length.
  • Making it long is not really a sunnah.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 869); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٠
«وَعَنْ أَنَسٍ قَالَ: كَانَ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ إِذَا قَالَ: «سَمِعَ اللَّهُ لِمَنْ حَمِدَهُ» قَامَ حَتَّى نَقُولَ: قَدْ أَوْهَمَ ثُمَّ يَسْجُدُ وَيَقْعُدُ بَيْنَ السَّجْدَتَيْنِ حَتَّى نَقُولَ: قَدْ أَوْهَمَ. رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Anas  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Class Notes
  • This is not the normal practice; the hadith is a proof that these positions should not be too long.
  • The sitting between the two prostrations is called the jalsa (جلسة). (User first said qawma, then corrected to jalsa — verify against commentary.)
  • The standing after the bowing is called the qawma (قومة).
  • For ordinary prayers these were light — it was not always this long.
  • A word here means "to cause to be mistaken / to confuse" (lemma to pull from commentary — possibly lubbisa, لُبِّس).
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Lamaat):
  • jalsa / qawma confirmed — Mirqat 869: the sitting between the two prostrations = «وَجُلُوسُهُ بَيْنَهُمَا» (al-julūs/al-jalsa); the standing after rukūʿ = «وَقِيَامُهُ حِينَ رَفَعَ» (al-qiyām/al-qawma). Lamaat names them: «الْقَوْمَةُ مِنَ الرُّكُوعِ، وَالْجِلْسَةُ بَيْنَ السَّجْدَتَيْنِ... يَنْبَغِي أَنْ تَكُونَ الْقَوْمَةُ وَالْجِلْسَةُ وَاجِبَتَيْنِ لِمُوَاظَبَتِهِ عَلَيْهِمَا» (wājib per Ibn al-Humām; farāʾiḍ per Abū Yūsuf, sunan per the other two).
  • Correction: the word glossed "cause to be mistaken" is أَوْهَمَ (not lubbisa). Mirqat 870: «أَوْهَمْتُهُ إِذَا أَوْقَعْتُهُ فِي الْغَلَطِ»; Ibn Ḥajar: «وَقَعَ فِي وَهْمِ النَّاسِ أَنَّهُ تَرَكَهَا» — he lingered so long that we suspected he'd dropped a rakʿa / the 2nd sajda. (لُبِّس NOT FOUND here.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 870); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧١
«وَعَنْ عَائِشَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهَا قَالَتْ: كَانَ النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يُكْثِرُ أَنْ يَقُولَ فِي رُكُوعِهِ وَسُجُودِهِ: «سُبْحَانَكَ اللَّهُمَّ رَبَّنَا وَبِحَمْدِكَ اللَّهُمَّ اغْفِرْ لِي» يَتَأَوَّلُ الْقُرْآنَ. مُتَّفَقٌ عَلَيْهِ»
Narrated by 'A'isha  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim
Class Notes
  • The word yataʾawwal (يتأوّل) here means "acting upon / applying" — i.e. he was acting upon the Qur'an.
  • General note (re 869–871): the main narrations show this lengthening was not his usual practice — so we take it as applying to optional (nafl) and sunnah prayers.
  • Footnote (Lamaat — full vocalized Arabic to be grabbed):
  • yataʾawwal means he would act upon what he was commanded with (i.e. do it abundantly) — based on Sūrat al-Naṣr, āya 3: «fa-sabbiḥ bi-ḥamdi rabbika wa-staghfirh» (so his tasbīḥ + istighfār in rukūʿ and sujūd is him applying that āya).
  • The original meaning of the word (the root of taʾwīl) is to return / to turn around; it then came to be used for that which the matter or command turns to (its outcome/end).
  • The word subḥān(ak) is a maṣdar (verbal noun). confirm.
  • Commentary fill (Lamaat — exact footnote):
  • «قَوْلُهُ (يَتَأَوَّلُ الْقُرْآنَ) حَالٌ مِنْ فَاعِلِ (يَقُولُ)، أَيْ يُكْثِرُ قَوْلَ ذَلِكَ حَالَ كَوْنِهِ مُبَيِّنًا مَا هُوَ الْمُرَادُ مِنْ قَوْلِهِ تَعَالَى ﴿فَسَبِّحْ بِحَمْدِ رَبِّكَ وَاسْتَغْفِرْهُ﴾، وَأَصْلُ الْأَوْلِ الرُّجُوعُ وَالِانْصِرَافُ، وَالْمَآلُ مَا يَرْجِعُ إِلَيْهِ الْأَمْرُ، وَ(سُبْحَانَكَ) مَصْدَرٌ لِفِعْلِهِ الْمُقَدَّرِ، أَيْ سَبَّحْتُكَ وَنَزَّهْتُكَ كَمَا يَلِيقُ بِنَزَاهَتِكَ، وَمَعْنَى قَوْلِهِ (وَبِحَمْدِكَ) بِتَوْفِيقِكَ وَفَضْلِكَ الْمُوجِبِ لِحَمْدِكَ سَبَّحْتُكَ لَا بِحَوْلِي وَقُوَّتِي».
  • This confirms: taʾwīl's root = to return/turn (al-rujūʿ); subḥān is a maṣdar of an implied verb; acting on Sūrat al-Naṣr. (Tibi adds: «مَأْخُوذٌ مِنْ آلَ إِذَا رَجَعَ».)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 871); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٢
«وَعَنْهَا أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ كَانَ يَقُولُ فِي رُكُوعِهِ وَسُجُودِهِ: «سُبُّوحٌ قُدُّوسٌ رَبُّ الْمَلَائِكَةِ وَالرُّوحِ». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by 'A'isha  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Class Notes
  • This is a good duʿāʾ — you should memorize it. (identify the duʿāʾ from the matn.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 872); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٣
«وَعَنِ ابْنِ عَبَّاسٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «أَلَا إِنِّي نُهِيتُ أَنْ أَقْرَأَ الْقُرْآنَ رَاكِعًا أَوْ سَاجِدًا فَأَمَّا الرُّكُوعُ فَعَظِّمُوا فِيهِ الرَّبَّ وَأَمَّا السُّجُودُ فَاجْتَهِدُوا فِي الدُّعَاءِ فَقَمِنٌ أَنْ يُسْتَجَابَ لَكُمْ». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Ibn 'Abbas  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Class Notes
  • qamin (قَمِن) — "most worthy / deserving." The sense: it is more worthy that your call/request be answered while you are in prostration.
  • taʿẓīm (تعظيم) — to magnify / glorify Allah; i.e. what we say in the bowing (subḥāna rabbiya al-ʿaẓīm, etc.).
  • Meaning of being abundant / intense in duʿāʾ in the sujūd: do a lot of duʿāʾ — specifically the transmitted (maʾthūr) duʿāʾs that do not resemble human speech; try to do these in the optional prayers.
  • Second opinion: it means recite the main dhikr (subḥāna rabbiya al-aʿlā) with much vigor and strength — but that is not the apparent meaning.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Tibi):
  • قَمِنٌ — Mirqat: «بِفَتْحِ الْمِيمِ وَتُكْسَرُ... وَالْمَعْنَى جَدِيرٌ وَخَلِيقٌ وَلَائِقٌ وَحَقِيقٌ أَنْ يُسْتَجَابَ لَكُمْ» (with fatḥ it's a maṣdar, invariable; with kasr a description) — most worthy that you be answered, since sujūd is the closest one is to his Lord.
  • taʿẓīm in rukūʿ: «فَأَمَّا الرُّكُوعُ فَعَظِّمُوا فِيهِ الرَّبَّ» = say «سُبْحَانَ رَبِّيَ الْعَظِيمِ»; duʿāʾ in sujūd: «فَاجْتَهِدُوا فِي الدُّعَاءِ» = بَالِغُوا (be abundant).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 873); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٤
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «إِذَا قَالَ الْإِمَامُ: سَمِعَ اللَّهُ لِمَنْ حَمِدَهُ فَقُولُوا: اللَّهُمَّ رَبَّنَا لَكَ الْحَمْدُ فَإِنَّهُ مَنْ وَافَقَ قَوْلُهُ قَوْلَ الْمَلَائِكَةِ غُفِرَ لَهُ مَا تَقَدَّمَ مِنْ ذَنْبِهِ»»
Narrated by Abu Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari and Muslim
Class Notes
  • About what is said on rising from the bowing/prostration.
  • There is a division of roles: when the imām says one thing, the follower says the other — which shows that the followers do not say the other part.
  • The underlying principle: division negates sharing/partnership — exact Arabic to find, likely «al-qisma tunāfī l-shirka» (القسمة تنافي الشركة).
  • The masʾala — tasmīʿ vs. taḥmīd:
  • One praying alone (munfarid): does both — the tasmīʿ (samiʿa Allāhu liman ḥamidah) and the taḥmīd (rabbanā wa-laka l-ḥamd).
  • Follower (muqtadī): does only the taḥmīd.
  • The imām:
    • tasmīʿ only — according to Abū Ḥanīfa and Imām Mālik.
    • both — according to the two companions (Abū Yūsuf & Muḥammad), al-Shāfiʿī, and Aḥmad.
    • Their evidence: to find from the commentary.
  • Commentary fill:
  • NOT FOUND in the cached pages: the tasmīʿ/taḥmīd munfarid–imām–muqtadī division, the maxim «القسمة تنافي الشركة», and the imāms' evidences are not in Mirqat/Tibi/Lamaat on hadith 874 (they only gloss «سمع الله لمن حمده» = «تَقَبَّلَ اللهُ حَمْدَهُ وَأَجَابَهُ»). Keep your dictated version pending — this detail lives in the fiqh manuals, not this sharḥ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 874); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٦
«وَعَنْ أَبِي سَعِيدٍ الْخُدْرِيِّ قَالَ: كَانَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ إِذَا رَفَعَ رَأْسَهُ مِنَ الرُّكُوعِ قَالَ: «اللَّهُمَّ رَبَّنَا لَكَ الْحَمْدُ مِلْءَ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَمِلْءَ الْأَرْضِ وَمِلْءَ مَا شِئْتَ مِنْ شَيْءٍ بَعْدُ أَهْلَ الثَّنَاءِ وَالْمَجْدِ أَحَقُّ مَا قَالَ الْعَبْدُ وَكُلُّنَا لَكَ عَبْدٌ اللَّهُمَّ لَا مَانِعَ لِمَا أَعْطَيْتَ وَلَا مُعْطِيَ لِمَا مَنَعْتَ وَلَا يَنْفَعُ ذَا الْجَدِّ مِنْكَ الْجَدُّ». رَوَاهُ مُسْلِمٌ»
Narrated by Abu Sa'id al-Khudri  ·  Riwayah: Muslim
Class Notes
  • He said all of this in his qawma (قومة) — the standing when you are raised from the bowing.
  • jadd (جَدّ) (explanation to grab from Mirqat) — from «lā yanfaʿu dhā l-jaddi minka l-jadd»: the rich man's wealth will not benefit him; rather his actions and obedience will benefit him. (jadd = wealth / fortune / good luck.)
  • We should fulfill the responsibilities / rights of wealth — iktifāʾ? (اكتفاء) verify the exact Arabic word.
  • Related narrations: the first to enter Paradise are the poor people; the rich will be chained up — some narrations mention 40 years.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Tibi):
  • الْجَدّ — Mirqat: «الْمَشْهُورُ فَتْحُ الْجِيمِ، بِمَعْنَى الْعَظَمَةِ أَوِ الْحَظِّ أَوِ الْغِنَى أَوِ النَّسَبِ»; al-Tūrbishtī: «لَا يَنْفَعُ ذَا الْغِنَى مِنْكَ غِنَاهُ» («مِنْكَ» = عِنْدَكَ); al-Rāghib: «لَا يُتَوَصَّلُ إِلَى ثَوَابِ اللَّهِ بِالْجَدِّ [fortune] وَإِنَّمَا بِالْجِدِّ [diligence] بِالطَّاعَةِ». (variant kasr: «لَا يَنْفَعُهُ مُجَرَّدُ جِدِّهِ وَجُهْدِهِ، إِنَّمَا يَنْفَعُهُ التَّوْفِيقُ وَالْقَبُولُ».)
  • the "poor enter Paradise first / rich detained" line is NOT FOUND in the cached pages — keep as your own note.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 876); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٧
«وَعَنْ رِفَاعَةَ بْنِ رَافِعٍ قَالَ: كُنَّا نُصَلِّي وَرَاءَ النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَلَمَّا رَفَعَ رَأْسَهُ مِنَ الرَّكْعَةِ قَالَ: «سَمِعَ اللَّهُ لِمَنْ حَمِدَهُ». فَقَالَ رَجُلٌ وَرَاءَهُ: رَبَّنَا وَلَكَ الْحَمْدُ حَمْدًا كَثِيرًا طَيِّبًا مُبَارَكًا فِيهِ فَلَمَّا انْصَرَفَ قَالَ: «مَنِ الْمُتَكَلِّمُ آنِفًا؟» قَالَ: أَنَا. قَالَ: «رَأَيْتُ بِضْعَةً وَثَلَاثِينَ مَلَكًا يَبْتَدِرُونَهَا أَيُّهُمْ يَكْتُبُهَا أَوَّلُ». رَوَاهُ الْبُخَارِيُّ»
Narrated by Rifa'a b. Rafi'  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari
Class Notes
  • The word biḍʿ (بِضْع) = a number from 33 to 39 — here 33–39 angels (hastening to record the words of taḥmīd). confirm word from commentary.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat):
  • «بِضْعَةً وَثَلَاثِينَ مَلَكًا — (بِضْعَةً) وَهِيَ مِنَ الثَّلَاثَةِ إِلَى التِّسْعَةِ» — meaning biḍʿ = 3–9, hence 33–39 angels. «الظَّاهِرُ أَنَّ لِكُلِّ حَرْفٍ مَلَكًا، فَإِنَّ حُرُوفَ الْكَلِمَاتِ أَرْبَعٌ وَثَلَاثُونَ» (one angel per letter — the phrase has 34 letters); «يَبْتَدِرُونَهَا» = يُسَارِعُونَ فِي كِتَابَةِ هَذِهِ الْكَلِمَاتِ أَيُّهُمْ يَكْتُبُهَا أَوَّلَ.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 877); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٧٨
«عَنْ أَبِي مَسْعُودٍ الْأَنْصَارِيِّ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «لَا تُجْزِئُ صَلَاةُ الرَّجُلِ حَتَّى يُقِيمَ ظَهْرَهُ فِي الرُّكُوعِ وَالسُّجُودِ». رَوَاهُ أَبُو دَاوُدَ وَالتِّرْمِذِيُّ وَالنَّسَائِيُّ وَابْنُ مَاجَهْ وَالدَّارِمِيُّ وَقَالَ التِّرْمِذِيُّ: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ حَسَنٌ صَحِيحٌ»
Narrated by Abu Mas'ud al-Ansari  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud, Tirmidhi, Nasa'i, Ibn Majah, al-Darimi  ·  Grade: Hasan Sahih (Tirmidhi)
Class Notes
  • (You said "787" — taken as 878; confirm.)
  • This is a negation of the completeness/perfection of the act according to us (Ḥanafīs) — i.e. nafy al-kamāl, not nafy al-ṣiḥḥa (it negates perfection, not validity). confirm.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat):
  • 878 «لَا تُجْزِئُ صَلَاةُ الرَّجُلِ حَتَّى يُقِيمَ ظَهْرَهُ» — al-Muẓhir: the point is al-ṭumaʾnīna; wājib per al-Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad (they make it farḍ), wājib (not farḍ) per Abū Ḥanīfa & Muḥammad (established only by ẓannī proof). Its least = the span of one tasbīḥa. So «مَا صَلَّيْتَ» (885) negates a complete/correct prayer — a tahdīd, i.e. nafy al-kamāl, not literal invalidity. ✓ your note confirmed.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 878); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٨٠
«وَعَنْ عَوْنِ بْنِ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ عَنِ ابْنِ مَسْعُودٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «إِذَا رَكَعَ أَحَدُكُمْ فَقَالَ فِي رُكُوعِهِ: سُبْحَانَ رَبِّيَ الْعَظِيمِ ثَلَاثَ مَرَّاتٍ فَقَدْ تَمَّ رُكُوعُهُ وَذَلِكَ أَدْنَاهُ وَإِذَا سَجَدَ فَقَالَ فِي سُجُودِهِ سُبْحَانَ رَبِّيَ الْأَعْلَى ثَلَاثَ مَرَّاتٍ فَقَدْ تَمَّ سُجُودُهُ وَذَلِكَ أَدْنَاهُ». رَوَاهُ التِّرْمِذِيُّ وَأَبُو دَاوُدَ وَابْنُ مَاجَهْ وَقَالَ التِّرْمِذِيُّ: لَيْسَ إِسْنَادُهُ بِمُتَّصِلٍ لِأَنَّ عَوْنًا لَمْ يَلْقَ ابْنَ مَسْعُودٍ»
Narrated by Ibn Mas'ud (via 'Aun b. 'Abdallah)  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud, Ibn Majah  ·  Grade: isnad not connected
Class Notes
  • tamma / atamma (تمّ / أتمّ) means completion.
  • The dhikr (tasbīḥ) counts form tiers: the lowest of its completion = 3, the middle = 5, and 7 times = the (full) completion. Also mentioned in Mirqat. confirm the 3 / 5 / 7 tiers from Mirqat.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Lamaat):
  • «فَقَدْ تَمَّ رُكُوعُهُ» أَيْ كَمُلَ، وَإِلَّا فَأَصْلُ الْكَمَالِ يَحْصُلُ بِوَاحِدَةٍ. «وَذَلِكَ أَدْنَاهُ» = أَدْنَى الْكَمَالِ فِي الْعَدَدِ، وَأَكْمَلُهُ سَبْعُ مَرَّاتٍ، فَالْأَوْسَطُ خَمْسُ مَرَّاتٍ»— so adnā = 3, awsaṭ = 5, akmal = 7 ✓. (Ibn al-Humām: beyond 3 is better if kept odd** — 5 or 7; this is for the munfarid; the imām considers the followers. Per 883, aʿlā al-kamāl = 11.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 880); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٨١
«وَعَنْ حُذَيْفَةَ: أَنَّهُ صَلَّى مَعَ النَّبِيِّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَكَانَ يَقُولُ فِي رُكُوعِهِ: «سُبْحَانَ رَبِّيَ الْعَظِيمِ» وَفِي سُجُودِهِ: «سُبْحَانَ رَبِّيَ الْأَعْلَى». وَمَا أَتَى عَلَى آيَةِ رَحْمَةٍ إِلَّا وَقَفَ وَسَأَلَ وَمَا أَتَى عَلَى آيَةِ عَذَابٍ إِلَّا وَقَفَ وَتَعَوَّذَ. رَوَاهُ التِّرْمِذِيُّ وَأَبُو دَاوُدَ وَالدَّارِمِيُّ وَرَوَى النَّسَائِيُّ وَابْنُ مَاجَهْ إِلَى قَوْلِهِ: «الْأَعْلَى». وَقَالَ التِّرْمِذِيُّ: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ حَسَنٌ صَحِيحٌ»
Narrated by Hudhaifa  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud, al-Darimi (Nasa'i, Ibn Majah partial)  ·  Grade: Hasan Sahih (Tirmidhi)
Class Notes
  • This (the taʿawwudh, التعوّذ — confirm the exact act) is only for optional prayers.
  • According to al-Shāfiʿī and Aḥmad, it can be done in the obligatory prayer as well.
  • According to the Ḥanafīs, it is only in optional prayers — and not even in Tarāwīḥ, because Tarāwīḥ is attached to the obligatory prayers.
  • There is no narration that he ﷺ was doing taʿawwudh [in the obligatory prayer]. confirm wording/point.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Lamaat) — a correction on the act:
  • The act in 881 is stopping at each verse during recitation to ask (suʾāl) at a mercy-verse and seek refuge (taʿawwudh) at a punishment-verse — «وَمَا أَتَى عَلَى آيَةِ رَحْمَةٍ إِلَّا وَقَفَ وَسَأَلَ... وَمَا أَتَى عَلَى آيَةِ عَذَابٍ إِلَّا وَقَفَ وَتَعَوَّذَ». Mirqat: «حَمَلَهُ أَصْحَابُنَا وَالْمَالِكِيَّةُ عَلَى أَنَّ صَلَاتَهُ كَانَتْ نَافِلَةً لِعَدَمِ تَجْوِيزِهِمُ التَّعَوُّذَ وَالسُّؤَالَ أَثْنَاءَ الْقِرَاءَةِ فِي صَلَاةِ الْفَرْضِ». So: Ḥanafī & Mālikī: only in nāfila (✓ your point), others allow it even in farḍ. A specific mention of Tarāwīḥ was not in the cached pages — keep that part as your own note.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 881); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٨٣
«وَعَنِ ابْنً جُبَيْرٍ قَالَ: سَمِعْتُ أَنَسَ بْنَ مَالِكٍ يَقُولُ: مَا صَلَّيْتُ وَرَاءَ أَحَدٍ بَعْدَ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ أَشْبَهَ صَلَاةً بِصَلَاةِ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ مِنْ هَذَا الْفَتَى يَعْنِي عُمَرَ بْنَ عَبْدِ الْعَزِيزِ قَالَ: قَالَ: فَحَزَرْنَا رُكُوعَهُ عَشْرَ تَسْبِيحَاتٍ وَسُجُودَهُ عَشْرَ تَسْبِيحَاتٍ»
Narrated by Ibn Jubair  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud and Nasa'i transmitted it.
Class Notes
  • It is mentioned that ʿUmar b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz learned from Anas b. Mālik — fact-check: correct. ʿUmar narrated from Anas; when ʿUmar was governor of Medina (~87–93 AH) Anas was there. The famous report: Anas said "I have not prayed behind anyone whose prayer more resembles the Messenger's ﷺ than this youth" (= ʿUmar b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz).
  • "He estimated his (rukūʿ / qawma) to be ten and ten" — i.e. about ten tasbīḥāt in the bowing and ten in the standing/sujūd.
  • Whose bowing? The one praying is ʿUmar (the youth), but since the point is that his prayer mirrors the Prophet's, the pronoun can refer to the Prophet ﷺ or to ʿUmar himself. verify which from the commentary.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 883); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الرُّكُوعِ
حديث ٨٨٤
