  • From other hadiths of ʿĀʾisha, we understand this was a habitual practice of the Prophet ﷺ — he would pray these two after ʿAṣr (they were the make-up of the missed Ẓuhr sunnah).
  • There is also iḍṭirāb (اضطراب) in the narrations — i.e. inconsistency / discrepancy (a technical hadith defect: conflicting, unreconcilable versions; "confusion" loosely). The Prophet ﷺ said one should not do it; Umm Salama narrates one thing (then another), and ʿĀʾisha another — so the reports are muḍṭarib. (the word اضطراب itself wasn't in the cached pages for this hadith — keep as your own characterization.)
  • Commentary fill (exact Arabic — Mirqat / Lamaat):
  • The two rakʿas = the two of Ẓuhr (matn): «فَشَغَلُونِي عَنِ الرَّكْعَتَيْنِ اللَّتَيْنِ بَعْدَ الظُّهْرِ، فَهُمَا هَاتَانِ».
  • Making up a sunna is sunna (Shāfiʿī) — Mirqat: «وَهَذَا يَدُلُّ عَلَى أَنَّ قَضَاءَ السُّنَّةِ سُنَّةٌ، وَبِهِ أَخَذَ الشَّافِعِيُّ، قَالَهُ ابْنُ الْمَلَكِ».
  • Apparent meaning = khaṣāʾiṣ — Mirqat: «وَظَاهِرُ الْحَدِيثِ أَنَّ هَذَا مِنْ خُصُوصِيَّاتِهِ ﷺ لِعُمُومِ النَّهْيِ لِلْغَيْرِ»; al-Bayhaqī (Lamaat): «الَّذِي اخْتَصَّ بِهِ ﷺ الْمُدَاوَمَةُ عَلَى ذَلِكَ لَا أَصْلُ الْقَضَاءِ».
  • ʿĀʾisha — he did them regularly — Mirqat: «وَرَدَ فِي أَحَادِيثَ عَنْ عَائِشَةَ أَنَّهُ كَانَ يُصَلِّيهِمَا دَائِمًا»; Muslim's addition: «وَلَمْ يَزَلْ يُصَلِّيهِمَا حَتَّى فَارَقَ الدُّنْيَا». (Bukhārī: «كَانَ لَا يُصَلِّيهِمَا فِي الْمَسْجِدِ مَخَافَةَ أَنْ يَثْقُلَ عَلَى أُمَّتِهِ».)
  • Umm Salama's "shall I make them up?" — "No" — Mirqat (via al-Ṭaḥāwī): «فَقُلْتُ: يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ أَفَنَقْضِيهَا إِذَا فَاتَتْنَا؟ قَالَ: لَا».
  • Ibn Ḥajar's conclusion — Mirqat: «أَيْ وَقَدْ عَلِمْتِ أَنَّ مِنْ خَصَائِصِي أَنِّي إِذَا عَمِلْتُ عَمَلًا دَاوَمْتُ عَلَيْهِ، فَمِنْ ثَمَّ فَعَلْتُهُمَا وَنَهَيْتُ غَيْرِي عَنْهُمَا» — al-Qārī objects: «إِذَا كَانَ مِنْ خُصُوصِيَّاتِهِ فَلَا يَصْلُحُ لِلِاسْتِدْلَالِ».
  • ʿUmar beating those who prayed after ʿAṣr (your "1050" note actually sits here in Lamaat): «إِنَّ عُمَرَ كَانَ يَنْهَى عَنْهُمَا وَيَضْرِبُ عَلَيْهِمَا، فَقَالَتْ [عائشة]: صَدَقَ، وَلَكِنْ كَانَ النَّبِيُّ ﷺ يُصَلِّيهِمَا»; reason (Zayd b. Khālid): «لَوْلَا أَنِّي أَخْشَى أَنْ يَتَّخِذَهُمَا النَّاسُ سُلَّمًا إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ حَتَّى اللَّيْلِ لَمْ أَضْرِبْ فِيهِمَا». no explicit ijmāʿ sukūtī statement in the cache.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 1043); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْأَوْقَاتِ الَّتِي تُكْرَهُ فِيهَا الصَّلَاةُ
حديث ١٠٤٤
«وَعَنْ مُحَمَّدِ بْنِ إِبْرَاهِيمَ عَنْ قَيْسِ بْنِ عَمْرٍو قَالَ: رَأَى النَّبِيُّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ رَجُلًا يُصَلِّي بَعْدَ صَلَاةِ الصُّبْحِ رَكْعَتَيْنِ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ: «صَلَاةُ الصُّبْحِ رَكْعَتَيْنِ رَكْعَتَيْنِ» فَقَالَ الرَّجُلُ: إِنِّي لَمْ أَكُنْ صَلَّيْتُ الرَّكْعَتَيْنِ اللَّتَيْنِ قَبْلَهُمَا فَصَلَّيْتُهُمَا الْآنَ. فَسَكَتَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ.»
Narrated by Qais b. 'Amr  ·  Riwayah: Abu Dawud and Tirmidhi; al-Albani: sahih
Class Notes
  • This is a proof for Imām al-Shāfiʿī: what is said here is "two by two" (rakʿatayn rakʿatayn) — i.e. pray the two sunnah first, then the two [obligatory] second (of the Fajr prayer).
  • We (Ḥanafī) say: this chain is not connected (munqaṭiʿ) and is weak — yet it is the proof al-Shāfiʿī relies on.
  • The other three madhhabs: [make them up] after the rising of the sun — and they say there is no make-up of any sunnah except the sunnah of Fajr.
  • The two Shaykhs (al-Shaykhayn = Abū Ḥanīfa & Abū Yūsuf): the make-up of the sunnah is only together with the [make-up of the] obligatory prayer — it follows it subordinately (tabaʿan, تبعًا) — and applies only to the Fajr prayer.
  • Imām Muḥammad (al-Shaybānī): you may make up just the sunnah, without the obligatory prayer.
  • The fatwa allows both, but some prefer Imām Muḥammad's view, because nowadays many people miss their ṣalāh and their sunnahs.
  • Their (Shāfiʿī) proof: a hadith — "whoever does not pray the two cycles of the morning prayer [Fajr sunnah], let him pray them after the morning prayer [/after sunrise]." find exact wording + source.
  • Our (Ḥanafī) proof: the night of taʿrīs (laylat al-taʿrīs, ليلة التعريس) — the incident where the Prophet ﷺ and companions overslept past Fajr while traveling and prayed it (with its sunnah) after sunrise.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Tibi / Lamaat — fullest in Lamaat from al-Hidāya):
  • Shāfiʿī's "two-by-two" proof is munqaṭiʿ/weak — al-Tirmidhī: «إِسْنَادُ هَذَا الْحَدِيثِ لَيْسَ بِمُتَّصِلٍ؛ لِأَنَّ مُحَمَّدَ بْنَ إِبْرَاهِيمَ لَمْ يَسْمَعْ مِنْ قَيْسِ بْنِ عَمْرٍو»; Mirqat: «الْحَدِيثُ لَمْ يَثْبُتْ فَلَا يَكُونُ حُجَّةً عَلَى أَبِي حَنِيفَةَ».
  • Abū Ḥanīfa & Abū Yūsuf — only tabaʿan, only Fajr; Muḥammad — the sunna alone — Lamaat: «وَعِنْدَ أَبِي حَنِيفَةَ وَأَبِي يُوسُفَ: لَا قَضَاءَ لِسُنَّةِ الْفَجْرِ بَعْدَ الْفَوْتِ لَا قَبْلَ طُلُوعِ الشَّمْسِ وَلَا بَعْدَهَا... وَإِنَّمَا قَضَاهُمَا تَبَعًا لِلْفَرْضِ فِي لَيْلَةِ التَّعْرِيسِ... وَقَالَ مُحَمَّدٌ: أَحَبُّ إِلَيَّ أَنْ يَقْضِيَهُمَا إِلَى وَقْتِ الزَّوَالِ؛ لِأَنَّهُ ﷺ قَضَاهُمَا بَعْدَ ارْتِفَاعِ الشَّمْسِ غَدَاةَ لَيْلَةِ التَّعْرِيسِ».
  • The Shāfiʿī proof hadith — Lamaat: «مَنْ لَمْ يُصَلِّ رَكْعَتَيِ الْفَجْرِ فَلْيُصَلِّهَا بَعْدَ مَا تَطْلُعُ الشَّمْسُ» (Tirmidhī: «لَا نَعْرِفُهُ إِلَّا مِنْ هَذَا الْوَجْهِ»; held by al-Thawrī, Ibn al-Mubārak, al-Shāfiʿī, Aḥmad, Isḥāq).
  • laylat al-taʿrīs is exactly the Ḥanafī basis above — the make-up was tabaʿan li-l-farḍ after the sun rose, on the night of taʿrīs.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 1044); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْأَوْقَاتِ الَّتِي تُكْرَهُ فِيهَا الصَّلَاةُ
حديث ١٠٤٥
«وَعَنْ جُبَيْرِ بْنِ مُطْعِمٍ أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ قَالَ: «يَا بَنِي عَبْدِ مَنَافٍ لَا تَمْنَعُوا أَحَدًا طَافَ بِهَذَا الْبَيْتِ وَصَلَّى أَيَّةَ سَاعَةٍ شَاءَ مِنْ لَيْلٍ أَوْ نَهَارٍ»»
Narrated by Jubair b. Mut'im  ·  Riwayah: Tirmidhi, Abu Dawud and Nasa'i; sahih
Class Notes
  • This is actually Imām al-Shāfiʿī's daleel — evidence that there was no disliked time to pray in Mecca.
  • They also use another narration (≈1051) as his proof.
  • We (Ḥanafī) respond: it is maʿlūl (معلول) — it has a hidden defect (ʿilla) — and there is also iḍṭirāb (اضطراب, inconsistency / a mix-up in the narrations). This iḍṭirāb was noted by Ibn al-Humām in his Fatḥ al-Qadīr (فتح القدير).
  • Imām Aḥmad: in Mecca, the only thing you may pray [in the disliked times] is the prayers of ṭawāf (~three opinions here — messy, check Mirqat).
  • Our further response: this "no disliked time in Mecca" narration contradicts the mashhūr prohibiting narrations — so we either interpret it (taʾwīl) or leave it (tark), as is always done with such conflicts.
  • al-Shāfiʿī's reasoning: the other [prohibition] hadiths are clear and explicit; the point here is that the disbelievers in Mecca had a number of false [forbidden] times in which they would prevent people from praying — so the Prophet ﷺ is stressing "let them pray": any time at the Kaʿba in Mecca is allowed. (This is Imām al-Shāfiʿī's view.)
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Tibi / Lamaat):
  • No disliked time in Mecca (al-Muẓhir, in Mirqat & Tibi): matn «لَا تَمْنَعُوا أَحَدًا طَافَ بِهَذَا الْبَيْتِ وَصَلَّى أَيَّةَ سَاعَةٍ شَاءَ مِنْ لَيْلٍ أَوْ نَهَارٍ»; «فِيهِ دَلِيلٌ عَلَى أَنَّ صَلَاةَ التَّطَوُّعِ فِي أَوْقَاتِ الْكَرَاهَةِ غَيْرُ مَكْرُوهَةٍ بِمَكَّةَ لِشَرَفِهَا... وَبِهِ قَالَ الشَّافِعِيُّ، وَعِنْدَ أَبِي حَنِيفَةَ حُكْمُهَا حُكْمُ سَائِرِ الْبِلَادِ فِي الْكَرَاهَةِ».
  • Imām Aḥmad — only the two rakʿas of ṭawāf — Lamaat: «وَافَقَهُ أَحْمَدُ فِي رَكْعَتَيِ الطَّوَافِ فِيمَا بَعْدَ الْفَجْرِ وَالْعَصْرِ، أَمَّا عِنْدَ الطُّلُوعِ وَالْغُرُوبِ وَالاسْتِوَاءِ فَفِيهِ عَنْهُ رِوَايَتَانِ».
  • maʿlūl / iḍṭirāb / Ibn al-Humām's Fatḥ al-Qadīr — NOT FOUND in the cached pages for this hadith (Lamaat's 1045 section runs off the cached page-end). Keep your dictated note pending; I can re-fetch the next Lamaat pages to confirm.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 1045); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْأَوْقَاتِ الَّتِي تُكْرَهُ فِيهَا الصَّلَاةُ
حديث ١٠٤٦
«وَعَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ: أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ نَهَى عَنِ الصَّلَاةِ نِصْفَ النَّهَارِ حَتَّى تَزُولَ الشَّمْسُ إِلَّا يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ. رَوَاهُ الشَّافِعِيُّ»
Narrated by Abu Hurayra  ·  Riwayah: Shafi'i; al-Albani: da'if
Class Notes
  • al-Shāfiʿī & Aḥmad: praying these two cycles at midday ("the half of the day" = nisf al-nahār / istiwāʾ, one of the prohibited times) is allowed on the day of Friday (Jumuʿa) — only on Friday.
  • The Ḥanafīs: our usual response (the mashhūr prohibition stands). check commentary: per Aḥmad & al-Shāfiʿī, is praying in that time on Friday allowed because of this hadith or for another reason? (User unsure.)
  • Our second proof: there are no narrations that the Prophet ﷺ prayed at this time at all.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat):
  • Matn (Abū Hurayra, via al-Shāfiʿī): «نَهَى عَنِ الصَّلَاةِ نِصْفَ النَّهَارِ حَتَّى تَزُولَ الشَّمْسُ إِلَّا يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ». Mirqat: «"إِلَّا يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ" مُسْتَثْنًى مِنَ الْكَرَاهَةِ، يَدُلُّ عَلَى أَنَّ صَلَاةَ النَّفْلِ نِصْفَ النَّهَارِ يَوْمَ الْجُمُعَةِ غَيْرُ مَكْرُوهَةٍ، وَبِهِ قَالَ الشَّافِعِيُّ، وَعِنْدَ أَبِي حَنِيفَةَ مَكْرُوهَةٌ، وَقَدْ وَافَقَ أَبُو يُوسُفَ الشَّافِعِيَّ» (the hadith «فِي سَنَدِهِ مَقَالٌ»). So the Friday-midday allowance is from this exception in the hadith — answering your "why on Friday?" flag.
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 1046); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْأَوْقَاتِ الَّتِي تُكْرَهُ فِيهَا الصَّلَاةُ
حديث ١٠٥٠
«وَعَنْ مُعَاوِيَةَ قَالَ: إِنَّكُمْ لَتُصَلُّونَ صَلَاةً لَقَدْ صَحِبْنَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَمَا رَأَيْنَاهُ يُصَلِّيهِمَا وَلَقَدْ نَهَى عَنْهُمَا يَعْنِي الرَّكْعَتَيْنِ بَعْدَ الْعَصْرِ»
Narrated by Muʿāwiya (confirm)  ·  Riwayah: Bukhari
Class Notes
  • Muʿāwiya says the Prophet ﷺ prohibited these two [rakʿas], and that he never saw [him pray] them.
  • If we accept this narration, then [the practice] is specific to the Prophet ﷺ.
  • It is mentioned that ʿUmar b. al-Khaṭṭāb would beat those who prayed these two cycles — and he did so in front of all the companions, who did not object.
    • This is a consensus by silence — ijmāʿ sukūtī (إجماع سكوتي).
    • For us it takes the place of a mashhūr ruling, and ijmāʿ sukūtī yields ʿilm al-ṭumaʾnīna (علم الطمأنينة — firm, reassuring knowledge).
  • This (1050) is once again our (Ḥanafī) proof.
  • Commentary fill (Mirqat / Lamaat — now fetched):
  • Mirqat on the matn: «"إِنَّكُمْ لَتُصَلُّونَ صَلَاةً" أَيْ رَكْعَتَيْنِ، فَإِنَّهُمَا أَقَلُّ مَا يُطْلَقُ عَلَيْهِ الصَّلَاةُ كَمَا هُوَ مَذْهَبُنَا. "فَمَا رَأَيْنَاهُ يُصَلِّيهِمَا" أَيْ مُطْلَقًا، أَوْ لِأَنَّهُ كَانَ يُصَلِّيهِمَا فِي الْبَيْتِ لِئَلَّا يُقْتَدَى بِهِ لِاخْتِصَاصِهِمَا بِهِ. "وَلَقَدْ نَهَى عَنْهُمَا" أَيْ نَهْيًا عَامًّا» — so Muʿāwiya never saw him pray them because he prayed them at home (a khaṣīṣa, lest he be emulated), and the prohibition was general.
  • al-Ṭaḥāwī (the mutawātir point you flagged): «فَقَدْ جَاءَتِ الْآثَارُ عَنْ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ ﷺ مُتَوَاتِرَةً بِالنَّهْيِ عَنِ الصَّلَاةِ بَعْدَ الْعَصْرِ، ثُمَّ عَمِلَ بِذَلِكَ أَصْحَابُهُ مِنْ بَعْدِهِ، فَلَا يَنْبَغِي لِأَحَدٍ أَنْ يُخَالِفَ ذَلِكَ، وَقَدْ ثَبَتَ عَنْ عُمَرَ أَنَّهُ كَانَ يَضْرِبُ فِي الصَّلَاةِ بَعْدَ الْعَصْرِ حَتَّى يَنْصَرِفَ مِنْ صَلَاتِهِ».
  • Ibn al-Humām — the ijmāʿ sukūtī (exactly your point): «وَكَانَ ضَرْبُهُ بِمَحْضَرٍ مِنَ الصَّحَابَةِ مِنْ غَيْرِ نَكِيرٍ، فَكَانَ إِجْمَاعًا عَلَى أَنَّ الْمُتَقَرِّرَ بَعْدَهُ ﷺ عَدَمُ جَوَازِهِمَا» — ʿUmar's beating was in the presence of the companions, without any objection, so it was an ijmāʿ that the settled position after the Prophet ﷺ is the impermissibility of those two rakʿas. (So the "mutawātir" and "ijmāʿ sukūtī" notes belong here at 1050, not 1040–41.)
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 1050); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).
Kitab كِتَابُ الصَّلَاةِ بَابُ الْأَوْقَاتِ الَّتِي تُكْرَهُ فِيهَا الصَّلَاةُ
حديث ١٠٥١
«وَعَنْ أَبِي ذَرٍّ قَالَ وَقَدْ صَعِدَ عَلَى دَرَجَةِ الْكَعْبَةِ: مَنْ عَرَفَنِي فَقَدْ عَرَفَنِي وَمَنْ لَمْ يَعْرِفْنِي فَأَنَا جُنْدُبٌ سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ يَقُولُ: «لَا صَلَاةَ بَعْدَ الصُّبْحِ حَتَّى تَطْلُعَ الشَّمْسُ وَلَا بَعْدَ الْعَصْرِ حَتَّى تَغْرُبَ الشَّمْسُ إِلَّا بِمَكَّةَ إِلَّا بِمَكَّةَ إِلَّا بِمَكَّةَ»»
Narrated by Abu Dharr  ·  Riwayah: Ahmad and Razin (weak according to al-Albani)
Class Notes
  • Referenced under 1045 as another proof used for al-Shāfiʿī (no disliked time in Mecca).
  • Commentary (Tibi): the emphasis + repetition «إِلَّا بِمَكَّةَ» three times, with its restriction (ḥaṣr), is al-Shāfiʿī's daleel for what he held re Jubayr b. Muṭʿim's hadith «لَا تَمْنَعُوا أَحَدًا طَافَ بِهَذَا الْبَيْتِ وَصَلَّى أَيَّةَ سَاعَةٍ شَاءَ» — i.e. no disliked time for prayer in Mecca (this is the "≈1051 narration" you mentioned at 1045). Note: graded weak (Aḥmad & Razīn; ḍaʿīf per al-Albānī).
Source: Matn, narrator & translation via sunnah.com (Mishkat al-Masabih 1051); class commentary from the teacher's Mishkat 1 lesson notes (402–1047 / 1051).

How to Use This Guide

مشكاة المصابيح (١) — Mishkat 1, hadith 402–1051. A quick guide to what's here and how to move around.
📖 Each entry has the Arabic matn and the class notes — or, for a few reference-only entries with no separate class notes, which madhhab position it serves as a daleel for.
🔗 Gold underlined numbers are tappable. Class notes often cite another hadith as a daleel (evidence) for a fiqh position — tap the number to jump straight to it, then use Prev to come back.
◀ ▶ Prev / Next buttons move one hadith at a time. You can also drag the gold bar at the top to jump anywhere in the guide instantly.
🎞️ Slide View (switch, bottom-right) turns the whole guide into one continuous scroll — like flipping through a PDF, no buttons needed.
🌙 Sun / moon button (top-left) toggles dark mode. Your choice is remembered next time.
📑 الْفِهْرِس (Table of Contents) — every hadith currently in the guide, grouped by كِتَاب & بَاب. Tap a bāb to expand it, tap a number to jump there.
Coverage note: 222 entries span hadith 402–1051, but not every number in between — this covers what was actually taught in class, plus a handful of "reference-only" entries (matn only, no class notes) added because another hadith cited them as a daleel. Those are labeled "Reference-only entry" at the bottom of the entry.
